# Specifications

The design of TweaksV as it is built: every module of the source, what it does, what it checks, what it writes and what it logs, so that the source can be understood, forked or built on without opening it.
Four files carry the whole truth about TweaksV, each with its own job:

| File | Carries |
|---|---|
| [README.md](README.md) | the player's view: what each part does, every key of `TweaksV.ini`, building, testing, other mods |
| SPECIFICATIONS.md, this file | the design: the files, the tables, the start-up, the formats, the shared code, the mechanism of every part, the rules, the tests, how to extend it |
| [PATTERNS.md](PATTERNS.md) | the game facts: every byte pattern, what the match holds, where it matched on the current build, how the sites are encoded |
| [NOTES.md](NOTES.md) | what each part does with the checks it makes, the measurements, the open issues |

Credits and derivations are in [CREDITS.md](CREDITS.md), the notices in [THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md](THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md).
[SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md) says how to report a security problem and what it covers; [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md) holds what I plan next and [PLAN.md](PLAN.md) the phases of a change while one is under way, a placeholder while none is.
"I" in these files is the author, dachdecker369.
TweaksV is GPL-3.0-only.

## 1. What TweaksV is

One ASI plugin for GTA V Enhanced, `TweaksV.asi`, loaded by an ASI loader into `GTA5_Enhanced.exe`.
It does nothing in any other program.
Sixteen parts change the running game in memory, never a file on disk: the heap size and the memory budget of the game's pools (HeapAdjuster), the modkit array (ModkitLimit), the packfile array and the archive count of the game's configuration (PackfileLimit), the box streamer's traversal list (BoxStreamer), the decal definitions array (Decals), a mods folder with unencrypted archives, whose packs it checks for GTA V Legacy, and the cache check (RageOpenV), the three start-up screens (StraightToStoryMode), the files DirectStorage keeps locked (DirectStorage), the threshold up to which the game's own script makes the vanilla blips (Blips), the room for `weapons.meta` files and for weapon component definitions, each with its guard (WeaponLimits), the props per anchor of a ped model (PedProps, experimental and off by default), the video memory budget and the most objects for streamed textures and buffers (Resources), the ray tracing buffer of moving geometry (RayTracing), a job's lists, the frame's light stores and the corona store (LightCapacity), the siren setting IDs and the sirens per vehicle (SirenSettings) and the static bounds pool's size (StaticBounds).
Every part but DirectStorage, which hooks two functions of Windows and needs no pattern, finds the game's code by byte patterns, checks every site before it writes, writes all of its sites in one go or none, leaves alone what another mod has already changed, and says in the log and in one start-up message box what it could not do.
With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` the parts that have a measurement also measure how the game uses what they change, and TweaksV measures the limits of parts to come before it changes the game - the sirens per vehicle, the lighting capacity, the two model budgets, the ped pool and the render queues - and writes nothing for them but one redirected call and one redirected jump (section 11.16).

Everything is driven by three tables - the settings, the patterns, the parts - and by shared services: the log, the start-up summary, the scanner, the patch module, the relocation core.
A new part is an entry in each table plus one file of its own.

## 2. The code at a glance

All of the source is in `source\`, loose in one folder next to the solution `TweaksV.slnx` and the project `TweaksV.vcxproj`; the project's filters group the files in Solution Explorer only.
Every file is `TweaksV*` and every symbol is in `namespace TweaksV`, except `dllmain.cpp`, `version.rc` and MinHook in `vendor\MinHook`, compiled unchanged.

| File | Job |
|---|---|
| `dllmain.cpp` | the one `DllMain`: the start-up in ten steps (section 3) |
| `TweaksVSettings.h`, `.cpp` | the settings table and every reader of a setting (section 4.1) |
| `TweaksVIni.h`, `.cpp` | `TweaksV.ini`, the file: where it is, creating it, adding missing keys, replacing a 1.x file (section 5) |
| `TweaksVFiles.h`, `.cpp` | the own-file checks: TweaksV's files are opened only where they are plain files in their own folder (section 5.2) |
| `TweaksVLock.h` | the lock guard: an `SRWLOCK` held for a scope and released by an exception too, for the log and the summary (section 6.1) |
| `TweaksVLog.h`, `.cpp` | `TweaksV.log`, the console, the text helpers (section 6) |
| `TweaksVSummary.h`, `.cpp` | the problems and findings, the start-up summary message box and the boxes of the reports after it (section 6.4) |
| `TweaksVMeasure.h`, `.cpp` | the measurements: one loop for every part that sums up what it counts, `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`'s and DirectStorage's `UNLOCK_FILES` summary (section 6.6), and one check of the game's data |
| `TweaksVConflicts.h`, `.cpp` | the plugins next to TweaksV that do the job of one of its parts, `IsPluginInstalledFor`, and `IsInsideGameImage` and `IsInsideGameData` (section 7) |

| `TweaksVLegacyPacks.h`, `.cpp` | the check for packs made for GTA V Legacy, a job of the RageOpenV part, started by its one call once the mods folder is in use (section 11.6) |

| `TweaksVPedPool.h`, `.cpp` | the ped pool, measured first (section 11.16.5) |
| `TweaksVRenderQueues.h`, `.cpp` | the render queues of streamed peds and vehicles, measured first (section 11.16.6) |
| `TweaksVStaticBounds.h`, `.cpp` | the StaticBounds part (section 11.15) |
| `TweaksVDump.h`, `.cpp` | test builds only: `TEST_DUMP_CODE`, a copy of the game's code, and `TEST_DUMP_DATA`, named windows of its `.data` (section 13.2), and `TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT`, a copy of a script program (section 13.3) |
| `version.rc` | the version resource of `TweaksV.asi`, from `BuildInfo.h` (section 15) |

Conventions every `.cpp` and `.h` follows: the first line is `// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only`, the second says what the file is (`version.rc`, the test sources and the test scripts carry the SPDX line in their own comment syntax, after the shebang or `@echo off` in the scripts, and `test\base.ini` in its comment block; the project files and the two INI files that stand for TweaksV's own, `expected_default.ini` and `layout1.ini`, carry none); a part's `.cpp` opens with a comment that explains what the part does, every switch it reads and the credit for the idea or the patterns; a part has one log tag (`kTag`, the feature name of section 4.1), one entry function `Init<Part>()` called by the feature table - a part of two modules one per module, `Init<Module>()`, which its entry calls in turn (WeaponLimits), and a part named apart from its one module that module's (Resources, `InitTextureBudget()`) - and keeps its state in file-scope variables inside an anonymous namespace; a module of a part measured first does the same with its part's tag and one entry function `Measure<Limit>()`, called by the table of the parts measured first (sections 4.3 and 11.16).
Tabs for indentation; CRLF line endings in the source (git normalises them).
The parts carry no code of the projects they derive from but StaticBounds, whose key, default and rule come from the source of the Static Bounds Limit Adjuster (GPL-3.0-or-later, section 11.15); what remains of the others is the console opener in `TweaksVLog.cpp` (RageOpenV's, GPL-3.0) and the scanner's pattern parser (section 8), all three named in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md.

## 3. The start-up

`DllMain` runs these steps on `DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH`, inside a `try` so that no exception leaves it, and returns `TRUE` whatever happens:

1. `IsGtaVEnhanced()`: the running program's file name is `GTA5_Enhanced.exe` (case-insensitive) or nothing more happens.
2. `CreateOrUpdateIni()`: `TweaksV.ini` exists from here on (section 5).
3. `LoadSettings()`: every setting is read once; the file is let go of afterwards (section 4.1).
4. `[TWEAKSV] ENABLED=0` stops here: the file is kept, nothing else is done.
   Otherwise the module pins itself (`GetModuleHandleExW` with `GET_MODULE_HANDLE_EX_FLAG_PIN`): `TweaksV.asi` stays loaded until the game exits, because the game's code calls into it once hooked, and an early `FreeLibrary` would crash the game at the next hooked call.
   A pin that fails is reported (`TweaksV.asi could not be pinned in memory (...): an ASI loader that unloads it later would crash the game.`).
5. `CheckForConflicts()` with `CONFLICT_CHECK=1` (section 7), before the log is started, so that the header's `Features:` line says `off` for every part the check switches off.
6. `ClearLogFile()` and `WriteLogHeader()`: the log is started and its header written (section 6.2).
   Lines logged before this point - the settings' problems, a pin that fails, the conflict check's lines - are kept and written after the header; the console, opened next, does not show them.
7. `OpenConsole()` with `CONSOLE=1`.
   A test build then runs `StartCodeDump()` (section 13.2), before anything is patched, `StartDataDump()`, which takes the windows' first copies when `TEST_DUMP_DATA` is set (section 13.2), and `StartScriptDump()`, which joins the script programs' hook when `TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT` is set (section 13.3) - before the Blips part joins it at step 8, so that its copy shows a program as the game placed it.
8. `StartFeatures()`: every part that is on, in table order (section 4.3).
   Each part runs its whole job here, except RageOpenV, which only hooks `GetSystemTimeAsFileTime` and does its job at the game's first time request, because the game's file system is not up in `DllMain`, and DirectStorage, which hooks `NtCreateFile` here, with `UNLOCK_FILES` `NtSetInformationFile` too - and with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` `DStorageGetFactory`, and `OpenFile` when the game creates its factory (section 11.8) - and Blips, which joins the script programs' hook here and does its job each time the game places `blip_controller` (section 11.9).
   With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` the parts measured first follow, after the parts (section 11.16).
9. `ShowStartupSummaryLater()` starts the thread that shows the start-up summary (section 6.4), unless `StartFeatures` returned true because a part shows it itself later (RageOpenV, at the first time request).
10. `DllMain` returns `TRUE`, also after an exception; one that reaches its own `try` is reported (`Unexpected exception during the start-up - the rest of TweaksV is skipped this session.`) and the summary thread started, so the box names it even when the log never started.

On `DLL_PROCESS_DETACH` the log is flushed and closed (`CloseLog`), without waiting for a lock another thread may have died holding.

The game's file version comes from the version resource of the running program (`ReadGameVersion`, four 16-bit numbers; `GameVersionNumber` packs them into one number, read once, 0 when unreadable).
The PackfileLimit part chooses patterns by it; the log header prints it.

## 4. The three tables

### 4.1 Settings (`TweaksVSettings.h`)

A `SettingsSection` is a section of `TweaksV.ini` and the part it belongs to: `{ name, feature }`, the feature name being the log tag and the name in the `Features:` line and the start-up summary.
The sections, in file order, which is also the order the parts start in: `kTweaksVSection` (`TWEAKSV`, `TweaksV`), `kHeapSection` (`HEAP_ADJUSTER`, `HeapAdjuster`), `kModkitSection` (`MODKIT_LIMIT`, `ModkitLimit`), `kPackfileSection` (`PACKFILE_LIMIT`, `PackfileLimit`), `kBoxStreamerSection` (`BOXSTREAMER`, `BoxStreamer`), `kDecalsSection` (`DECALS`, `Decals`), `kRageOpenVSection` (`RAGEOPENV`, `RageOpenV`), `kStoryModeSection` (`STRAIGHTTOSTORYMODE`, `StraightToStoryMode`), `kDirectStorageSection` (`DIRECTSTORAGE`, `DirectStorage`), `kBlipsSection` (`BLIPS`, `Blips`), `kWeaponLimitsSection` (`WEAPON_LIMITS`, `WeaponLimits`), `kPedPropsSection` (`PEDPROPS`, `PedProps`), `kResourcesSection` (`RESOURCES`, `Resources`), `kRayTracingSection` (`RAYTRACING`, `RayTracing`), `kLightCapacitySection` (`LIGHTCAPACITY`, `LightCapacity`), `kSirenSettingsSection` (`SIRENSETTINGS`, `SirenSettings`), `kStaticBoundsSection` (`STATICBOUNDS`, `StaticBounds`); `kSections` lists them in that order.

A `Setting` is `{ section, key, kind, defaultValue, minValue, maxValue, testBuildOnly, comment }` with `kind` one of `Switch` (0 or 1), `Number` (an `int` within `minValue..maxValue`) or `Text` (test builds only, never written to the file), and `comment` the short note the default file carries after the value, empty for none.
The builders `Switch(section, key, on[, comment])`, `Number(section, key, value, min, max[, comment])` and `TestText(section, key)` make the entries of `kSettings`, the one table, in the order of the default file: the keys of `[TWEAKSV]` (`ENABLED`, `LOG`, `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, `DRY_RUN`, `CONSOLE`, `ERROR_POPUP`, `CONFLICT_CHECK`, `VERSION` with default 5 and range 2..1000000, then the test-only `TEST_BREAK_PATTERN`, `TEST_TWICE_PATTERN`, `TEST_LOG_LIMIT_KB`, `TEST_DUMP_CODE`, `TEST_DUMP_DATA`, `TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT`), then per part `ENABLED` and its own keys: `HEAP_SIZE` 2000 (1..2047), `POOL_MEMORY` 256 (136..2047), `MODKITARRAY_SIZE` 65536 (1..65536), `PACKFILEARRAY_SIZE` 13000 (1..65535), `TRAVERSAL_LIST_SIZE` 10000 (1000..100000), `DECALDEFS_SIZE` 2048 (260..16384), `MODS_FOLDER`, `OPEN_RPF`, `SKIP_RPF_CACHE_CHECK`, `SKIP_SPLASH_SCREEN`, `SKIP_LEGAL_SCREEN`, `SKIP_LANDING_PAGE`, `UNLOCK_FILES`, all on, `ACTIVE_BLIP_LIMIT` 1900 (150..1997), `WEAPON_INFO_FILES` 1024 (160..16384), `WEAPON_COMPONENTS_SIZE` 1024 (470..16384), `VIDEO_MEMORY_BUDGET` 0 (0..16384), `DYNAMIC_BVH_VERTICES` 0 (0..26843545), `LARGE_SIREN_IDS`, on, and `STATICBOUNDS_SIZE` 16000 (1..32767); `[PEDPROPS]` holds `ENABLED` alone, the one `ENABLED` that defaults to 0 (section 11.11).
`kIniLayoutVersion` is 5 and `kIniFirstLayout` 2, the first layout with `VERSION` - its minimum - and the oldest one carried forward (section 5.3).
A `static_assert` on `IniKeyCount()` pins the layout at 47 keys in the file: a new key means a new layout number.
`kIniCommentColumn`, 28, is the column at which every comment of the default file starts, and a second `static_assert`, on `CommentsFitTheColumn()`, refuses a key whose `KEY=default` would reach it.

`FindSetting(section, key)` is `consteval`: it returns the table entry, and a name that is not in the table does not compile.
Every part reads its settings through a named reference made that way (`kHeapSize`, `kPoolMemory`, `kModkitArraySize`, `kPackfileArraySize`, `kTraversalListSize`, `kDecalDefsSize`, `kModsFolder`, `kOpenRpf`, `kSkipRpfCacheCheck`, `kSkipSplashScreen`, `kSkipLegalScreen`, `kSkipLandingPage`, `kUnlockFiles`, `kActiveBlipLimit`, `kWeaponInfoFiles`, `kWeaponComponentsSize`, `kVideoMemoryBudget`, `kDynamicBvhVertices`, `kLargeSirenIds`, `kStaticBoundsSize`, `kEnabled`, `kLog`, `kDiagnosticMode`, `kDryRun`, `kConsole`, `kErrorPopup`, `kConflictCheck`, `kIniVersion`, the six test keys); the functions that work on the whole table (`IsFeatureOn`, `DescribeSettings`, `DefaultIniText`, the missing-key check of section 5.3) iterate `kSettings`.

`LoadSettings()` reads every entry once through `ReadIniValue` (a build that is not the test build skips the six test keys) and keeps the values in one array; afterwards nothing reads the file again.
There is no in-game panel and no reload key, by design: the parts patch the game before it runs, so a setting changed live would change nothing.
A value is parsed by its kind: a switch accepts `1`, `0`, `true`, `false`, `yes`, `no`, `on`, `off` in any case; a number is parsed with `wcstoll`, clamped to its limits with a reported problem when it lay outside, and a value that is no number is reported and the default used; a text is kept as it is.
The report lines are `TweaksV.ini: [SECTION] KEY=value is not a valid on/off value - using 1.`, `... is not a number - using 2048.` and `... is outside 260..16384 - using 16384.`.
A `VERSION` above 5 is reported as a file from a newer TweaksV.
The readers: `IsOn(setting)`, `GetNumber(setting)`, `GetText(setting)`, `IsDryRun()`, `IsDiagnosticMode()`, and for the WeaponLimits part's guards `GuardRoom(tag, guard, partRoom, gameRoom)` - the room the part gave the game, in a test build with `TWEAKSV_TEST_GUARD_ROOM=1` the game's own (section 13.1); `IsFeatureOn(section)` is the section's `ENABLED` unless `DisableFeatureForSession(section)` switched the part off (the conflict check does that), `IsEnabledInIni(section)` the `ENABLED` alone; `DefaultIniText()` builds the default file from the table (section 5.4); `DescribeSettings()` builds the header's `Features:` line and one line per part with its values (section 6.2).

### 4.2 Patterns (`TweaksVPatterns.h`)

A `GamePattern` is `{ label, text }`: the label is the one PATTERNS.md gives the pattern (`H-E1`, `P-E-R31a`, `D-E-L12`, ...), the text its bytes in hex with one space between them and `?` for any byte.
`kPatterns` lists every pattern once, in the order of PATTERNS.md, grouped per part with a comment that says what the group's matches hold.
Two `consteval` checks run when TweaksV is compiled: `PatternsAreValid()` (every text is well-formed hex and wildcards, every label is unique) and `Pattern(label)`, the only way the code names a pattern, which returns the table entry and refuses to compile for a label that is not there.
`PatternByte(pattern, index)` gives the byte a pattern holds at an index (-1 for a wildcard), so that a constant can be checked, when TweaksV is compiled, against the byte its pattern pins it with (`TweaksVScripts`, section 13.3), and `PatternHolds(pattern, at, bytes)` and `PatternImm32(pattern, at)`, built on it, do so for an instruction's bytes and for an imm32, one copy for every part.
The `patterns` test suite checks that PATTERNS.md lists exactly the table's patterns with the same bytes, and that the code uses each one exactly once (section 14.2).
The table holds 274 patterns.
1.0.1158.16 uses 271 of them (the packfile part chooses one of three direct-size patterns and one of two references by build), and the check boot (`DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` and `CONSOLE=1`, every part on - `[PEDPROPS] ENABLED=1` among them, which ships off - with or without `DRY_RUN=1`) looks for every one: the Blips part the script programs' `Y-E-H1`, `Y-E-N1` and `Y-E-I1` on every boot it is on (section 13.3) and its own `L-E-P1` and `L-E-P2` with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`; WeaponLimits its seven, `W-E-I1`, `W-E-I2` and `W-E-C1` to `W-E-C5`, on every boot it is on (section 11.10); PedProps eleven of its thirteen on every boot it is on, `A-E-C2` and `A-E-S1` with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, and with the part off `A-E-C1`, `A-E-C2` and `A-E-S1` with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` (section 11.11); Resources `Q-E-T1` to `Q-E-T4` while `VIDEO_MEMORY_BUDGET` or `STREAMED_OBJECTS` is not 0, `Q-E-T6` while `STREAMED_OBJECTS` is not 0, and all seven, `Q-E-T5` and `Q-E-T7` the measurement's, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` (section 11.12); RayTracing `T-E-B1`, `T-E-C1` and `T-E-C2` while `DYNAMIC_BVH_VERTICES` is not 0 or with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` (section 11.13); SirenSettings the siren IDs' 14 while `LARGE_SIREN_IDS` is on or with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, `I-E-H1` to `I-E-H4` with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` and the part at work, and with `SIRENS_PER_VEHICLE` above 20 the sirens' 29, `I-E-Z1` to `Z7`, `I-E-X1` to `X4`, `I-E-Y1` to `Y4`, `I-E-L03` to `L08` and `I-E-N02` to `N09` (sections 11.14, 11.14.1); LightCapacity its 22 on every boot it is on, and with `CORONA_STORE` above 1088 `G-E-I1` to `I9` and `J1` to `J5` (sections 11.17, 11.17.1); StaticBounds `C-E-L1` and `C-E-D1` while it is on (section 11.15); and the parts measured first with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, the siren count its 4, the lighting capacity `G-E-K1` and `G-E-K2`, the model budgets their 16, the ped pool its 18 and the render queues their 24 (section 11.16).
A normal boot looks for fewer: none of PedProps' while that part is off, as it ships, `R-E-C1` only with `CONSOLE=1`, `R-E-F1..F8` only with a mods folder in the game's current directory or with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1`, `Q-E-T1` to `Q-E-T4` and the three `T-E-` patterns only with their part's key not 0 or with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1`, and `L-E-P1`, `L-E-P2`, `A-E-C2`, `A-E-S1`, `Q-E-T5`, `Q-E-T7`, `I-E-H1` to `I-E-H4` and the 64 of the parts measured first only with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1`, the lighting capacity's first 22 being looked for by its part, which ships on.

### 4.3 Features (`TweaksVFeatures.cpp`)

A `Feature` is `{ section, oldPlugins[3], peerPlugins[1], start }`: the part's section (its `ENABLED` switch and its name), up to three stand-alone `.asi` files that did its job before TweaksV and that TweaksV replaces, up to one stand-alone plugin that does its job too and stays installed because another mod needs it, and the function that starts it, which returns true if the part shows the start-up summary itself later.
Such a plugin is a `PeerPlugin`, `{ file, description }` - its `.asi` file and what it is, for the start-up summary - defined once as a constant and named in the entry of every part whose job it does: `kNveLoader`, `onigiri.asi`, "NaturalVision Enhanced's loader", a mods loader that skips the splash and legal screens too and patches the sites of RageOpenV and StraightToStoryMode after TweaksV, and `kResourceExtensions`, `ResourceExtensions.asi`, "Resource Extensions [Enhanced]", a closed-source plugin for Enhanced that raises many of the game's capacities - the archive count, the story blips' threshold and extra capacity for the ray tracing of moving geometry among them - and asks to remove the heap and packfile adjusters: it stays for its other jobs, so HeapAdjuster, PackfileLimit, Blips, RayTracing and LightCapacity are off while it is there (NOTES.md, "Conflicts").
`kFeatures` lists the parts in the order of `kSections` (checked by a `static_assert`):

| Part | Old plugins | Plugins that stay | Start |
|---|---|---|---|
| HeapAdjuster | `HeapAdjuster.asi`, `ZHeapAdjuster.asi` | `ResourceExtensions.asi` (`kResourceExtensions`) | `InitHeapAdjuster()` |
| ModkitLimit | `ModkitLimitAdjusterEnhanced.asi` | | `InitModkitLimit()` |
| PackfileLimit | `PackfileLimitAdjusterEnhanced.asi` | `ResourceExtensions.asi` (`kResourceExtensions`) | `InitPackfileLimit()` |
| BoxStreamer | `DecalPatch.asi` | | `InitBoxStreamer()` |
| Decals | `DecalPatch.asi` | | `InitDecals()` |
| RageOpenV | `RageOpenV.asi`, `OpenRPF.asi`, `OpenIV.asi` | `onigiri.asi` (`kNveLoader`) | `InitRageOpenV()`, true when its hook is on and its start-up check runs: the start-up at the first time request shows the summary (false when the hook failed or the check's thread could not be started, and `DllMain` shows it) |
| StraightToStoryMode | `StraightToStoryMode.asi` | `onigiri.asi` (`kNveLoader`) | `InitStraightToStoryMode()` |
| DirectStorage | `DirectStorageFix.asi` | | `InitDirectStorage()` |
| Blips | `BlipLimitFix.asi` | `ResourceExtensions.asi` (`kResourceExtensions`) | `InitBlips()` |
| WeaponLimits | `WeaponLimitsAdjusterEnhanced.asi` | | `InitWeaponInfos()`, then `InitWeaponComponents()`, each inside its own `try` in the entry: an exception ends only that half, whose problem names it (section 11.10), and the other still starts |
| PedProps | | | `InitPedProps()` |
| Resources | `GTAV.ResourceAdjusterEnhanced.asi` | | `InitTextureBudget()` |
| RayTracing | | `ResourceExtensions.asi` (`kResourceExtensions`) | `InitRayTracing()` |
| LightCapacity | | `ResourceExtensions.asi` (`kResourceExtensions`) | `InitLightCapacity()` |
| SirenSettings | | | `InitSirenIds()` |
| StaticBounds | `StaticBoundLimitAdjuster.asi`, `StaticBoundsLimitAdjuster.asi` | | `InitStaticBounds()` |

`StartFeatures()` calls each start function whose part is on, each inside its own `try`: an exception ends only that part, reported as `unexpected exception - this part is skipped for this session`.
`Features()` hands the table to the conflict check.

`kMeasuredFirst`, beside it, lists the parts measured first (section 11.16), one entry per limit in the order of that section's subsections: `MeasuredFirst { feature, start }`, the part's log tag and its module's `Measure<Limit>()`, and last `MeasurePedPropsWhileOff`, the ped props' measurement while that part is off (section 11.11).
It is a `std::initializer_list`, so it may be empty: a limit leaves it when its part is built - the part then gets its section and its entry of `kFeatures` - and when it is not.
`The lighting capacity stays in it beside its part, since its measurement goes on over whatever the part did (section 11.17).
With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1`, `StartFeatures()` starts them after the parts, each inside its own `try`: an exception ends only that measurement and is logged, not reported, since a measurement is an aid (section 12) - `DIAGNOSTIC: unexpected exception - a measurement of this part is skipped for this session.`

## 5. TweaksV.ini

### 5.1 Where it is

`GetGameFolder()` is the folder of the running program, found once from its path.
`GetFallbackFolder()` is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\TweaksV`, empty when `LOCALAPPDATA` is not set.
The file in use (`GetIniPathW()`): the game folder's `TweaksV.ini` if it exists, else the one in the fallback folder if that exists, else a new file - in the game folder, or in the fallback folder when the game folder cannot take it.
A file in the game folder always wins; the header says when an unused copy sits in the fallback folder.

### 5.2 The own-file checks (`TweaksVFiles`)

TweaksV writes only to its own files, `TweaksV.ini`, `TweaksV.log`, `TweaksV.old.ini` and the test-build dumps, and only where each is what its path says: a plain file directly in the folder it belongs in.
A link in its place could otherwise make TweaksV write to another file, which matters when the game runs as administrator.
`FileFolder` says which kind of folder the file is in: `Game`, which may itself be a link (an install moved to another drive), or `Own`, TweaksV's folder in `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, which may not.
`Check` refuses a file that is a reparse point, a folder, a hard link (more than one name for the same file), a file whose folder is a reparse point where that is not allowed, a file whose final path (`GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`, every link followed) is not the folder's own final path plus the name (compared case-insensitively), and a file whose attributes, folder or final path cannot be read at all.
An `OwnFile` result carries the handle, the Windows error if the open failed, and `unsafe`, the reason the file is not what its path says; `ReadOnly()` tells a read-only folder (`ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED`, `ERROR_WRITE_PROTECT`) from every other failure, because a read-only game folder is normal and only logged.
`FinalPathOf(path)` gives the final path of any existing file or folder the same way, empty if it cannot be read; RageOpenV compares the current directory with the game folder by it (section 11.6).
`FileExists(path)` says whether a file, not a folder, is there - for the places of `TweaksV.ini`, the conflict check and DirectStorage's note on `DirectStorageFix.asi`.

- `OpenOwnFile(folder, name, where, disposition, truncate)` opens for writing with `OPEN_ALWAYS` or `CREATE_NEW`, the link itself and never its target (`FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT`), checks, deletes a file it created itself when the check fails, and empties a file that passes when asked.
- `HoldOwnFile(folder, name, where)` opens an existing file for reading without delete sharing: while the handle is open, nobody can rename, delete or replace the file or rename a folder above it, so the Win32 INI functions that open it by its path reach this very file.
- `RenameOwnFile(folder, name, newName, where)` renames through a handle on the file after the same check; a file already at the new name is replaced only when it passes the check itself, otherwise nothing is renamed.

### 5.3 Creating, updating, replacing (`TweaksVIni`)

`CreateOrUpdateIni()`:

1. An existing file (game folder first, else fallback) is held (`HoldOwnFile`); one that fails the check is reported (`TweaksV.ini (<path>) is not used: <reason>. TweaksV uses its built-in defaults this session.`) and never read.
2. A held file without the key `VERSION` under `[TWEAKSV]` has the layout of TweaksV 1.x, whatever else it holds - a 1.6.0 file, an empty or damaged one, a layout-2 file whose `VERSION` line was deleted.
   Only the key names of that section are read to decide; no value of such a file is ever used.
   The hold is released, the file renamed to `TweaksV.old.ini` through `RenameOwnFile` (an older `TweaksV.old.ini` that passes the check is replaced), the default file written in its place with `CREATE_NEW`, held, and the problem reported once: `TweaksV.ini had the layout of TweaksV 1.x. It was renamed to TweaksV.old.ini and a fresh TweaksV.ini written with the default settings - set your values again.` A file that cannot be renamed is reported in its own words every boot (`... has the layout of TweaksV 1.x and could not be renamed to TweaksV.old.ini (<reason>). Delete or rename it yourself - TweaksV uses its built-in defaults until then.`) and not read; a renamed file whose replacement cannot be written is reported once (`TweaksV.ini had the layout of TweaksV 1.x and was renamed to TweaksV.old.ini, but a fresh TweaksV.ini could not be written in <folder> (<reason>). TweaksV uses its built-in defaults this session.`), and the next boot starts without a file (step 4).
3. Any other held file gets every key of the table it is missing, written with its default through `WritePrivateProfileStringW` - without a comment: TweaksV adds none to an existing file - and logged (`TweaksV.ini: added the missing key [SECTION] KEY=value.`); a key that cannot be added is logged when the file is read-only and reported otherwise.
   Existing values are never changed.
   A file of an older layout - its `VERSION`, read as `ReadIniValue` reads a value, a number from `kIniFirstLayout`, 2, to below `kIniLayoutVersion` - is then carried forward: this layout's number is written back in the old one's place, the rest of the line - a comment after the value - as it was (the value as `GetPrivateProfileStringW` gives it, 1023 characters at most; one cut short gets the number alone), and logged, `TweaksV.ini: carried from layout 4 to layout 5 (the keys it lacked are added).`; a `VERSION` that cannot be written back is logged when the file is read-only and reported otherwise (`TweaksV.ini: VERSION=5 could not be written back (<reason>), so the file keeps VERSION=4 and is carried forward again at the next start.`).
   A `VERSION` that is no number, or outside that range, is left as it is for `LoadSettings` (section 4.1): no number or below 2 it is reported, above 5 it is a newer TweaksV's file.
4. No file anywhere: the default file is written in the game folder; if that fails, in the fallback folder (the folder is created) - a read-only game folder is only logged (`The game folder is read-only (...), so TweaksV.ini was created with the default settings in <folder>.`), any other reason is reported; if both fail, the built-in defaults are used and reported.

The file stays held until `ReleaseIniFile()`, which `LoadSettings()` calls once every value is read.
`ReadIniValue(section, key)` reads at most 255 characters of a value through `GetPrivateProfileStringW`, cuts it at a comment - `//`, as the default file writes one, or `;` - since Windows' INI functions take everything after the `=` for the value, and trims it of spaces and tabs; nothing is read when the file is not in use.
`DescribeIniLocation()` gives the header its `TweaksV.ini:` line: the path, and in parentheses whether the 1.x file was renamed and this fresh file written, whether the game folder has no file, whether this file wins over an unused copy in the fallback folder, or why the file is not used.

### 5.4 The default file

`DefaultIniText()` builds the file from the table: the header comment, then every section with a blank line before it and its keys in table order, each as `KEY=default` with its comment from column 28 on where it has one (`DefaultLine`), CRLF line endings.
`test\expected_default.ini` is exactly this file, kept with LF endings: the `ini_default` suite gives it CRLF and compares byte for byte.

What each key does is README's table.
The two testing switches: `DRY_RUN=1` makes every part find and check everything, logging what it would change, and nothing in the game is written or hooked (only RageOpenV's one-shot hook on `GetSystemTimeAsFileTime`, which runs its checks and switches itself off) - the Blips part, which sees a script program only through the hook a dry run does not make, finds and checks the hook's sites and not the program; `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` makes every part that is on log every site it finds, the value it holds and every write, run its measurement where it has one, and makes the scanner log a line per pattern.
A part with `ENABLED=0` does nothing at all - but for the ped props' measurement (section 11.11) and SirenSettings' two (sections 11.16.1 and 11.16.2), whose siren count redirects its call - so a diagnostic check of every pattern needs every part on, `[PEDPROPS] ENABLED=1` among them, which ships off.
`DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` with `DRY_RUN=1` is the quiet check after a game update; `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` alone is the full measurement of the running game, which hooks a little more than a normal boot (README's "Diagnostics" has the list).

## 6. TweaksV.log and the start-up summary

### 6.1 The log file

`WriteLog(tag, text)` appends one line, `hh:mm:ss.mmm [Tag] text`, to `TweaksV.log` and to the console when one is open.
It is thread-safe (one `SRWLOCK`, held through the guard of `TweaksVLock.h`, which an exception releases too, so no failure can leave the lock taken and the next line waiting forever), lines longer than 2 KB, in a test build 64 KB, are cut and end in `...`, and every mention of the Windows user's profile folder is written as `%USERPROFILE%` - also in its short form (`C:\Users\NAME~1`), as `%TEMP%` often has it - because logs get posted in public and that folder is usually named after the user.
Lines written before the log is started (up to 1024) are kept and written after the header.
The file grows to 64 MB at most; the last line then says `TweaksV.log has reached its limit of 64 MB - nothing more is written to it this session.`, and the console still gets every line.

`ClearLogFile()` starts the file each session, truncated: in the game folder through `OpenOwnFile`, else - the folder read-only, a link in the file's place, the file in use by another program - in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\TweaksV`.
A read-only game folder is logged; any other reason for the fallback, and no file at all, are problems.
`LOG=0` writes no file.
`GetLogPathW()` is the path in use, which the message boxes print - with `LOG=0`, `(off - LOG=0)` in its place.

### 6.2 The header

`WriteLogHeader()` writes, all tagged `[TweaksV]`:

```
TweaksV 2.7.0 (git <hash>)
TweaksV.asi: <path>
Game: <path>, file version 1.0.1158.16, PE timestamp 0x6AA45F10 (<UTC date>)
TweaksV.ini: <path> (<notes, section 5.3>)
TweaksV.log: <path>
mods folder: <game folder>\mods (present | not present)
Features: HeapAdjuster on, ModkitLimit on, PackfileLimit on, BoxStreamer on, Decals on, RageOpenV on, StraightToStoryMode on, DirectStorage on, Blips on, WeaponLimits on, PedProps off, Resources on, RayTracing on, SirenSettings on, StaticBounds on
TweaksV: LOG=1 DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=0 DRY_RUN=0 CONSOLE=0 ERROR_POPUP=1 CONFLICT_CHECK=1 VERSION=5
HeapAdjuster: HEAP_SIZE=2000 POOL_MEMORY=256
ModkitLimit: MODKITARRAY_SIZE=65536
PackfileLimit: PACKFILEARRAY_SIZE=13000
BoxStreamer: TRAVERSAL_LIST_SIZE=10000
Decals: DECALDEFS_SIZE=2048
RageOpenV: MODS_FOLDER=1 OPEN_RPF=1 SKIP_RPF_CACHE_CHECK=1
StraightToStoryMode: SKIP_SPLASH_SCREEN=1 SKIP_LEGAL_SCREEN=1 SKIP_LANDING_PAGE=1
DirectStorage: UNLOCK_FILES=1
Blips: ACTIVE_BLIP_LIMIT=1900
WeaponLimits: WEAPON_INFO_FILES=1024 WEAPON_COMPONENTS_SIZE=1024
Resources: VIDEO_MEMORY_BUDGET=6000 STREAMED_OBJECTS=131072
RayTracing: DYNAMIC_BVH_VERTICES=0
SirenSettings: LARGE_SIREN_IDS=1
StaticBounds: STATICBOUNDS_SIZE=16000
```

The version and the git hash come from `BuildInfo.h` (section 15): a build from a tree with uncommitted changes carries `-dirty` after the hash, a build from a folder that is not a git repository says `unknown`.
A test build adds `TEST BUILD - not for release. TEST_BREAK_PATTERN=... TEST_TWICE_PATTERN=... TEST_LOG_LIMIT_KB=... TEST_DUMP_CODE=... TEST_DUMP_DATA=... TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT=...`.
A part that is off says `off` in the `Features:` line - off in `TweaksV.ini`, or switched off for the session by the conflict check, which runs before the header (section 3); its values line still follows, and the part itself logs nothing.
A part whose section holds `ENABLED` alone, PedProps, has no values line.

### 6.3 One wording for every part

Every part logs the same things the same way, under its own tag:

| What | Line |
|---|---|
| a patch of bytes | `<What> patched: <old bytes> -> <new bytes>.` (`Heap size patched: 646 MB -> 2000 MB.`, `Splash screen patched: 0F 84 85 01 00 00 -> E9 86 01 00 00 90.`, `RPF cache check patched: 41 57 -> C3 90.`) |
| a moved array | `The <array> moved and its limit raised from A to B entries.` |
| a switch that is off | `<KEY>=0 - <what> is not done.` (`SKIP_LEGAL_SCREEN=0 - the legal screen is not skipped.`, `MODS_FOLDER=0 - files in the mods folder are not used.`) |
| a job not done, for the summary | `PROBLEM: The <job> is not done this session: <why>` (`The modkit limit is not changed this session: M-E-R1 was not found.`, `The splash screen is not skipped this session: ...`, `Files in the mods folder are not used this session: ...`, `The files in the game folder are not unlocked this session: ...`, `The replace-renames into the game folder are not given POSIX semantics this session: ...`, `The ped props' limit is not raised this session: ...`, `The check for packs made for GTA V Legacy is not done this session: ...`) |
| a kind of line that comes per event, past its cap | `20000 lines of <kind> written - from now on they are only counted.` - ending `...counted; the summary every minute has the numbers.` where `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` gives such a summary, with `DIAGNOSTIC: ` before it for a kind that comes only with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` (RageOpenV's file requests, files served and archive headers and the packs its check for GTA V Legacy reads, DirectStorage's `OpenFile` calls and renames turned, the weapon infos' `weapons.meta` files, the ped props past 255), and nothing before it for the kinds that come without `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` (RageOpenV's mods files that could not be opened and OPEN archives left as they are, the packs its check names as made for GTA V Legacy or as not checked, the weapon component files their guard logs) - the check for packs made for GTA V Legacy has no summary every minute, so its three kinds end `...only counted.`, its last line having the numbers; `three kinds say it otherwise: the rooms of the sirens' bones, `DIAGNOSTIC: 20000 lines of vehicle models' rooms written - from now on they are not written.`, which nothing counts; the opens inside the game folder, `DIAGNOSTIC: 20000 open lines written - from now on the opens are only counted (see the summaries).` (section 11.8), and the folders of the mods folder that cannot be read, which stop after 20,000 lines without a line of their own, their number being in the problem RageOpenV reports (section 11.6) |
| a missing pattern | the scanner's `Pattern <label> not found: <bytes>` - or, for a pattern whose site must be unique and that matches twice or more, `Pattern <label> matches 2 or more times - not used (the site must be unique).` - then the part's reason above, `<label> was not found.` or `the game code holds <label> 2 or more times, not the expected 1.` (`Scanner::NotAsOften`, section 8) |
| something another mod changed | left alone and logged, not a problem (`The heap size is not changed this session: another heap adjuster already set it to 2000 MB - left as it is.`, `The RPF cache check is already skipped (another mod did it) - left as it is.`, `The RPF cache check's function is already hooked by another mod - left as it is.`, `Console hide call already patched (another mod did it) - left as it is.`), or refused with the reason `(another mod may have hooked it already)` where TweaksV cannot tell what it would be writing over |
| `DRY_RUN` | `DRY_RUN: would patch the <what>: <old> -> <new>`, `DRY_RUN: would move the <array> (N references) and raise its limit from A to B (M sites)<what the part adds>.` (` (M sites)` only where a limit is written, section 10.3), `DRY_RUN: would redirect the <call> at RVA ...`, `DRY_RUN: would hook the 8 file functions for the mods folder.`, `DRY_RUN: would hook NtCreateFile so that the game folder's other files are opened with write and delete sharing (UNLOCK_FILES).`, `DRY_RUN: would hook NtSetInformationFile so that a rename inside the game that replaces a file in the game folder gets POSIX semantics (UNLOCK_FILES).`, and a part's own line for a job of its own (`DRY_RUN: would widen the 11 narrowings of the ped props from 8 to 16 bits and raise their limit from 255 to 65535 per anchor.`, `DRY_RUN: would hook the weapon info loader at RVA ... so that a weapons.meta file past the room, 1024, is refused.`) |
| `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` | the parts' diagnostic lines start with `DIAGNOSTIC: `; the scanner's pattern lines and DirectStorage's call, `OpenFile`, open and rename lines carry their own wording (section 11.8; an open line gives the sharing asked for and the sharing `UNLOCK_FILES` gave it, `share R-- widened to RWD`, a rename line how the rename was asked and how it was made, `FileRenameInformation with ReplaceIfExists, a bare name -> FileRenameInformationEx, flags 0x00000003`): the scanner's `Pattern <label> found: <bytes> -> RVA 0x..., 1 match, bytes: ...` (and `  <label> match 2 of 7 at RVA 0x...: <16 bytes before> \| <48 bytes from the match>` for a pattern that matches more than once), a part's sites (`DIAGNOSTIC: reference at RVA ... -> RVA ... (M-E-R1)`, `DIAGNOSTIC: limit at RVA ... holds 1023 (N-1, M-E-L1) -> game size 1024`), every write (`DIAGNOSTIC: <what> at RVA ...: <old> -> <new>`) and the measurements |

Addresses inside the game are written as `RVA 0x...`, the offset from the start of `GTA5_Enhanced.exe` in memory, so they can be compared across boots and with PATTERNS.md; an address outside the game is written as `0x... (outside the game)`.

### 6.4 Problems and the start-up summary (`TweaksVSummary`)

`ReportFailure(tag, what)` logs `PROBLEM: <what>` under the part's tag and keeps the item (`[Tag] what`) for the start-up summary, at most 40 items, the rest counted; the list is kept under a lock held through the same guard, with the item built before the lock is taken.
`ShowStartupSummary()` runs once: with nothing to show it logs nothing; otherwise, with `ERROR_POPUP=0`, it logs `Start-up summary not shown (ERROR_POPUP=0): N problem(s), see above.`, and with `ERROR_POPUP=1` it logs `Showing the start-up summary (N problem(s)).` and shows one message box, `TweaksV could not apply everything:`, with one bullet (`•`) per item, `• ... and N more.` when items were dropped, the line `The game continues without the affected parts. Details are in TweaksV.log:` with the path on the next line (`(off - LOG=0)` with `LOG=0`), and `To stop this message, set ERROR_POPUP=0 in TweaksV.ini.` `ShowStartupSummaryLater()` does that from a thread of its own, which holds a reference to the module while it runs, because a message box must not block `DllMain` or the game's thread that first asks for the time.
`StartModuleThread(thread, what)` starts such a thread - this one, the one for the late reports (below, `late report`), RageOpenV's start-up check and its check for packs made for GTA V Legacy (`Legacy packs check`, section 11.6), DirectStorage's checks and its `OpenFile` summary, the static bounds check (`static bounds check`, section 11.15), and the measurements' threads (section 6.6) and the sampling threads of RayTracing (`ray tracing buffer sampling`, section 11.13), the lighting capacity (`lighting capacity sampling`, section 11.16.3), the model budgets (`population sampling`, section 11.16.4), the ped pool (`ped pool sampling`, section 11.16.5) and the render queues (`render queues sampling`, section 11.16.6), which never end and keep it - with the extra reference to the module, which the thread drops when it ends; one that cannot start is logged, `The <what> thread could not be started (Windows error N).`; the next problem or finding reported tries that thread again, for the start-up summary's and for the late reports' alike, and with none to come the start-up's problems stand in the log alone.
Both threads of this module run inside a `try`: a box that cannot be built is logged (`Unexpected exception while showing the start-up summary - the box is not shown; the problems are logged above.`) and skipped, and the next late problem starts a new thread.

A problem reported after the summary was shown - a part that stops working later, such as a file in the mods folder that RageOpenV cannot open - is shown by a thread of its own a second later, in a box with the heading `TweaksV: a part stopped working after the start-up:` and the last line `The game continues without the affected parts.`, logged as `Showing a problem after the start-up summary (N problem(s)).`.
A finding is no part failing: the part is doing its job and has found what the player must know.
`ReportFinding(tag, finding, what)` logs it as `PROBLEM: <what>` too, but it is never an item of the start-up summary, whose heading and last line do not fit it: it goes to the same thread whether the summary has been shown or not, into a box a second later, logged as `Showing a finding (N finding(s)).`.
Each kind of finding has its heading and its last line, in a table in `TweaksVSummary`: `Finding::LegacyPacks`, what the check for packs made for GTA V Legacy found (section 11.6), `TweaksV: its check of the packs in the mods folder found:` and `The game continues with the packs as they are.`; `Finding::RefusedFiles`, the files a guard of WeaponLimits kept from the game (section 11.10), `TweaksV: WeaponLimits refused files the game has no room for:` and `The game continues without the files refused.`
The thread shows a box per report with something in it, one after the other, at most three boxes of each per session, so that a part that keeps failing cannot fill the screen nor take a finding's box, and a list of more than 40 ends in `• ... and N more.`
After that, and with `ERROR_POPUP=0`, a report is only logged: `Problem after the start-up summary not shown (ERROR_POPUP=0): N problem(s), see above.`, `Problem after the start-up summary not shown: 3 message boxes were shown already this session.`, `Finding not shown (ERROR_POPUP=0): N finding(s), see above.`, `Finding not shown: 3 message boxes were shown already this session.`
Test builds show no box while the environment variable `TWEAKSV_TEST_NO_BOXES` is `1` or `2`, so the test suites never wait on one: the text is built as for the player, and in the box's place they log `TEST BUILD: message box not shown (TWEAKSV_TEST_NO_BOXES=1): <heading> ... <last line>`.
With `2` the whole text follows on a line of its own, `TEST BUILD: the text of that box: <text>`, each line break a ` | `, which a suite reads for the items, `... and N more.` and the log's path; only then, since the box repeats the words of the problems, which the suites count.

### 6.5 The console

`CONSOLE=1` opens a console window (`AllocConsole`) once the header is written, and it mirrors every log line from `Console opened (CONSOLE=1).` on; the RageOpenV part then NOPs the game's call that would hide it (section 11.6).
The console is a testing aid: when it cannot be opened or kept, the log says so and nothing is reported.

### 6.6 The measurements (`TweaksVMeasure`)

`DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`'s eleven measurements of the parts - the pool memory's use (HeapAdjuster), the box streamer's searches, the decal definitions registered, RageOpenV's file requests and archive headers, DirectStorage's opens in the game folder, the blip pool's slots in use, the `weapons.meta` files and the weapon component definitions loaded (WeaponLimits), the props per anchor of the ped models (PedProps), the streamed textures and buffers against their budget (Resources) and the ray tracing buffer's use (RayTracing) - the six of the parts measured first (section 11.16), DirectStorage's `UNLOCK_FILES` summary and the weapon infos guard's report (section 11.10.1) share one loop.
A part describes its measurement as a `Measurement { thread, firstMs, numbers, summary, start }` - the thread's name, when the first summary comes, a function whose number changes whenever the summary has something new to say, the function that writes the summary, given that number, and, if given, an event the part sets at the first thing it counts, which the thread waits for before `firstMs` begin, so that a hook never starts a thread itself (the `UNLOCK_FILES` summary, 10 seconds after the first open counted; an event that could not be made starts the clock at once) - and starts it with `StartMeasurement`, which starts its thread through `StartModuleThread` (section 6.4); the thread never ends, so it keeps its reference to the module.
The thread writes the first summary `firstMs` in - `kFirstSummaryMs`, 10 seconds, or `kSummaryEveryMs`, a minute, for DirectStorage's open summary - always, and then one every minute while the number changes - in a test build every `TWEAKSV_TEST_SUMMARY_MS` milliseconds when that is set (section 13.1) - inside a `try`, so nothing leaves the thread.
A measurement whose thread cannot start is logged (`The box streamer summary thread could not be started (Windows error 8).`, the names being `pool memory summary`, `box streamer summary`, `decal definitions summary`, `RageOpenV summary`, `DirectStorage open summary`, `UNLOCK_FILES summary`, `blip pool summary`, `weapon infos summary`, `weapon components summary`, `ped props summary`, `texture budget summary` and `ray tracing buffer summary`, and for the parts measured first `siren settings summary`, `siren count summary`, `lighting capacity summary`, `population summary`, `ped pool summary` and `render queues summary`), not reported: it is an aid that changes nothing in the game (section 12) - but for the weapon infos guard's report, `weapon infos guard report`, which is the guard's own: its thread not started, the guard is not set up, a problem of the part (section 11.10.1).
RayTracing's measurement samples its buffer every 50 ms on a thread of its own beside the loop, `ray tracing buffer sampling` (section 11.13).
The lighting capacity's samples its numbers every 10 ms on a thread of its own, `lighting capacity sampling` (section 11.16.3), the model budgets' both budgets once a second, `population sampling`, each summary taking a sample of its own so that it comes without that thread too (section 11.16.4), the ped pool's its numbers once a second, `ped pool sampling` (section 11.16.5), and the render queues' every 20 ms, `render queues sampling` (section 11.16.6).
`DecodePoolPointer(flag, value, key, rol, add)`, beside them, decodes a pool's pointer as the game keeps it, for a measurement that reads a pool: three fields and two constants of that pool, its code's `rol` and `add`, or 0 while the flag's bit 0 is clear, the pool not made yet.
`Misplaced(at, size, unit)`, beside them, says what keeps bytes from being the game's data read `unit` bytes at a time, each read whole, `not inside the game` or `not aligned to 8 bytes`, and `DataFault` adds `cannot be read`, with the verb a sentence about them goes on with; every part that reads the game's data checks it through them, a caller naming another stretch of the game in place of the whole (the render queues, the game's `.data`) and each its own words around the reason.
`kMaxLinesPerKind`, 20,000, is the cap of every kind of line that comes per event (section 12).

## 7. The conflict check (`TweaksVConflicts`)

`CheckForConflicts()` looks in the game folder and its `scripts` and `plugins` subfolders - the three places an ASI loader loads from, `kLoaderFolders` in `TweaksVConflicts.h`, which DirectStorage's look for `DirectStorageFix.asi` uses too - for every old plugin and every plugin that stays of every feature (section 4.3).
An old plugin found switches its part off for the session (`DisableFeatureForSession`) and is reported: `Old plugin found: <path>. TweaksV's <Feature> part is switched off for this session so nothing is patched twice. Delete the old file - TweaksV replaces it.` `DecalPatch.asi` is the old plugin of two parts and switches both off; `StaticBoundLimitAdjuster.asi` and `StaticBoundsLimitAdjuster.asi`, the two names of one original, are both old plugins of StaticBounds.
A plugin that stays does the same while its part is on in `TweaksV.ini` - `IsEnabledInIni`, not `IsFeatureOn`, so that an old plugin found before it, which switched the part off already, does not keep it from being said - and is reported in words of its own, with the switch that stops the message: `<path> is installed and does this job too (<description>): TweaksV's <Feature> part is switched off while it is there, so nothing is patched twice. To stop this message, set ENABLED=0 under [<SECTION>] in TweaksV.ini.` `onigiri.asi` is the plugin that stays of two parts and reports both, `RageOpenV` under `[RAGEOPENV]` and `StraightToStoryMode` under `[STRAIGHTTOSTORYMODE]`, and `ResourceExtensions.asi` the plugin that stays of four, `HeapAdjuster`, `PackfileLimit`, `Blips` and `RayTracing`; a part already off in `TweaksV.ini` gets no item, only a line in the log, not a problem: `Conflict check: <path> is installed; TweaksV's <Feature> part is off in TweaksV.ini already.` With no file of either kind found the log says `Conflict check: none of the plugins that do the job of one of TweaksV's parts were found.`; when a plugin that stays is found next to parts that are all off, its lines are written instead, so the log always shows what the check found.
`IsPluginInstalledFor(section)` answers whether that one search found a plugin that does the job of a part - an old plugin or one that stays - whatever `TweaksV.ini` says of the part, and looks for no file itself: false when the check found none or did not run (`CONFLICT_CHECK=0`).
It is for a part that leaves the game's code to such a plugin even while `TweaksV.ini` switches that part off: StaticBounds, whose check would hook the script programs Blips' plugins work on (section 11.15).
`IsInsideGameImage(p)` is the shared test for an address inside `GTA5_Enhanced.exe`'s image, from its PE headers read once.
`IsInsideGameData(p)` is the same for the section the game's section table names `.data`, for the one write TweaksV makes outside the game's code (section 11.14).

## 8. The scanner (`TweaksVScanner`)

The scanner finds TweaksV's patterns in the running game's code.
`GTA5_Enhanced.exe` is encrypted on disk, so the code is only readable in memory, and a game update changes it: what the scanner finds is the one thing that must be maintained after an update (PATTERNS.md, "When Game Updates").

- The image is read once: `GTA5_Enhanced.exe`'s base and size from the module information, its executable sections from the PE headers (read only where they lie inside the image; with no executable section the whole image is searched).
- Every search reads the readable parts of those sections afresh (`ReadableCode`: `VirtualQuery` over each section, committed and readable pages only, neighbouring regions joined), because TweaksV itself changes protections between two searches and a page the game protects must be skipped, not crash the game.
- A pattern's text is parsed into bytes and a mask (`ParsePattern`: tokens of exactly two hex digits, or `?`; any other token, or a text of wildcards only, refuses the pattern - nothing in the parser can throw - logged as `Pattern <label> is not valid (treated as not found): <text>`), and compiled with the positions of its two rarest fixed bytes, rarest by how often each byte occurs in the first 256 KB of the game's code (`ByteCounts`, read once).
  This only changes how fast the search is, never what it finds.
- `FindIn` compares those two bytes at 16 positions at a time with SSE2 and checks the whole pattern only where both match; a match must lie wholly inside one readable range.
- `Scanner::FindOnce(pattern, why)` returns the one match or `nullptr`: it asks `FindAll(pattern, 2)`, and a pattern that matches twice is not used, because what would be written over cannot be known - logged as `Pattern <label> matches 2 or more times - not used (the site must be unique).` It leaves the reason in `why` unless `why` holds one already, so that a part looks for all its patterns, the log naming each one missing, and refuses its job with the first, in the words of `Scanner::NotAsOften(pattern, found, expected)`, which end with no point, since the part's sentence ends with its own: `<label> was not found`, `the game code holds <label> 2 or more times, not the expected 1`, `... 1 time, not the expected 2` for too few (with `FindAll`, the relocation core's anchors and PedProps).
  `Scanner::FindAll(pattern, limit)` returns every match in address order up to `limit` (a site the game must have exactly n times is asked for n + 1, so that an n + 1th match shows); both log a miss as `Pattern <label> not found: <bytes>` and, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, the line of section 6.3 with the RVA, the number of matches (exact up to 99, `100+` from 100 on) and the bytes of the match, 32 at most, plus one line for each of the first ten matches with 16 bytes before and 48 from its start, for a pattern that matches more than once, so that a longer pattern can be chosen that matches only the place it is meant for.
  The scanner's tag is `[Scanner]`.
- `Scanner::FindRipRelativeLeas(target, limit)` finds every `lea r64, [rip+disp32]` (a REX prefix with the W bit, opcode `8D`, ModRM mod 00 r/m 101: 7 bytes) in the readable code whose displacement leads to `target`, in address order up to `limit`, with the same 16-at-a-time comparison on the opcode and ModRM bytes.
  It is how the relocation core finds the loads of an array the game loads in places no pattern can tell apart.
  `Scanner::FindRipRelativeReferences(target, limit)` is the same search for every `lea r64, [rip+disp32]` and every `mov [rip+disp32], r64` (the same prefix and ModRM, opcode `8D` or `89`), one helper behind both: how the core checks that the references an array's patterns name are all the game has.
  Nothing is logged.
- `Scanner::FindCallsTo(target, limit)` finds every `call rel32` (`E8` and a rel32: 5 bytes) in the readable code that leads to `target`, in address order up to `limit`, with the same 16-at-a-time comparison on two bytes: the opcode, and the rel32's lowest byte, which a call at p must hold as the lowest byte of target - (p + 5), so that over 16 positions in a row those bytes count down by one; the whole rel32 is compared only where both match.
  It is how the PedProps part checks that the game calls a function exactly as often as the research found (section 11.11).
  Nothing is logged.
- `Scanner::FindRipRelativeStores(target, size, limit)` finds every instruction in the readable code that writes to memory at `[rip+disp32]` and so to any of the `size` bytes from `target`, in address order up to `limit`, each given by its first byte, its prefixes counted: a `mov` of 1 to 8 bytes from a register or an immediate, the read-modify-writes of the integer unit (`add`, `or`, `adc`, `sbb`, `and`, `sub`, `xor`, `inc`, `dec`, `not`, `neg`, the shifts and rotations, `xchg`, `xadd`, `cmpxchg`, `cmpxchg8b` and `16b`, `setcc`, `bts`, `btr`, `btc`, `shld`, `shrd`, `pop`) and the SSE and AVX moves and extracts of 1 to 32 bytes (`movss` to `movdqu`, `movnti`, `movbe`, `pextr*`, `extractps`, their VEX forms, `vextract*128`, `vcvtps2ph`, the masked moves); x87 stores and AVX-512's are not looked for.
  Every ModRM of mod 00 and r/m 101 is found 16 positions at a time with SSE2, as the other searches find their bytes, and its disp32 checked to lead near the range - within the 4 bytes of an immediate and the 32 of the widest store before it - before the bytes in front of it are read every way an instruction could start among them, since any of them may end the instruction before instead: as one with a VEX prefix (`C4` with a map of 1 to 3, or `C5`), then with the escapes `0F 38`, `0F 3A` and `0F`, then with a one-byte opcode, the last two each with a REX right before the opcode and up to four legacy prefixes before that, and a reading whose `F3` or `F2` makes a store a load (`movq`'s, `crc32`'s) once more without that prefix and the bytes before it.
  The operand counts when any reading is a store that writes a byte of the range, given by that reading's first byte: a byte the instruction before ends with - a `0F` taken for an escape, a `C4` or `C5` for a VEX prefix, an `F3` for a prefix - hides no writer, and a load whose bytes read as a store some way (`movups`'s `0F 10` and `vmovups`'s `C5 FC 10` as `adc r/m8, r8` behind a `0F` or a `C5`, `movq`'s `F3 0F 7E` as `movd` behind an `F3`) is counted as one, given by the byte that reading starts at, which is safe where a missed writer is not; one scan of the code either way.
  The immediate after the disp32 counts, since the rip a store's operand is counted from lies past it; a prefix that narrows a store (`66`, `F2`, `F3`) is not counted for its width, since it may belong to the instruction before, so a store is taken for at most as wide as it can be, never narrower.
  It is how the texture budget checks that the start-up's two stores are the budgets' only writers (section 11.12).
  Nothing is logged.
- Test builds: `TEST_BREAK_PATTERN=<label>` makes that pattern count as not found (`TEST BUILD: pattern <label> is treated as not found (TEST_BREAK_PATTERN).`), to watch its part be skipped cleanly.
- Test builds: `TEST_TWICE_PATTERN=<label>` makes that pattern count as held once more than the game code holds it, twice for one that must match once, the match handed on twice (`TEST BUILD: pattern <label> is treated as held once more than the game code holds it (TEST_TWICE_PATTERN).`), to watch its part refuse it in the words of `NotAsOften`; no part writes to a pattern held too often.

The pattern parser is an altered version of ScriptHookVDotNet's memory scanner (THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md); the search is TweaksV's own.

## 9. The patch module (`TweaksVPatch`)

Every change to the game's code goes through this module, so that every part writes the same way and no part has to know about page protection, `DRY_RUN` or `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`.

- **`WriteCode(writes, tag)`**: `writes` is a list of `CodeWrite { address, bytes, what }`.
  A write without address or bytes stops everything.
  With `DRY_RUN` nothing is written and each write is logged as `DRY_RUN: would patch the <what>: <old> -> <new>`.
  Otherwise every page any write touches is made writable first (`PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE` for a page that was executable, `PAGE_READWRITE` otherwise; a page that is already writable is left; a page that is not committed memory, a guard page, or one `VirtualProtect` refuses stops everything before a byte is written, with the protections changed so far put back, and the reason logged), then all bytes are written, then every page gets its own protection back, newest first - through a guard that runs on every exit, an exception included; nothing that can throw runs while a page is writable, so the old bytes for the log are read before and the lines written after - and `FlushInstructionCache` makes the processor see the new code; with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` each write is logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: <what> at <address>: <old> -> <new>`.
  False means nothing was written.
  `BytesOf(value)` gives the bytes of a value for a write.
- **`RedirectCalls(redirects, alsoWrite, tag)`**: `redirects` is a list of `CallRedirect { site, function, what, original }`, each a 5-byte relative call (`E8 rel32`) in the game to send to `function`; `original`, when given, receives the function the call led to, and is set before the call is rewritten, so a hook that calls it is ready the moment it can run.
  A site that is not a call stops everything.
  With `DRY_RUN` each is logged as `DRY_RUN: would redirect the <what> at RVA ...` and only `WriteCode(alsoWrite)` runs, which writes nothing either.
  Otherwise: a block of 16 bytes per call is allocated near the game (`AllocateNearGame`, read/write), each stub written as `FF 25 00 00 00 00` followed by the 8-byte address of `function` and two `CC`, the block made read+execute and never changed again (a call stub is never writable and executable at once), and then, in one `WriteCode`, every call's rel32 pointed at its stub together with `alsoWrite`.
  A call reaches only 2 GB, which is why the stubs sit near the game; a stub out of reach, no memory near the game, or a block that cannot be made read+execute stops everything with nothing changed.
- **`HookFunctions(hooks, tag)`** hooks functions at their start with MinHook: `FunctionHook { function, hook, original, what }`; every hook is created and queued, then all are switched on together (`MH_ApplyQueued`: the threads are frozen once per group, not once per hook); if any step fails, the hooks made so far are removed again and the reason logged: `<what> could not be hooked (MinHook: <status>)` when a hook cannot be created or queued, `The N hooks could not be switched on (MinHook: <status>)` when they cannot be applied, with ` - none of the N hooks is on.` for a group of more than one.
  `original` receives MinHook's trampoline.
  **`HookExport(module, function, hook, original, target, tag)`** hooks an exported function of a loaded DLL at once (`GetSystemTimeAsFileTime`, DirectStorage's exports, `NtCreateFile`, `NtSetInformationFile`) and hands back its address in `target`; **`DisableHook(function, tag)`** switches a hook off again, the function's own bytes back and the trampoline kept for a thread that is inside the hook right now.
  MinHook is initialised on first use; its trampolines are writable and executable, an accepted open issue (NOTES.md, T-HARDEN).
- **`AllocateNearGame(size)`**: `size` bytes, read/write, committed and zeroed, within 1 GB below the game's image, where a rel32 in the game's code reaches: the free regions below the image are walked by allocation granularity, as MinHook's own allocator does, and the first that gives `size` bytes is taken.
  For the moved arrays and the call stubs.
- The readers: `CallTarget(site)` (the target of an `E8 rel32`, or `nullptr`), `Rel32Target(address)` (where a rel32 or a rip-relative disp32 at that address leads), `StartsWithJump(address)` (`E9`, `EB` or `FF 25`: the usual sign of another mod's hook), `FunctionStartOf(address)` (the start of the function the game's unwind data puts an address in, or `nullptr`), `IsFunctionStart(address, detail)` (the game's unwind data, asked of Windows with `RtlLookupFunctionEntry`, says so - `a function start (unwind data)`; an address inside a function gives `inside the function at RVA ... (unwind data)`; for code without unwind data the byte before it, read only where it is readable (`no unwind data, the byte before it cannot be read`), must be `CC`, `90` or `C3` - `no unwind data, byte before it CC`), `IsReadable(address, size)` (committed, readable, no guard page, over the whole range; used before any byte outside a pattern's match is read), `CopyMemorySafely(target, source, size)` (a copy that catches an access violation and returns false - nothing else is caught - for memory that may change or be freed between a check and the read: the pointers the game's DirectStorage calls hand the DirectStorage part's hooks, the game's heap data a measurement or a guard walks while the game changes it, sections 11.10 to 11.16), `DescribeAddress`, `HexBytes`.
- One copy of what the parts' log lines and stubs need: `DescribeAddress(address)`, `HexBytes(bytes, size)`, `Hex(value)` (`0x4B`), `Hex32(value)` (`0x2CDFE406`, all eight digits, for a pool's hash), `At(pattern)` (` (T-E-B1)`, the pattern a site comes from), `Holds(at)` (`holds 0x1C`, an offset a site's instruction carries, for the `DIAGNOSTIC` lines), `Printable(name)` (a name from a file or the game's memory with every byte outside printable ASCII made `?`, so that a line stays one line) and `PageSize()`.
- One template for a value as it lies in memory, which need not be aligned - an operand in the game's code, a field of its data or of a file: `ValueAt<T>(at, offset)`, read only once the bytes are known to be readable.
  `Load<T>(at)` reads a value of the game's data whole, which its threads may write meanwhile and its caller has checked to be aligned.

## 10. The relocation core (`TweaksVRelocation`)

Six parts move one of the game's static arrays to a larger one and raise the limit the game's code carries for it: the modkit array, the packfile array, the box streamer's traversal list, the decal definitions array, the weapon component list, whose size no instruction carries - it is the distance to the count that follows the list - and the siren setting ID table, whose size no setting chooses.
They describe their array in tables; the core does the work once for all of them.

### 10.1 The tables

- `ReferenceSite { offset, opcode, opcodeSize }`: where a match holds the rel32 of a `lea r64, [rip+disp32]` or of a rip-relative `mov` that addresses the array, so that the array is the address after the rel32 plus its value; `opcode` are bytes the instruction must start with, right before the rel32, or none.
- `LimitSite { kind, offset, opcode, opcodeSize, valueOffset, mul, add, end }`: `Immediate`, an imm32 at `valueOffset` that holds `N x mul + add` with N the array's size; `Variable`, the rel32 at `valueOffset` of a variable the game compares against, which holds `N + add`; or `Follower`, for an array whose size no instruction carries, the disp32 at `valueOffset` of an instruction that ends at `end` - an immediate may follow the disp32 - and addresses a variable lying right after the array: the distance from the array to it is N, in entries, and the variable is left as it is, never written (the weapon component list's count); `offset` is the instruction, `opcode` its first bytes, or none.
- `Anchor { pattern, matches, hasReference, reference, limitCount, limits[3] }`: a pattern of the part, how often it must match (1, or more for a site the game repeats), and what every match holds: a reference, up to three limits, both, or neither - a site the part reads itself through the plan.
  The builders: `Rel32(offset, opcode, opcodeSize)`, `Imm32(imm32, mul, add)`, `Imm32(instruction, opcode, opcodeSize, imm32, mul, add)`, `Variable(rel32, add)`, `Follower(instruction, opcode, opcodeSize, disp32, end)`, `Site(pattern)`, `Reference(pattern, site)`, `Limit(pattern, site)`, `Limits(pattern, matches, a, b[, c])`, `Repeated(pattern, matches, limit)`, `ReferenceAndLimit(pattern, ref, limit)`, `ReferenceAndLimits(pattern, ref, a, b)`.
- `RelocationSpec { tag, name, sizeName, job, entrySize, maxEntries, setting, anchors, scannedReferences, leaPrefixes, leaPrefixCount, expectedReferences, emptyEntry }`: the part's log tag, the array's name for the log (`modkit array`, `box streamer's traversal list`), the name of its size (`modkit list size`), the start of every failure line (`The modkit limit is not changed`), the bytes per entry, the new array's size (always allocated in full; the setting's maximum or more: the weapon component list holds one file more), the setting that asks for the size, or none for an array of a fixed size, `maxEntries`, every value of its index having its entry (the siren setting ID table), the anchors, for an array whose loads no pattern can tell apart exactly how many `lea` must load it, found by its address, with the REX bytes they may start with (`48` or `4C` for the decals and the weapon component list; any with REX.W for the box streamer), and for an array moved by its anchors' references how many references the game's code has to it (`expectedReferences`: 6 for the modkit array, 33 for the packfile array; 0, not checked, for the others), and `emptyEntry`, the byte the new array's entries past the game's own hold: zero, as Windows gives it, for five of them, 0xFF, a free ID, for the siren setting ID table.
- `RelocationPlan { array, gameSize, size, matches, references, limits }`: what was found: the game's own array, its size, the size to use, every anchor's matches (for the sites a part reads itself), the rel32 fields that are moved, the limits as `FoundLimit { value, mul, add }`.

### 10.2 `FindRelocation(spec, plan)`

Everything is found and checked before anything is written, and the first failure reports `<job> this session: <why>` and leaves the game as it is.
A spec whose tables name no reference, or no limit, is refused as the part's own mistake.
The anchors are worked through in table order - each one's matches, then for each match its reference and its limits - so a missing pattern late in the table is reported only after the sites before it passed:

1. The anchor must match exactly as often as it says (`FindAll` asks for one more): `<label> was not found.` or `the game code holds <label> N [or more] times, not the expected M.` (` or more` when more were found than expected).
2. Its reference: the instruction is readable and starts with the opcode when one is given (`is not the expected instruction (<bytes>)`); its target is logged with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` (`DIAGNOSTIC: reference at RVA ... -> RVA ... (<label>)`); the first reference's target must lie inside the game (`the <array> is not inside the game: <address> (another mod may have moved it already).`), every other must point at the same address (`the references to the <array> do not all point to the game's own array: <a> and <b> (<label>).`).
3. Its limits: readable, the expected instruction; a variable must lie inside the game; its value decodes to N through its encoding (`holds V, which is <encoding> for no size` when it does not divide), logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: limit[ variable] at RVA ... holds V (<encoding>, <label>) -> game size N`; a follower's variable must lie inside the game (`the variable that follows the <array> is not inside the game: <address> (<label>).`) and a whole number of entries after the array (`the variable at RVA ... does not lie a whole number of E-byte entries after the <array> at RVA ... (<label>).`), the distance N entries, logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: limit variable at RVA ... lies D bytes after the <array> (<label>) -> game size N`, and a table that names a follower before a reference to the array is the part's own mistake (`its tables name the variable that follows the <array> before a reference to it.`); the first size decoded in the whole job must be plausible (`the game's own <sizeName>, N, is not plausible.` beyond `maxEntries` or zero), every other must agree (`the limits of the <array> disagree about the game's own size: A and B (<label>).`).

Then:

4. The array is logged: `DIAGNOSTIC: the <array> is at RVA ... - N entries of E bytes`.
5. The references that are moved: the anchors' references, or - with `scannedReferences` - every `lea` that loads the array (`FindRipRelativeLeas`, each logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: reference at RVA ... -> RVA ... (lea scan)`), which must be exactly that many (`the game code loads the <array> in N [or more] places, not the expected M (another mod may have moved some of them, or the game has changed).`) and each start with an allowed prefix (`the load of the <array> at RVA ... is not a "lea r64, [rip+disp32]" with a 48 or 4C prefix: <7 bytes>.`).
6. With `expectedReferences`, the anchors' references must be every one the game has, because one no pattern names would keep the game's own, smaller array while the limits are raised: `FindRipRelativeReferences` (each site logged with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` as `DIAGNOSTIC: reference at RVA ... -> RVA ... (scan)`, the RVA of its rel32, as the anchors' lines have it) must find exactly that many (`the game code holds N [or more] references to the <array>, not the expected M (another mod may have moved some of them, or the game has changed).`), and every one must be an anchor's rel32 (`the reference to the <array> at RVA ... is not one its patterns name (another mod may have moved some of them, or the game has changed).`).
7. An array without a setting takes `maxEntries`: there is nothing to clamp and nothing to report. Else the size to use: the setting, clamped to the game's own size..the setting's maximum - where the game's own size lies past that maximum it is all there is to use, and the part decides whether its array has room for it - reported when it lay outside (`TweaksV.ini: <KEY>=x is outside A..B - using C.`, `ReportFailure`), so that the start-up summary names the size really used - the settings' own report (section 4.1) names only the table's limits.
   Smaller than the game's own would shrink the array.

### 10.3 `ApplyRelocation(spec, plan, alsoWrite, redirects, also)`

With `DRY_RUN`: `DRY_RUN: would move the <array> (N references) and raise its limit from A to B (M sites)<also>.` - ` (M sites)` only where a limit is written - and nothing else.
Otherwise the game's own entries must be readable (`the game's own <array> cannot be read.`), the new array is allocated in full (`entrySize x maxEntries`, `AllocateNearGame`; `the new <array> could not be allocated near the game.` when that fails) and the game's own entries are copied into it, the rest holding `emptyEntry` - so an array the image initialises keeps its content - every reference's rel32 is recomputed for it with the int32 distance check (`the new <array> is out of reach of the game code at RVA ...`), every limit is re-encoded for the new size (`N x mul + add`) - a follower stays as it is - the part's own writes are added, and all of it is written in one `WriteCode` - or, when the part redirects calls too, one `RedirectCalls` - all or nothing (`the game code could not be changed (details in TweaksV.log).` when not).
Then `The <array> moved and its limit raised from A to B entries.` The write lines carry `<array> reference` and `<array> limit` as their `what`.

What does not fit the core stays in the part's file and uses the plan: the packfile's hooked call, the box streamer's counted calls, the decals' count, loop and measurement, the weapon components' count, pool size call and measurement, the siren IDs' own writes, their base loader's call and their measurement (section 11).

## 11. The parts

Each part reads its switches once, at its start-up (RageOpenV's runs inside its one-shot time hook); the hooks that run per call never read them.
The game facts - the patterns, what each match holds, the offsets - are PATTERNS.md's; NOTES.md lists each part's behaviour and measurements.
Here is the mechanism.

### 11.1 HeapAdjuster (`InitHeapAdjuster`)

The game sizes its memory heap with one immediate in its start-up code: the imm32 of a `mov edx, imm32` that `H-E1` matches, the size in bytes at +1.
`HEAP_SIZE` in MB, 1..2047 - the most whole MB that imm32 holds as a positive int32.
