Steps: the match must start with `BA`; the value must be a whole number of MB other than 0 (`the game code found holds an unexpected value (0x...)`), logged with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` as `DIAGNOSTIC: heap size at RVA ... holds 646 MB`; a value of 1024 MB or more - up to 4095 MB, since the `mov` zero-extends its imm32, past what `HEAP_SIZE` allows too - is not the game's own (646 MB on the current build): another heap adjuster set it, and the part leaves it, logs `The heap size is not changed this session: another heap adjuster already set it to N MB - left as it is.` and reports nothing; the size to use is the setting clamped to the size found..2047, reported when it lay outside (`TweaksV.ini: HEAP_SIZE=100 is outside 646..2047 - using 646.`), so that the start-up summary names the size really used; a heap that already has the size asked for is logged and left (`Heap size is 646 MB already (HEAP_SIZE=100) - nothing to patch.`); otherwise one `WriteCode` of the new imm32, computed in 64-bit arithmetic, `what` = `heap size (A MB -> B MB)`, then `Heap size patched: A MB -> B MB.` Failures: `The heap size is not changed this session: H-E1 was not found.`, `... the game code found is not the expected instruction (<bytes>).`, `... the game code could not be made writable (details in TweaksV.log).`

A pool of the game's does not draw on that heap: it draws on a budget of its own, kept by a big-block allocator (PATTERNS.md, "A pool's memory"), past which an allocation returns null and the game writes through it, the crash at RVA 0x15A514.
`POOL_MEMORY` in MB, 136..2047, 256 by default, its limits the settings' (section 4.1): `H-E2`, the allocator's set-up in the same start-up function as `H-E1`, holds the budget as the imm32 of a `mov r9d, imm32` at +22, and the part writes it at the same moment.
It is a ceiling on a counter, so raising it allocates nothing; and it is a job of its own, so that either goes on without the other.
Steps: logged with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` as `DIAGNOSTIC: pool memory budget at RVA ... holds 136 MB, its allocator at RVA ..., its heap at RVA ..., its constructor's call at RVA ... -> RVA ... (H-E2)`; any value but the game's 136 MB is another mod's or a game update's, left, logged and no problem (`The pool memory budget is not changed this session: another mod or a game update already set it to 200 MB - left as it is.`, or `... to 0x0C800001 bytes - ...`); a key at 136 is logged and left (`Pool memory budget is 136 MB already (POOL_MEMORY=136) - nothing to patch.`).
Then what the write relies on: the constructor's call, whose rel32 lies past the match, can be read (`... its constructor's call at RVA ... cannot be read (H-E2).`, a problem), and the allocator, the heap and the constructor lie inside the game: what lies outside is another mod's, left, logged and no problem (`... its allocator at 0x... (outside the game) is another mod's (H-E2) - left as it is.`, and the same of `its heap` and `its constructor`).
Then one `WriteCode` of the new imm32, computed in 64-bit arithmetic, `what` = `pool memory budget (A MB -> B MB)`, then `Pool memory budget patched: A MB -> B MB.` Failures: `The pool memory budget is not changed this session: H-E2 was not found.`, `... the game code could not be made writable (details in TweaksV.log).`
Its effect comes later, when the game's start-up hands the immediate to the allocator's constructor, so that it is checked at the write: an allocator whose +0x20 already holds the game's own 136 MB, read after `IsReadable`, was set up before TweaksV started, and the write, made all the same as the heap's is, comes too late: `The pool memory budget is not changed this session: the game set its allocator up before TweaksV started, with 136 MB, so the new value at its site comes too late.`, a problem, with `DRY_RUN` too and with no `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`.
A field of 0, or of any other value, says nothing: nothing has proved what an allocator not yet set up holds, so no write is refused on it.
`FindPoolMemoryBudget(why)` gives the imm32's address to the ped pool's module (section 11.16.5), which works with the part off too: `H-E2` is looked for once, whoever asks first.
With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` the part measures the budget as it leaves it - with `DRY_RUN` and with another mod's value too - through `StartMeasurement`, thread `pool memory summary`: the allocator at the rel32 at +3 must be inside the game, aligned to 8 bytes and readable, else `DIAGNOSTIC: the pool memory is not measured: its allocator at ... is not inside the game (H-E2).` (`... is not aligned to 8 bytes ...`, `... cannot be read ...`), and so too `... H-E2 was not found.` and `... its summary thread could not be started.`, all logged, not reported.
It reads the allocator's +0x20 to +0x40 without a lock, each whole, after `IsReadable`, and a summary comes when the budget, the use, the least room or the blocks change: `DIAGNOSTIC: pool memory: 134.2 of 256 MB in use, at least 121.8 MB free so far, 412 blocks (900 ends the game).`, cut to a tenth of a MB; less than 1 MB of least room adds ` - nearly spent: a pool made now can crash the game at RVA 0x15A514 on 1.0.1158.16`, a line of the log and no box (D31).
The two things a pattern cannot pin are checked each time, and each adds a line before the numbers: `DIAGNOSTIC: pool memory: its allocator at RVA ... holds a budget of 136 MB, not the 256 MB its site holds: the game set it up before TweaksV started, or its layout is not as the research found it.`, and `DIAGNOSTIC: pool memory: 135.5 MB in use and 121.5 MB of room do not add up to its budget of 256 MB: the game may have been changing them, or its layout is not as the research found it.`
A budget of 0 is `DIAGNOSTIC: no pool memory yet - the game has not set its allocator up.`, and fields that cannot be read `DIAGNOSTIC: pool memory: its allocator at RVA ... or its site at RVA ... cannot be read.`

### 11.2 ModkitLimit (`InitModkitLimit`)

The game's array of vehicle modkit IDs: 1024 entries of 2 bytes on the current build, loaded by six `lea` (`M-E-R1..R6`, the rel32 at the offset PATTERNS.md gives) and sized by eight immediates (`M-E-L1..L8`: N-1, N+3, N+3, N+3, N+3, 2N, N-1, N-1; `M-E-L2`'s, which lies past its pattern, checked as the imm32 of `cmp rdi, imm32`, `48 81 FF`).
Entirely on the core: spec `{ "ModkitLimit", "modkit array", "modkit list size", "The modkit limit is not changed", 2 bytes, 65536 entries, MODKITARRAY_SIZE, the 14 anchors, no scanned references, 6 expected references }` - the six leas must be every reference the game's code has to the array; `FindRelocation`, then `ApplyRelocation` with nothing of its own.
Modkit IDs are 16-bit, which caps the array at 65536.

### 11.3 PackfileLimit (`InitPackfileLimit`)

The game's array of RPF archives: 7260 entries of 8 bytes on the current build, referenced by 33 instructions - the 32 of `P-E-R01..R30`, `P-E-R32` and `P-E-R33` on every build, plus `P-E-R31a` on builds from 1.0.1158.13 or `P-E-R31b` before; the references of `P-E-R09` and `P-E-R17`, which lie past their patterns, checked as `lea rcx` (`48 8D 0D`), `P-E-R32`'s as `lea r15`, `P-E-R33`'s as `lea rcx` - and sized by two limits: an immediate, `P-E-D3` from 1.0.1158.13, `P-E-D2` from 1.0.1013.17, `P-E-D1` before, each a `cmp rcx, imm32`, and a variable the game compares an archive index against (`P-E-I1`, the rel32 of a `cmp eax, [rip+disp32]`), which holds four less than the array's size - the core's `Variable(2, -4)` keeps that margin: the variable must hold N-4 and gets the new size minus four.
The anchors are assembled at start-up by the game's file version (`GameVersionNumber`; without one: `The packfile limit is not changed this session: the game's file version could not be read, so the patterns for this game build cannot be chosen.`), the call site `P-E-H1` last as a `Site`.
After `FindRelocation` the part reads that site: the call at +0x10 of the match must be readable and lead into the game (`the call that reads the game's streaming configuration at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already).`), logged with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` as `DIAGNOSTIC: streaming configuration call at RVA ... -> RVA ...`.
`ApplyRelocation` then takes that call as a redirect, in the same all-or-nothing write as the 33 references and 2 limits, to `SetStreamingInterface(char* configuration)`: it replaces the archive count at offset 24 of the configuration the game read from `gameconfig.xml` with the new size - exactly, also a count above it: the game's own configuration holds 8512 on 1.0.1158.16, more than its array's 7260, so a `PACKFILEARRAY_SIZE` below 8512 lowers the count - logs `The archive count of the game's configuration, 8512, is replaced by 13000.` (nothing may throw there: the log line is in a `try`), and calls the game's own function.
The status lines: the core's, and `The streaming configuration call at RVA ... is redirected: the archive count the game's configuration holds is replaced by N when the game reads it.` Archive indices are 16-bit, which caps the array at 65535.
Spec: `{ "PackfileLimit", "packfile array", "packfile list size", "The packfile limit is not changed", 8 bytes, 65535 entries, PACKFILEARRAY_SIZE, the anchors of the build, no scanned references, 33 expected references }` - the 33 must be every reference the game's code has to the array (25 `lea` and 8 `mov` stores on 1.0.1158.16), which the dry-run line names (`DRY_RUN: would move the packfile array (33 references) and raise its limit from A to B (2 sites) and redirect the streaming configuration call at RVA ...`).

### 11.4 BoxStreamer (`InitBoxStreamer`)

The game's box streamer answers "which map boxes lie in this area" in four query functions that hand one static list of 1000 entries of 8 bytes, and its capacity, 1000, as the fifth argument (`mov dword ptr [rsp+20h], imm32`) to one of two tree searches; a search stops 16 entries short of the capacity (`kSearchHeadroom`, a game fact in PATTERNS.md, which is also the measurement's threshold).
Eight `lea r64, [rip+disp32]` load the list, two per query function; two of the functions are near-identical copies, so the second `lea` of each cannot be told apart by the bytes around it.
The anchors: `B-E-L1` a limit (the capacity's imm32, N), `B-E-L2..L4` a limit and a reference (the `lea r13, [rip+disp32]` that follows), and `scannedReferences` = 8 with any REX.W prefix: the core takes the list's address from the three `lea`, scans the code for every load of it, which must find exactly eight, and moves all eight.
The part's own, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` only: the call to the tree search after each capacity site (offsets 30, 34, 42, 30 in the four matches; the first and third call one search, the second and fourth the other) must be readable and lead into the game (`the tree-search call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already) (B-E-L2).`), and each pair to the same function (`the tree-search calls of the box streamer do not lead to the same function: ... (B-E-L3).`), logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: tree-search call at RVA ... -> RVA ...` - a call that fails either check refuses the whole job, the list included: the one place where `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` changes what a part does (section 12), because the measurement is the reason the switch is on; the four calls are then redirected in the same write as the references and capacities (`... and redirect the 4 tree-search calls (DIAGNOSTIC_MODE)` in the dry-run line) to two counting wrappers, `CountedSearch0` and `CountedSearch1`, which call the game's search with the game's arguments and count the result with atomics - nothing else on the game's streaming thread - and the measurement (section 6.6; its thread, if it cannot start, is logged as `The box streamer summary thread could not be started (...).`) logs, announced by `DIAGNOSTIC: the 4 tree-search calls are counted; a summary follows after 10 seconds, then every minute while the numbers change.`, 10 seconds in and then every minute while the number of searches changes, `DIAGNOSTIC: N searches so far; the largest returned X of 10000 entries; Y returned more than the game's own list (1000 entries) could hold.` - ending `could hold (the list has the game's own size, so no search can exceed it).` when `TRAVERSAL_LIST_SIZE` keeps the game's own size, because a search then stops where the game's own would and Y is 0 by construction.
Spec: `{ "BoxStreamer", "box streamer's traversal list", "box streamer traversal list size", "The box streamer's traversal list is not enlarged", 8 bytes, 100000 entries, TRAVERSAL_LIST_SIZE, 4 anchors, 8 scanned references }`.

### 11.5 Decals (`InitDecals`)

The game reads its decal definitions from the `decals.dat` files into one static array of 260 entries of 80 bytes (the entry layout is in PATTERNS.md), with the count registered so far in a dword 16 bytes before it; once the count is past 259 the parser refuses every further definition, silently.
22 instructions carry the limit as an imm32 (13 hold N-1, 9 hold N), 31 `lea` load the array.
The anchors, in this order: `D-E-L1` a limit (the parser's "full" check, `cmp rsi, N-1`; the disp32 of its `mov esi, [rip+disp32]` at +2 is the count's, read by the part), `D-E-L2` a reference (the parser's `lea r13`), `D-E-L10` and `D-E-L11` sites (the decal manager's start-up loop), `D-E-L3` three limits in one match, `D-E-L4` two, `D-E-L5..L9` one each, `D-E-L12` one limit matching exactly 7 times (the check seven decal-placing functions share), `D-E-L13..L16` one each; `scannedReferences` = 31 with the prefixes `48` and `4C`.
The part's own checks after `FindRelocation`: the count must lie inside the game and the array exactly 16 bytes after it (`the decal definitions array at ... does not lie 16 bytes after its count at ... (another mod may have moved it already).`), logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: the count of registered definitions is at RVA ...`; the start-up loop's `lea` (at +5 of `D-E-L10`, its start offset at +1) must load the array, it must clear a whole number of definitions per pass (the imm32 at +2 of `D-E-L11`, 0x820 = 26 x 80), and its end (the imm32 at +8) must be the start offset + N x 80 (`the end of the decal manager's start-up loop at RVA ... holds V, not its start offset S + N x 80.`), logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: loop end at RVA ... holds 22808 (D-E-L11): the start-up loop clears 26 definitions per pass from offset 2008`.
The part adds one write: the loop end becomes the start offset + 80 x the largest multiple of the entries per pass (26) that is not above the new size - 2028 of the default 2048; the entries it leaves are zero anyway, `what` = `decal definitions array loop end`, `... and the end of its start-up loop` in the dry-run line.
The count stays where it is: nothing reaches it through the array, nor the array through it.
The measurement, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`: nothing is hooked; announced by `DIAGNOSTIC: the number of registered decal definitions is logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while it changes.` (` (DRY_RUN: the game's own array is in use).` in a dry run), the measurement (section 6.6; its thread, if it cannot start, is logged as `The decal definitions summary thread could not be started (...).`) reads the game's count 10 seconds in and then every minute while it changes and logs `DIAGNOSTIC: N decal definitions registered (the game's own array holds 260, this one 2048)` - ` and is in use` in place of `, this one 2048` with `DRY_RUN`, which measures the unpatched game - with ` - the game's own array would have dropped M of them` added once N is past 260 and ` - it is full: any further definitions were dropped` once N reaches the array in use.
A definition's index is a uint16 in the game's decal instances, so a larger array would be possible; 16384 entries (1.3 MB) is the limit TweaksV allows.
Spec: `{ "Decals", "decal definitions array", "decal definitions limit", "The decal definitions limit is not raised", 80 bytes, 16384 entries, DECALDEFS_SIZE, 16 anchors, 31 scanned references, "48 4C" }`.

### 11.6 RageOpenV (`InitRageOpenV`)

Three jobs with a switch each, each done all or nothing and left undone alone, plus the console patch.
`InitRageOpenV` only hooks `kernel32`'s `GetSystemTimeAsFileTime` (`HookExport`; a failure reports `RageOpenV could not start: the GetSystemTimeAsFileTime hook failed (details in TweaksV.log). The mods folder, OPEN archives and the cache check skip are off this session.`).
The hook runs the start-up once, on whichever thread first asks for the time - the claim is an atomic exchanged before the start-up runs, because the start-up can ask for the time itself - then shows the start-up summary from a thread of its own and switches the hook off (`DisableHook`), so the game's later calls go straight to Windows.
`InitRageOpenV` also starts a thread (`StartModuleThread`, which holds a reference to the module; one that cannot start is logged, `The RageOpenV start-up check thread could not be started (...).`, and reported, `The check that RageOpenV's start-up runs is not done this session: its thread could not be started (details in TweaksV.log).`, and `InitRageOpenV` then returns false, so that `DllMain` shows the start-up summary itself, as when the hook failed; the hook stays on, the start-up still runs at the game's first time request and does its jobs, its problems come after the summary, with boxes of their own, and its own call to show the summary does nothing, since the summary is shown once - section 6.4) that waits 30 seconds and, if the start-up has not run by then, claims it through the same atomic, reports `RageOpenV's start-up never ran: the game did not ask for the time through GetSystemTimeAsFileTime - the mods folder, OPEN archives and the cache check skip are off this session.`, shows the start-up summary - which would otherwise never come, for any part - and switches the hook off.
The start-up, inside a `try` (`Unexpected exception during the start-up - the rest of RageOpenV is skipped this session.`; the whole hook body is inside another, so nothing leaves it):

1. **The current directory** is where the mods folder is looked for, because the game's own file functions resolve `mods/...` there too.
   When it is not the game folder - the two compared by their final paths (`FinalPathOf`: `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`, every link followed; by their text when one cannot be read), so that a short name or a junction of the game folder is the game folder - the log says so (`The current directory is <dir>, not the game folder - the mods folder is looked for there.`), and with `MODS_FOLDER=1` a `mods` folder in the game folder, which the game then does not use, is a problem: `Files in the mods folder are not used this session: the game runs in <dir>, not in the game folder.`
2. **The console** (`CONSOLE=1` only): the `call [rip+disp32]` 16 bytes into `R-E-C1`'s match, which must start with `FF 15`, becomes six NOPs through `WriteCode` (`Console hide call patched: ... -> 90 90 90 90 90 90.`); six NOPs there already are another mod's patch, left as they are (`Console hide call already patched (another mod did it) - left as it is.`), and other bytes are logged (`Console: unexpected bytes at RVA ... - left as they are, so the game may hide the console.`).
   Not a problem for the summary when it cannot be.
3. **The cache check** (`SKIP_RPF_CACHE_CHECK`): the call that `R-E-K1` matches must lead into the game (`The RPF cache check is not skipped this session: the call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already).`) and to a function start, checked with `IsFunctionStart` as the computed file-function starts are (`The RPF cache check is not skipped this session: its function at RVA ... is not a function start (no unwind data, byte before it 48).`); a function that already starts with `C3` was skipped by another mod, and one that starts with a jump was hooked by another (`The RPF cache check's function is already hooked by another mod - left as it is.`) - both are left alone, logged, not a problem; otherwise its first two bytes become `C3 90` (`ret; nop`) through `WriteCode` (`RPF cache check patched: 41 57 -> C3 90.`).
4. **The mods folder** (`MODS_FOLDER`): without a `mods` folder in the current directory nothing is hooked (`No mods folder in the current directory - the file functions are not hooked.`; with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` the patterns are still checked first, and the line says so).
   The eight file functions of the game's two file devices - OpenBulk, GetFileTime, GetFileSize, GetAttributes, twice - are found: `R-E-F1`, `F4`, `F5`, `F8` at the function's start, `F2`, `F3` at 0x10D and `F6`, `F7` at 0x15D before the match, those four checked with `IsFunctionStart` (`Files in the mods folder are not used this session: GetFileTime 2 at RVA ... is not a function start (no unwind data, byte before it 48).`); every start must lie inside the game (`... GetFileTime 2 is not inside the game: 0x... (outside the game) (R-E-F6).`) and be readable (`... GetFileTime 2 at RVA ... is not readable memory (R-E-F6).`); one that already starts with a jump is another mod's hook, logged (`... already starts with a jump - another mod may have hooked it; the hook goes on top and chains through.`) and hooked on top - which holds for the four computed starts only: `R-E-F1`, `F4`, `F5` and `F8` match the function's own first bytes, so another mod's hook on OpenBulk or GetAttributes makes their pattern miss and the mods folder is not used (`... R-E-F1 was not found (OpenBulk 1).`); with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` each is logged (`DIAGNOSTIC: OpenBulk 1 at RVA ...`; the four computed starts with what `IsFunctionStart` found: `DIAGNOSTIC: GetFileTime 1 at RVA ... - a function start (unwind data)`).
   A missing pattern reports `Files in the mods folder are not used this session: R-E-F7 was not found (GetFileSize 2).` Then the folder is read once (`ReadModsFolder`, by the mods folder's one walk, `WalkModsFolder` in `TweaksVModsFolder`, which the check for packs made for GTA V Legacy below goes by too: every file and folder under `mods`, relative to the current directory, where the game's own file functions resolve `mods/...`, as a relative path in UTF-8 with `/` between the names - the game's file functions take their paths in UTF-8 on every Windows (PATTERNS.md, "When Game Updates" item 5), so each name is converted from UTF-16 by `WideCharToMultiByte(CP_UTF8, WC_ERR_INVALID_CHARS)` and the ANSI code page plays no part - spelled as the hooks spell the game's paths (`ListSpelling`, below), into an `unordered_set` that is allocated once and never freed, so that no hook can look it up after TweaksV's static objects are destroyed at `DLL_PROCESS_DETACH` - a deliberate leak at the end of the process; a name that is not valid Unicode (an unpaired surrogate, which UTF-8 cannot spell, so the game cannot ask for it) and a path longer than 254 bytes in UTF-8 - a hook hands the game `mods/` and the path, and its functions read 259 bytes of that - are left out and counted; at most 200,000 entries - the walk counts every name it hands on, one that folds like an earlier name too, and stops at the first past them, `Only part of the mods folder is used this session: the list stopped at 200000 files and folders.`, and it looks at 800,000 names at most, handed on or left out, so that a folder of names left out is bounded too and not by the disk alone, and stops at the first past those, `Only part of the mods folder is used this session: the list stopped at 800000 names, most of them too long or not valid Unicode.` - and 32 levels deep: a folder at that depth is listed, but what it holds is not read; it is counted and the reading goes on, `Only part of the mods folder is used this session: 1 folder deeper than 32 levels was skipped.`; a folder that cannot be listed - its full path past `MAX_PATH` too, in a process that is not long-path aware, as for the game's own functions (`Windows error 3, path not found`) - is logged, `The folder mods\locked could not be read (Windows error 5, access denied) - its files are not used.` (20,000 such lines at most), counted with the first one's error and reported, `Only part of the mods folder is used this session: N folders could not be read (Windows error 5, access denied).`, and the reading goes on; a listing that fails half-way - `FindNextFileW` with any error but `ERROR_NO_MORE_FILES`, a share that drops - is one of those too, its names so far kept, `The folder mods\x could not be read to its end (Windows error 64) - the rest of its files are not used.`, the mods folder's as well, unless no name of it was handed on, which makes it a mods folder that cannot be listed; a folder that holds nothing, not even `.` (`ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND`: a drive's root behind a junction), is empty, not unreadable), logged as `The mods folder has N files and folders.` and, when names were left out, `Names in the mods folder left out of the list: N whose name is not valid Unicode (an unpaired surrogate) - the game cannot ask for such a name; M whose path is longer than 254 bytes.` (each reason only with a count); a mods folder that cannot be listed at all hooks nothing, `Files in the mods folder are not used this session: the mods folder could not be read (Windows error 5, access denied).`; an empty list hooks nothing (`The mods folder is empty - the file functions are not hooked.`, or `Files in the mods folder are not used this session: none of the N names in the mods folder can be used (see the line above).` when names were left out, the one problem then, a walk that stopped not said beside it); otherwise the eight functions are hooked together through `HookFunctions` (`Files in the mods folder are used (8 file functions hooked).`; with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` one line per function, `DIAGNOSTIC: OpenBulk 1 hooked at RVA ...`; in a dry run `DRY_RUN: would hook the 8 file functions for the mods folder.` comes before the folder is read, and nothing is hooked).
   The hooks (`OpenBulkHook<Device>`, `PathHook<Device, Function>`): the path the game asks for is spelled the list's way on the stack (`ListSpelling`, one fold for the list and the lookups, as Windows compares file names: `/` for `\`, a doubled `/` inside the path collapsed, the letters in Windows' file-system upper case - on the bytes for a path of ASCII alone, and for any other through UTF-16, `MultiByteToWideChar(CP_UTF8, MB_ERR_INVALID_CHARS)`, `LCMapStringEx(LOCALE_NAME_INVARIANT, LCMAP_UPPERCASE)` - the file-system casing, without `LCMAP_LINGUISTIC_CASING` - and `WideCharToMultiByte(CP_UTF8)` back, in buffers on the stack sized for what that can make of 254 bytes, three bytes of UTF-8 for each UTF-16 unit (an upper case can take more bytes than its letter: U+0250's, U+2C6F, takes three to its two); upper case and not lower, because the file systems compare upcased names and the two differ - the Kelvin sign, U+212A, shares its lower case with `k`, not its upper case - and only the letters of the Basic Multilingual Plane, the runs between surrogates, because the file systems upcase a name unit by unit, by a table of that plane, so that a letter outside it, a surrogate pair, keeps its case, where `LCMapStringEx` would upcase it (a Deseret letter) - all of it checked against NTFS on my PC on 2026-10-27; a path that starts with a separator or a drive (`C:`) is never redirected, because only a relative path names a file of the game folder, nor is one longer than 254 bytes, which the game's functions would read cut short behind `mods/` - its length taken before the fold, since an upper case can take more bytes than its letter, so that a request of 255 bytes with U+2C6F folds to the spelling of a listed name of 253 bytes with U+0250 - nor one whose bytes are not valid UTF-8, which they would read with replacement characters) and looked up; a path the list has is handed to the game's own function as `mods/` plus the game's own spelling, otherwise the game's path is passed on untouched.
   What the game's function returns for the mods path is what the game gets: none of the four falls back to the game's own file, so the player's file is never replaced behind their back.
   When OpenBulk cannot open the mods file, the game gets that failure, as for any file the game's function cannot open, and each such call is logged, `OpenBulk 1 could not open mods/update/update.rpf - the game gets that failure, not its own file.` (20,000 such lines at most, then only counted: `20000 lines of mods files that could not be opened written - from now on they are only counted.`, ending `; the summary every minute has the numbers.` with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`); the first of a session is reported once (an atomic flag), `A file in the mods folder could not be opened - the game gets that failure, not its own file: mods/update/update.rpf.`, as a rule a problem after the start-up summary.
   OpenBulk's `served` line comes only once the mods file is open, and the game's last error is kept around both lines; the three path functions answer for the mods file.
   A path with `..` is never redirected, because the list never holds one.
   No file system call, no memory allocation and no lock on the game's file calls: a hook spells the path on the stack, hashes it, looks it up and on a hit copies the mods path onto the stack; `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` off costs one comparison more.
5. **OPEN archives** (`OPEN_RPF`): five call sites, `R-E-E1..E4` at the match and `R-E-E5` at +19, each must be readable and lead into the game (`Unencrypted (OPEN) RPF archives cannot be loaded this session: the DecryptHeader call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already).`), the two DecryptHeader2 calls to the same function (`... lead to different functions (... and ...)`), logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: the FindEncryption call at RVA ... -> RVA ...`; then all five are redirected in one `RedirectCalls` (`Unencrypted (OPEN) RPF archives can be loaded (5 calls redirected).`; `DRY_RUN: would redirect the 5 RPF encryption calls.` in a dry run).
   The hooks: `FindEncryptionHook(encryption)` replaces the game's function - it keeps the tag in a `thread_local` and answers whether the archive is NG-encrypted (`(encryption & 0x0FF00000) == 0x0FE00000`); `DecryptHeaderHook` and `DecryptHeader2Hook` skip the game's decryption when the tag, or the encryption argument, is `OPEN` (`0x4E45504F`); `ParseHeaderHook` calls the game's parser, then uses the tag up - it copies it and sets it to 0 on every exit, so that only a FindEncryption made for this archive counts (the game's ParseHeader makes that call before it can return true, PATTERNS.md "When Game Updates" item 5) - and, keeping the game's last error around its own lines, for an OPEN archive marks every named binary entry of the entry table (entries of 16 bytes: a type at +4 that is neither the folder type `0x7FFFFF00` nor has bit 31 set, a name offset at +0 above zero, an encryption field at +12 that is not zero) with `0x0FEFFFFF` and sets the packfile's current file offset (at +0xBC) to the same value, as the game expects for an unencrypted archive; the entry table is the pointer at +0x28 and its count the int32 at +0x30 (all three offsets counted from the `fiPackfile` object's start, as PATTERNS.md "When Game Updates" item 5 gives them), walked only for an OPEN archive and only when it is plausible - a table pointer and a count of 0..0x100000, otherwise `The OPEN archive <name> has an implausible entry table (N entries) - left as it is.` - and when its N x 16 bytes can be read (`IsReadable`; a count of 0 walks nothing), otherwise `The OPEN archive <name> has an entry table that cannot be read (N entries at 0x... (outside the game)) - left as it is.`: the table is the game's heap memory, and a field moved by a game update would point anywhere (20,000 such lines at most, the two reasons together, then only counted: `20000 lines of OPEN archives left as they are written - from now on they are only counted.`, ending `; the summary every minute has the numbers.` with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`), and the first such archive of a session is reported once (an atomic flag): `Unencrypted (OPEN) RPF archive <name> could not be prepared: its entry table is implausible (N entries) - its files may not load.`, or `... its entry table cannot be read (N entries at 0x... (outside the game)) - its files may not load.` The tag is per thread, so archives opened on two threads at once never mix.
6. **The diagnostic lines** (`DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, once at least one of the two jobs is hooked; announced by `DIAGNOSTIC: every file request, every file served from the mods folder and every archive header read are logged, 20000 lines per kind at most; a summary follows after 10 seconds, then every minute while the numbers change.`): every file request (`DIAGNOSTIC: OpenBulk 2 asked for update/update.rpf`), every file served (`DIAGNOSTIC: OpenBulk 2 served mods/update/update.rpf`) and every archive header read (`DIAGNOSTIC: archive update/update.rpf: 1790 entries, OPEN - read without decryption` or `..., encrypted`) get a line, 20,000 lines of each kind at most (`DIAGNOSTIC: 20000 lines of file requests written - from now on they are only counted; the summary every minute has the numbers.`), then only a count; the measurement (section 6.6; its thread, if it cannot start, is logged as `The RageOpenV summary thread could not be started (...).`) logs, 10 seconds in and then every minute while the numbers change, `DIAGNOSTIC: N file requests so far, M of them served from the mods folder; K archive headers read, J of them OPEN.`, with ` and F whose mods file could not be opened` after the files served and ` (U left as they are)` after the OPEN archives when there are any.
   A game path in a log line is read as UTF-8, as the game's functions read it (`MultiByteToWideChar(CP_UTF8)`, at most 259 bytes of it; bytes that are not UTF-8 become replacement characters, as they do for the game), and written as UTF-8 like every line, so that a name outside ASCII comes out right and `%USERPROFILE%` stands for the profile folder in it too.

**The check for packs made for GTA V Legacy** (`TweaksVLegacyPacks`, no key of its own).
A pack made for GTA V Legacy loads in Enhanced, but its models and textures keep Legacy's resource versions, and a vehicle made of them traps the game as it is created (PATTERNS.md, "RageOpenV").
At the end of step 4, once the eight hooks are on, `HookModsFolder` makes its one call, `StartLegacyPacksCheck()`, which runs once (an atomic) and starts the check's thread through `StartModuleThread` (`Legacy packs check`), so the game's thread never waits for it; it never throws into the start-up, and a thread that cannot start, a mods folder the check cannot list and an exception that ends the thread are each a problem: `The check for packs made for GTA V Legacy is not done this session: its thread could not be started (details in TweaksV.log).` (`... an unexpected exception ended it.`).
With `DRY_RUN`, and wherever the mods folder is not used - `MODS_FOLDER=0`, no mods folder, an empty one, a pattern missing - the call is never made.
The thread goes by the walk `ReadModsFolder` goes by (`WalkModsFolder`, `TweaksVModsFolder`), so that the two list the same files the same way and stop at the same name: the traversal, its bounds and its one root form are the walk's, and what each does with a name is its own, in a callback per name - RageOpenV's list, its counts and lines; the check's packs.
The root is `mods`, relative to the current directory, where the game's own file functions resolve `mods/...`, and the hooks serve it from; a folder's names come in the order Windows lists them, and of the folders it holds each listed is pushed and the last pushed read next.
32 levels deep - a folder at depth 32 is handed on, what it holds is not read - and 200,000 names at most: the walk counts every name it hands on, one that folds like an earlier name too, and the first past them ends it, not handed on (the last line then ends ` - the walk stopped at 200000 files and folders, where RageOpenV's list stops`); a name that is not valid Unicode, whose path below `mods\` is longer than 254 bytes in UTF-8 or that the lookups' fold refuses is left out, not counted against them; every name the walk looks at, left out or not, counts against a second bound, 800,000 names, four times the first, and the first past it ends the walk the same way (` - the walk stopped at 800000 names, most of them too long or not valid Unicode, where RageOpenV's list stops`); a folder that cannot be listed, or not to its end, is handed on as such and the walk goes on - RageOpenV reports it; the check skips it, but gives it a line and names it in its one problem, since its walk comes later, on a thread of its own, by a relative path, and can lose a folder RageOpenV's did not; the names that came before a listing failed half-way stand for both - but the mods folder itself, which RageOpenV has just listed, ends the check with a problem and without its last line when no name of it can be handed on, `The check for packs made for GTA V Legacy is not done this session: the mods folder could not be read (Windows error 5, access denied).` (one that holds nothing, not even `.`, is empty, for both).
Every file whose name ends in `.rpf`, in any case, is a pack, named in the lines by its path from the current directory (`mods\update\x64\dlcpacks\lspdcvpi08\dlc.rpf`), and a folder's packs are read in the order Windows lists them, once its find handle is closed.
A pack is opened by the path the walk gives it, relative, as the game's file functions open it, in the same process, so that Windows refuses the check what it refuses the game: in a process that is not long-path aware a folder whose full path passes `MAX_PATH` is listed by neither, and a pack whose full path passes it - a name the list holds - is named as not checked, `it could not be opened (Windows error 3, path not found)`, with no verdict on a pack the game cannot read.
It is opened with read access and every sharing, so that the game opens it as if the check were not there, and held only while its tables are read.
Its archives are read in the order they are found, the pack's own first, from a queue - no recursion - each from its header of 16 bytes: an archive is left with its reason - the pack keeps the first - when it is shorter than a header (`it is shorter than an archive's header (0 bytes)`), its magic is not RPF7 (`it is not an RPF7 archive (its first bytes are 50 4B 03 04)`), its entry count is 0 or above 1,048,576 (`it has no entries`, `it has 1048577 entries, more than 1048576`) or its header, entry table and names run past its end (`it has an entry table and names that run past its end (192 of 64 bytes)`); an archive whose encryption field is neither OPEN nor 0 is not OPEN - 0 names no encryption, so such a table is read as it is, and were it encrypted after all, its first entry would not be the root folder and the pack would be named as not checked - and a pack whose own archive is not OPEN is not read (`it is not OPEN (encryption 0x0FEFFFFF, NG) - only archives that are not encrypted are read, and the game's own archives are encrypted`, `AES` for 0x0FFFFFF9, nothing after the number for another value), and a nested archive that is not OPEN - by its header, or by the encryption of its bytes in the archive that holds it - is not read: the pack gets a line as not checked in full, `... in full: 2 archives nested in it are not OPEN - only archives that are not encrypted are read, and the game's own archives are encrypted.`, or, beside a damage of its own, `... in full: its nested archive packed.rpf is compressed; 1 archive nested in it is not OPEN.`, and is counted in the last line.
The entry tables and names a pack reads are bounded at 64 MB (`its entry tables and names pass 64 MB`, the pack left there) and the whole check's at 1 GB (`the check had read 1024 MB of entry tables and names before it`, for that pack and every pack after it, none of them read further); of the names only what a file's 16-bit name offset can reach is read, 65,792 bytes, so that no buffer is sized by a file's numbers unchecked - the table at most 16 MB, both buffers kept from archive to archive.
The first entry must be the root folder (`it has no folder as its first entry`); then every entry but a folder: its name must end in a zero within 256 bytes inside the names read (`it has a name outside its names (entry 1)`, the rest of that archive left); a resource whose name ends in `.yft`, `.ydr`, `.ydd` or `.ytd`, in any case, is counted by its version, and one with Legacy's - `yft` 162, `ydr` and `ydd` 165, `ytd` 13, where Enhanced's are 171, 159 and 5 - makes the pack one made for Legacy, the first such kept with the nested archive that holds it; a binary named `.rpf` is an archive nested in this one, queued when its bytes are not encrypted (else counted as not OPEN), it is stored (`its nested archive packed.rpf is compressed`), it lies inside this archive past its names (`its nested archive out.rpf lies outside the archive that holds it`) - so it is smaller than this one, and the nesting ends - it is at most 8 levels deep (`its nested archive l1.rpf/l2.rpf/.../l9.rpf is nested deeper than 8 levels`) and the pack has fewer than 4,096 archives (`it holds more than 4096 archives`, the pack left there).
A nested archive is named by the names from the pack down (`vehicles.rpf/lights.rpf`), every byte of a name from a pack outside printable ASCII written as `?`, so that a pack's names never break a line.
Each pack made for Legacy gets a line, `The pack mods\update\x64\dlcpacks\lp\dlc.rpf was made for GTA V Legacy: 2 yft 162, 1 ydr 165, 1 ydd 165, 1 ytd 13 - the first lp.yft in its nested archive vehicles.rpf.`, and each pack not read in full one with its reason, `The pack mods\x.rpf was not checked for GTA V Legacy versions: <reason>.` - `... versions in full: <reason>.` when part of it was read; A folder the walk could not list gets a line too, `The folder mods\locked could not be read (Windows error 5, access denied) - its packs were not checked for GTA V Legacy versions.` (`The folder mods\half could not be read to its end (Windows error 64) - the rest of its packs were not checked for GTA V Legacy versions.`).
20,000 lines of each kind at most (`20000 lines of packs made for GTA V Legacy written - from now on they are only counted.`, `... of packs not checked ...`, `... of folders not read ...`).
Then one problem names them, ten of each kind and `, and N more`: `A pack in the mods folder was made for GTA V Legacy, and a Legacy vehicle stops the game as it is created - convert it for Enhanced with CodeWalker, or take it out: mods\...\dlc.rpf.` (`N packs in the mods folder were made ..., convert them ..., or take them out: ...`), followed by ` Not checked for Legacy versions (TweaksV.log says why): ...` - or, with no pack made for Legacy, `A pack in the mods folder could not be checked for GTA V Legacy versions (TweaksV.log says why): ...` - the packs not read only for archives that are not OPEN, their own or nested ones, left out of it, since the game's own archives are encrypted and a pack made with OpenIV or CodeWalker is OPEN.
It names the folders not read as well, ten and `, and N more`: ` Folders not read, so their packs not checked (TweaksV.log says why): mods\locked.`, or, with nothing else to say, `A folder in the mods folder could not be read, so the packs in it were not all checked for GTA V Legacy versions (TweaksV.log says why): mods\locked.` (`N folders in the mods folder could not be read, so the packs in them were not all checked ...`).
The problem is a finding (section 6.4): a box of its own, whether or not the start-up summary has been shown, as `ERROR_POPUP` allows; that the check is not done is a problem of a part failing.
The last line counts: `Packs checked for GTA V Legacy versions: 13 in the mods folder, 1 made for Legacy, 12 not checked (2 of them not OPEN), 1 folder could not be read.` (`none made for Legacy`; `none in the mods folder`; `none in the folders read` when a folder could not be read).
With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` each pack read gets a line, `DIAGNOSTIC: the pack mods\lpnest.rpf: 2 archives (1 nested), 2 nested not OPEN, 7 entries; 1 yft 162, 1 ytd 5.` (`no yft, ydr, ydd or ytd`; 20,000 at most, `DIAGNOSTIC: 20000 lines of packs read written - from now on they are only counted.`), and before the last line `DIAGNOSTIC: the check read 0.3 MB of entry tables and names from 52 archives in 0.12 s.` The check writes nothing, to the game or to a pack.
The RPF7 facts it reads by - the magic, the header's fields, the encryption tags, an entry's size and fields, the folder's mark, the resource bit, the block - are RageOpenV's own, one set in `TweaksVRpf.h` that both files include, so the two never disagree.
In a test build `TWEAKSV_TEST_LEGACY_FOLDER=<folder>` in the environment has the check read that folder in place of the mods folder, said once, `TEST BUILD: the check for packs made for GTA V Legacy reads <folder> in place of the mods folder (TWEAKSV_TEST_LEGACY_FOLDER).`, so that a suite reaches a folder the check cannot list, which RageOpenV's own reading of the mods folder stops at first, `TWEAKSV_TEST_MODS_ENTRIES=<n>` lowers the walk's limit for both and `TWEAKSV_TEST_MODS_FAIL_LISTING=<folder>` fails a listing half-way for both (section 13.1).

### 11.7 StraightToStoryMode (`InitStraightToStoryMode`)

Three screens, three small patches, one switch each, each failing alone (`The <screen> is not skipped this session: <why>`): the splash screen's `jz rel32` (`0F 84`) 0x0D before `S-E1`'s match becomes a `jmp rel32` to the same target computed from the game's own displacement (one byte shorter, so the displacement, counted from the end of the instruction, grows by one) plus a NOP; the legal screen's 6-byte `jz` 0x0F before `S-E2`'s match becomes six NOPs; the function the call at the start of `S-E3`'s match leads to gets `31 C0 C3` (`xor eax, eax; ret`), so the landing page's choice returns 0, Story Mode, at once.
A site before a match is read only after `IsReadable` (`the game code before the pattern at RVA ... cannot be read.`); the splash and legal sites must hold the game's `jz` (`the game code found is not the expected instruction (<bytes>)`), and the splash jump must still reach its target one byte shorter (`the jump found reaches too far for the patch.`); the landing page's site must hold a call (`the game code found is not a call (<5 bytes>).`) that leads into the game (`the call found does not lead into the game code (another mod may have redirected it).`) to a function that does not start with a jump (`another mod has already hooked the function it patches.`); a write that fails says `the game code could not be made writable (details in TweaksV.log).`; a screen already patched - the splash site holding `E9` and a NOP, the legal site six NOPs, the function `31 C0 C3` - is skipped quietly (`Splash screen already patched - skipped.`).
With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` each site found is logged with the bytes it holds, before they are checked: `DIAGNOSTIC: splash screen jz at RVA ...: <6 bytes>`, `DIAGNOSTIC: legal screen jz at RVA ...: <6 bytes>`, `DIAGNOSTIC: landing page function at RVA ...: <3 bytes>`.
Each patch is one `WriteCode`, `what` = the screen, then `<Screen> patched: <old> -> <new>.`

### 11.8 DirectStorage (`InitDirectStorage`)

With DirectStorage, the game asks it to open every file in the game folder at start-up, subfolders included (`IDStorageFactory::OpenFile`), and DirectStorage opens each with `CreateFileW` for reading while letting others only read it (`NtCreateFile` with access 0x80100080, share `R--`, options 0x48) and keeps it open, so mods cannot write their own files there and scripts cannot be reloaded while the game runs.
The part uses no game pattern, and its job needs no DirectStorage: it hooks `NtCreateFile` in `ntdll.dll` and decides every open by its own name, access and sharing, and with `UNLOCK_FILES` `NtSetInformationFile` as well, deciding every rename that replaces a file by its target, whoever makes them.
With `UNLOCK_FILES=0` and `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=0` it does nothing (`UNLOCK_FILES=0 and DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=0 - nothing to do.`).
It logs the versions of `dstorage.dll` and `dstoragecore.dll` in the game folder and whether `dstorage.dll` is loaded (`dstorage.dll <version>, dstoragecore.dll <version>; dstorage.dll is loaded.`, a version being four numbers, `not found` or `unknown version`), as information.
With `CONFLICT_CHECK=0`, an installed `DirectStorageFix.asi` is noted (`Note: <path> is installed as well - it changes DirectStorage's file opens, too.`).
With `DRY_RUN` one "would" line per hook of a job that is on and nothing else: `DRY_RUN: would hook NtCreateFile so that the game folder's other files are opened with write and delete sharing (UNLOCK_FILES).` and `DRY_RUN: would hook NtSetInformationFile so that a rename inside the game that replaces a file in the game folder gets POSIX semantics (UNLOCK_FILES).`, and with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` `DRY_RUN: would hook dstorage.dll to log the game's DirectStorage calls.` and `DRY_RUN: would hook NtCreateFile to log the opens in the game folder.`

- `NtCreateFile` is hooked at the start-up (`HookExport`) whenever `UNLOCK_FILES` or `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` is on, one hook for both jobs, `dstorage.dll` loaded or not: `UNLOCK_FILES: NtCreateFile is hooked - a read-only open of a file in the game folder other than .rpf and .cache that shares reading gets write and delete sharing too, so those files stay free for other programs.` A hook that cannot be made is a problem of the start-up summary with `UNLOCK_FILES` (`The files in the game folder are not unlocked this session: NtCreateFile could not be hooked (details in TweaksV.log).`), and `HookExport`'s line alone with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` alone.
- With `UNLOCK_FILES`, `CheckOpen` decides before the call, on the stack - no memory allocation, no lock of TweaksV's, nothing that can throw - every field of the call read within the length it gives, under a guard that catches a bad pointer; an open made on a thread that is inside one of TweaksV's hooks (`t_inside`) is not examined.
  Where the open lies: a name `\??\C:\...` (`\??\UNC\server\share\...` for a game folder on a network share) is compared with the game folder, letters in ASCII case - below it, the game folder itself, or outside, a sibling folder whose name starts with the game folder's and every other spelling (a device, `\Device\...`) among the outside; a name relative to a folder handle, which is what Windows makes of every relative path that lies in the current directory, lies below the game folder when that handle is the game folder's, told by its identity - `FileIdInformation`'s volume serial number and 128-bit file ID, on a file system without them `FileInternalInformation`'s 64-bit file number with `FileFsVolumeInformation`'s serial number, queries that need no access to the handle (the current directory's is opened for traversing only, which `GetFileInformationByHandle` is refused) and leave the caller's last error alone; the game folder's is read once before the hook is made, the handle's asked of Windows once per such open, twice on a file system without file IDs.
  An open relative to another folder, an open by file ID (`FILE_OPEN_BY_FILE_ID`) and a name that cannot be read cannot be examined.
  An open in the game folder is then, in this order: a directory (`FILE_DIRECTORY_FILE`, or the game folder itself; a folder opened without the flag cannot be told from a file before the call), an archive (the last name's extension - from its last `.` that is not its first letter, up to a `:` that starts a stream, so that `x.rpf::$DATA` is the archive - `.rpf` or `.cache` in any case), a write open (asking for `GENERIC_WRITE`, `GENERIC_ALL`, `MAXIMUM_ALLOWED`, `DELETE`, `FILE_WRITE_DATA`, `FILE_APPEND_DATA`, `FILE_WRITE_EA`, `FILE_WRITE_ATTRIBUTES`, `WRITE_DAC` or `WRITE_OWNER`), an exclusive open (its sharing leaves out `FILE_SHARE_READ`), and otherwise an open that gets `FILE_SHARE_WRITE` and `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` added before the call goes on to Windows.
  Every other open goes on untouched.
  Windows decides sharing per handle: a new open succeeds only if its access fits the sharing of every handle open on the file and its own sharing allows every such handle's access, so a program that opens a widened file for writing while sharing nothing still gets a sharing violation while the game holds it - the first limit README.md states.
  The second is Windows' rule for a rename: a file with an open handle, whatever that handle's sharing, cannot be replaced by a classic rename (`FileRenameInformation` with ReplaceIfExists, what `MoveFileEx` makes of `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING`), which fails with access denied, while a rename with POSIX semantics (`FileRenameInformationEx` with `FILE_RENAME_POSIX_SEMANTICS`), `ReplaceFile`, a delete followed by a new file and a write in place go through - for a program outside the game's process; inside it, the `NtSetInformationFile` hook below gives a classic replace-rename POSIX semantics.
  A game folder whose identity cannot be read is a problem, since no relative open - nor a rename's name relative to a folder handle - can then be told to lie in it: `Files in the game folder opened by a relative path are not unlocked this session: the game folder's file ID could not be read (<error>).`
- Every examined open is counted after the call in exactly one of six counts, with atomics, whatever its result: widened, archives, write opens, exclusive opens, directories, not examinable; an open outside the game folder is not counted; every replace-rename into the game folder is counted in exactly one of three counts, whatever its result: given POSIX semantics, made again as the caller made it, passed on with a name too long.
  A widened open's file is told apart by a hash of its path below the game folder (FNV-1a over its letters, ASCII in lower case, up to a stream) in a fixed table of 16,384 slots that the threads fill without a lock or an allocation, 8,192 files at most; the type of each new file, its extension with the dot in lower case (`(none)` without one), is counted in a fixed table of 15 types in the order they come, a type past the 15th or one that does not fit 8 bytes counting as `others`.
  The summary is a measurement (section 6.6) whose thread starts with the hooks and waits for an event they set at the first open or rename they count, so that it comes 10 seconds after it, then every minute while the sum of the counts changes, each count read once: `UNLOCK_FILES: in its first 10 seconds, N opens of M files in the game folder other than .rpf and .cache were given write and delete sharing, so those files stay free for other programs; by type: .txt 4, .asi 1, (none) 1, others 1; left as they were: A opens of archives, B write opens, C exclusive opens, D directories, E whose path could not be examined; replace-renames into the game folder: R given POSIX semantics, F made again as the caller made them where the file system refused that, P passed on as made, their names longer than 1024 characters.` - `so far` in place of `in its first 10 seconds` later, `more than M files` once the table of files is full, no types while there are none.
  Without `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` no line per open is written.
- With `UNLOCK_FILES`, `NtSetInformationFile` is hooked at the start-up too (`HookExport`), right after `NtCreateFile` and also when that hook could not be made: `UNLOCK_FILES: NtSetInformationFile is hooked - a rename inside the game that replaces a file in the game folder gets POSIX semantics, so it goes through while the game holds that file open.` A hook that cannot be made is a problem of the start-up summary: `The replace-renames into the game folder are not given POSIX semantics this session: NtSetInformationFile could not be hooked (details in TweaksV.log).` With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` alone it is not made.
  Windows refuses the classic rename of a new file over one that somebody holds open, whatever the sharing of the handles on it, with access denied; with POSIX semantics the rename goes through as long as every handle on that file shares deleting, which `CheckOpen` gives the game's opens - the holders go on reading the file they opened, and the name is the new file's.
  The hook passes every information class but the two renames, `FileRenameInformation` (10) and `FileRenameInformationEx` (65), on at once, the class compared and nothing read, and so it passes every call made on a thread inside one of TweaksV's hooks (`t_inside`).
  For a rename, `CheckRename` decides before the call, on the stack - no memory allocation, no lock of TweaksV's, nothing that can throw, the caller's last error kept: under the guard against a bad pointer, a buffer of at least 24 bytes, `FILE_RENAME_INFORMATION`'s size, gives its flags - `FileRenameInformation`'s ReplaceIfExists their first byte - the folder handle and the name's length, and a name that is not empty, of even length and within the buffer is copied into a `FILE_RENAME_INFORMATION` on the stack when it has 1024 characters at most; a buffer Windows refuses itself goes on as made.
  A replace-rename - ReplaceIfExists set, or `FILE_RENAME_REPLACE_IF_EXISTS` without `FILE_RENAME_POSIX_SEMANTICS` - is examined on the copy, so that what is examined is what goes to Windows; every other rename goes on as made.
  Its target lies in the game folder when its name is relative to a folder handle that is the game folder's - told by its identity as for an open, one query, two on a file system without file IDs - and does not start with `\`; when its full name lies below the game folder, compared as an open's; and when it is a bare name - no folder handle, no leading `\`, a rename inside the source's own folder for Windows - whose source lies below the game folder: the source's object name (`NtQueryObject`, `\Device\HarddiskVolume3\...`), one query into a stack buffer of 1024 characters, compared with the game folder's object name read the same way at the start-up, letters in ASCII case - not its final path (`GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`), which would allocate, take locks and ask the mount manager inside the hook, so a source opened by a short 8.3 path goes on as made.
  A bare name with a `\`, which Windows refuses, a name or a source with a `:` below the game folder - a stream, no file in it - and every target elsewhere go on as made.
  A replace-rename into the game folder goes to Windows as `FileRenameInformationEx` with `FILE_RENAME_REPLACE_IF_EXISTS`, `FILE_RENAME_POSIX_SEMANTICS` and the caller's other flags (`FILE_RENAME_IGNORE_READONLY_ATTRIBUTE`, say), from the copy, its length 20 bytes and the name's, 24 at least; one whose name has more than 1024 characters is passed on as made.
  Where Windows answers the rename turned with `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, `STATUS_INVALID_INFO_CLASS`, `STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER`, `STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST` or `STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED` - a file system that does not take the class or the flag, a FAT drive, a network share, a Windows older than 10 1607 - the call is made again as the caller made it and its result returned; every other answer, a failure too, is returned as Windows gave it.
  The test build stands in for such a file system with `TWEAKSV_TEST_NO_RENAME_EX=1` (section 13.1).
  The game folder's object name is read once before the hook is made, with `NtQueryObject` on the folder; one that cannot be read is a problem, since no rename by a bare name can then be told to lie in it: `Replace-renames in the game folder by a bare name are not given POSIX semantics this session: the game folder's path could not be read (<error>).` With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, after the call (`AfterCall`), each rename turned is a line: `rename #N: <target below the game folder> | <result> | <asked>, <form> -> FileRenameInformationEx, flags 0x... | from <callers>` - the result `ok` or the status, or where the file system refused POSIX semantics `refused (<status>), made again as made: <result>`; asked `FileRenameInformation with ReplaceIfExists` or `FileRenameInformationEx, flags 0x...`; the form `a full name`, `a name relative to the game folder` or `a bare name`, whose target is the source's folder and the name; the callers as an open line's - 20,000 lines at most (`DIAGNOSTIC: 20000 lines of renames turned written - from now on they are only counted.`), the renames counted all the same.
  Programs outside the game are not touched: their classic replace over a file the game holds still gets access denied, the limit README.md states.
- With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, `DStorageGetFactory`, `DStorageSetConfiguration` and `DStorageSetConfiguration1` are hooked in a loaded `dstorage.dll`, one hook for the two configuration functions (`DIAGNOSTIC: the game's DirectStorage calls are logged (N of 3 functions hooked).`; not loaded, `dstorage.dll is not loaded, so the game does not use DirectStorage here: there are no DirectStorage calls to log.`), and each call into DirectStorage is logged with its caller and its values: `the game asked for the DirectStorage factory (call #N, interface {...}) -> ok, from ...`, with the factory's first eight functions (`DIAGNOSTIC: the factory's first 8 functions: [0] dstorage.dll+0x... ... [7] ...`, or `DIAGNOSTIC: the factory's function table could not be read.`), and `the game set the DirectStorage configuration (DStorageSetConfiguration) -> ok, from ...` with `DIAGNOSTIC: DStorageSetConfiguration values: ...` (7 values, 8 for `DStorageSetConfiguration1`).
  When the game gets its factory for the interface `{6924EA0C-C3CD-4826-B10A-F64F4ED927C1}`, the hook reads the factory's function table (safely: a bad pointer is caught, never a crash) and hooks its function [4], `OpenFile`, which must lie in `dstorage.dll` or `dstoragecore.dll`, before the game gets the factory back, so that it sees the first `OpenFile` already (`DIAGNOSTIC: the game's OpenFile calls are logged (OpenFile at dstoragecore.dll+0x... hooked).`), tried once under a lock a second caller waits on; what keeps it from being made is a line, `DIAGNOSTIC: OpenFile is not logged: <why>.` - `the factory's function table could not be read`, `the factory's OpenFile is not in DirectStorage's DLLs (<address>)`, `OpenFile (<address>) could not be hooked (details in TweaksV.log)`, `OpenFile could not be hooked (unexpected exception)`, the last made before the attempt so that logging it needs no new text.
  Another interface, whose functions TweaksV does not know, asked for once the hook was tried is a line, once a session: `The game also asked for another DirectStorage interface, {...}, whose functions TweaksV does not know - left as it is.` Asked for before, it starts (once) a check, a thread of its own, which 10 seconds later writes that line if the hook has been tried by then - the known interface asked for in the meantime - and otherwise `DIAGNOSTIC: OpenFile is not logged: the game asked only for another DirectStorage interface, {...}, whose functions TweaksV does not know.` A thread started with the factory's hook writes, if the game has not called `DStorageGetFactory` 30 seconds after TweaksV started, `DIAGNOSTIC: the game has not asked for its DirectStorage factory since TweaksV started.` Each of these threads, if it cannot start, is logged (`StartModuleThread`); nothing of this is reported, since nothing depends on the factory (NOTES.md, T-DIAG).
- `HookOpenFile` passes every call on as it is and logs it, its own work done on the stack: the path is copied safely (at most 2047 characters), `/` made `\` in the copy only, and made full in a second buffer - a `\\?\UNC\server\share` path, the prefix in any case, as the network path `\\server\share`, any other `\\?\` path without the prefix and as it is, as Windows takes it, and every other path through `GetFullPathNameW`, which makes it absolute from the current directory, as DirectStorage does, and collapses `.` and `..` - and every call is counted in exactly one of four counts, with atomics: an archive in the game folder (decided as above), another file in the game folder, a path outside it, a path that cannot be read, is longer, or cannot be made full.
  Ten seconds after the first call a line from a thread of its own (one that cannot start is logged) gives the four counts, each read once and the total their sum: `OpenFile: in its first 10 seconds the game asked DirectStorage for N files: A .rpf and .cache files, B other files in the game folder and C outside it[, U with an unreadable path] - all of them passed on to DirectStorage.` Every call gets a line (`OpenFile #N: <name> -> ok | 0x...`, the first with its callers), 20,000 at most, then the calls are only counted (`DIAGNOSTIC: 20000 lines of OpenFile calls written - from now on they are only counted.`).
  A line names a path inside the game folder below it (`update\update.rpf`) and any other by a category and its file name only - `(outside the game folder) SGTA50000`, `(temp) cloud_tweaksv.dat` for one in `%TEMP%` (`GetTempPathW` and its long form, read at the start), `(network) cloud.dat` for a `\\server\share` path - so that no other folder, such as a profile's, reaches the log; an unreadable path is `(unreadable path)`.
  The name is made only for a line that is written.
- With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` the `NtCreateFile` hook also logs, after the call, every open inside the game folder (`DIAGNOSTIC: every open of a file or folder in <game folder> is logged, 2 lines per file, caller and sharing; a summary follows every minute.`): `open #N: <relative path> | <status> | access 0x..., share R-- widened to RWD, open, options 0x... | from <callers>` - the sharing asked for and, where `UNLOCK_FILES` widened it, the sharing given; a name relative to a folder handle made full through `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`; the callers being the first three frames outside TweaksV and the system DLLs - at most 2 lines per file, caller and sharing given, 20,000 lines in all (`DIAGNOSTIC: 20000 open lines written - from now on the opens are only counted (see the summaries).`), and counts it in four tables - the ways of opening (file, caller and sharing given), the files, the callers and the types - of 100,000 entries each at most: an open a full table cannot take is only counted, said once (`DIAGNOSTIC: a table of the open summary is full (100000 entries) - the opens it cannot take are only counted (see the summaries).`).
  The measurement (section 6.6; its thread, if it cannot start, is logged as `The DirectStorage open summary thread could not be started (...).`) writes a minute in and then every minute while the count changes `summary: N opens of P files and folders in the game folder (S not shown above[, T not tracked: 100000 entries per table at most]); W file opens kept others from writing the file | by caller: ... | by type: ...` (at most 15 types, then `others N`), W by the sharing given.
- Every hook does its own work after the game's call through one helper, `AfterCall`: not on a thread that is inside a hook already (`t_inside`, so that nothing TweaksV opens there itself is examined or logged), with the game's `GetLastError` kept and every exception caught.
  `CheckOpen` and `CheckRename`, before `NtCreateFile`'s and `NtSetInformationFile`'s calls, are the pieces that run first, and they keep the caller's last error too.

### 11.9 Blips (`InitBlips`)

The game's script `blip_controller` makes the vanilla blips behind a guard: a loop that calls the native that counts the active blips, compares the count with a threshold of its own, 150 on 1.0.1158.16, and while the count is at or above it waits 2000 ms and counts again (PATTERNS.md, "Blips").
The part raises that threshold to `ACTIVE_BLIP_LIMIT` - 150..1997, 1997 being the count's lowest answer with the pool full, so that a higher limit could let the script make a blip into a full pool - in the script's code each time the game places the script, at the start and again at every save load, a new program object each time, as a user of the script programs' hook (section 13.3).
`InitBlips` reads the limit and `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` once, then calls `Scripts::AddUser({ "Blips", before, after }, reason)`: a hook that cannot be made is the part's problem with the module's reason (`blip_controller's threshold is not raised this session: Y-E-H1 was not found.`); a hook made is said, `Each time the game places blip_controller, its guard's threshold is set to 1900 (ACTIVE_BLIP_LIMIT; the game's own: 150).`; in a dry run the call is not redirected and the part sees no program, `DRY_RUN: the natives' resolution call is not redirected, so blip_controller is not looked at - its guard is found and checked on a boot without DRY_RUN.`

`before`, at every placement, on the game's resource placement thread: a program object that cannot be read (`IsReadable`, 0x80 bytes) is logged once and left; one whose hash at +0x58 is not `blip_controller`'s (0x80CFE974, `Scripts::NameHash`) costs that read and nothing more; one with the hash and another name, read with `Scripts::ReadName` within 64 bytes, is logged once (`A script program has the hash of blip_controller, 0x80CFE974, but is named blip_imposter - left as it is (logged once).`).
For `blip_controller` itself, its placements counted from 1: its code length 1 byte..16 MB and its native count 1..65536 (the module's bounds), its native table readable, its code's page table and every page readable (`PageLength`); then the whole code is decoded from 0 with the opcode lengths of the game's own table, constants in the part (`kLengthRanges`, checked when TweaksV is compiled to cover 0x00..0x82 once, in order), ENTER 5 bytes and its name's (the byte at +4), SWITCH 2 and 6 per entry (the byte at +1).
A byte above 0x82, or an instruction that runs past the code or past its page - SWITCH's count within its page, its entries within the page of ip + 2 - refuses the program (`its code cannot be decoded: the byte 0xA0 at 0x200 is no instruction`), so the bytes inside operands, names and SWITCH tables are never taken for a match.
The decode marks every instruction's start in a bitmap, a bit per byte of code, allocated per placement inside a `try` (`there was not enough memory to read its code`), and notes the first CATCH, THROW or CALLINDIRECT.

The guard is tried at every instruction start that is a NATIVE with no argument and one result, in a window of 256 bytes from it (fewer at the code's end), every instruction of the guard on an instruction start in the window and ending in it: the count's NATIVE; any NOPs; the threshold's push, PUSH_CONST_U8 or PUSH_CONST_S16, 1..1997; IGE or ILT, maybe NOT, then JZ - or the fused IGE_JZ or ILT_JZ in their place; the branch's target an instruction start in the window.
JZ jumps when what it takes is 0 and a fused jump when its comparison is false, so the branch leaves the guard either while the count is below the threshold - the game's ILT NOT JZ, and IGE JZ or IGE_JZ - or while it is not - ILT JZ, IGE NOT JZ, ILT_JZ: the way out and the body are the branch's target and the instruction after it, in that order or the other.
From each, NOPs and jumps (J) are followed within the window, a circle or a step off an instruction start failing the match: the body is a push of 2000 (PUSH_CONST_S16, PUSH_CONST_U24 or PUSH_CONST_U32) and a NATIVE with one argument and no result, WAIT, after which NOPs and jumps lead back to the count; the way out may not lead back into the guard.
The two natives' indices (big-endian, at +2 of their NATIVE) lie below the native count and differ.
A sequence with the guard's head but not the rest is a miss with its reason, the first of which the problem names when no guard is found (`its guard was not found in its code - the count and a comparison with a threshold are at 0x3FE, but its WAIT calls native 3, which is not below its native count, 3`).
Refused, the program left as the game placed it: no guard (`its guard was not found in its code`); a second one (`its guard was found twice in its code, at 0x3FE and 0x800 - which one is meant cannot be told`); a CATCH, THROW or CALLINDIRECT anywhere in the code, whose target is computed while the script runs and could lead into the guard (`its code holds a CATCH, THROW or CALLINDIRECT at 0x..., whose target is computed while the script runs and could lead into its guard at 0x3FE of its code`); a jump (J, JZ and the fused jumps: ip + 3 + their signed 16-bit offset), a CALL (its unsigned 24-bit address) or a SWITCH case (its unsigned 16-bit offset from the entry's end), as the interpreter reads them from anywhere that leads into the bytes from the end of the count's NATIVE through the branch (`a jump at 0x100 leads into its guard at 0x3FE of its code, between 0x402 and 0x408`).

The threshold found: the configured limit already - left, `blip_controller's guard at 0x3FE of its code holds the threshold 1900 already (ACTIVE_BLIP_LIMIT=1900) - nothing to patch (placement 1).`; one other than the game's 150 - left as it is and logged, not a problem, as the heap adjuster leaves a heap another adjuster set (`blip_controller's threshold is not raised (placement 1): its guard at 0x3FE of its code holds 240, not the game's own 150 - left as it is.`); the game's 150 - the rewrite is prepared: the bytes from the end of the count's NATIVE to the branch become NOPs, the new threshold's push right-aligned against the branch - PUSH_CONST_U8 up to 255, PUSH_CONST_S16 above - and the comparison that keeps the polarity, IGE where the branch leaves the guard while the count is below the threshold and ILT where it leaves it otherwise; a fused branch's opcode becomes JZ, its offset and so its target unchanged; nothing after the branch moves - `43 6C 07 0B` for 1900, `00 25 C8 0B` for 200, in the place of the game's `25 96 0C 06`.
Too little room - the push and the comparison longer than the bytes before the branch, or a push that would run past the end of a page - refuses it (`its guard at 0x3FE of its code has 3 bytes before its branch, and the threshold 1900 needs 4`).
What `before` found goes to `after` in a record per thread, `thread_local`, without an allocation - the module calls a user's `before` and `after` in turn on one thread - with the two natives' hashes from the table before the resolution.

`after`, once the game has resolved the natives: for a rewrite, the program is read again - its code length, native count and page table, the pointers of the window's pages, and the window's bytes against those `before` read - and the window decoded again from the count and matched again, which must give the same guard, or nothing is written (`its guard at 0x3FE of its code changed between the game's placement and the write (another mod may be at work) - left as it is`); then one `WriteCode`, a piece per page the bytes lie in, `what` = `threshold in blip_controller's guard at 0x3FE of its code (placement 1)` (a write that fails: `its code could not be changed (details in TweaksV.log)`), and the line `blip_controller's guard at 0x3FE of its code: the threshold 150 -> 1900 (placement 1).` With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, for every guard found, one line with both natives - index, hash and handler, the table's entry after the resolution: `DIAGNOSTIC: blip_controller's count native index 10, hash 0xC802478C8862BDAD, handler RVA 0x1D51F40; WAIT index 47, hash 0x4EDE34FBADD967A6, handler RVA 0x920EA0.` Every refusal is `blip_controller's threshold is not raised (placement N): <why>.`, a problem the first time a session for each kind of failure - after the start-up summary in a box of its own (section 6.4) - and afterwards a line with the count, `... - 2 times this session, reported once.`, since a save load places the same program again.
Both functions run inside the module's `try`, and their own work that can throw inside one more.

The measurement, `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` only: `L-E-P1` gives the blip pool, 2000 slots of 8 bytes (the rel32 of the allocator's `lea rbx, [rip+disp32]`, at +10), and `L-E-P2` its high-water mark, a dword (the disp32 of `cmp [rip+disp32], esi`, at +5) - the two instructions' opcode bytes checked against the patterns when TweaksV is compiled - both inside the game, the pool's last byte too.
A pattern not found, an address outside the game or a thread that cannot start is logged (`DIAGNOSTIC: the blip pool is not measured: L-E-P1 was not found.`), not reported: an aid (NOTES.md, T-DIAG).
Announced by `DIAGNOSTIC: the blip pool at RVA 0x3DEBD70 (L-E-P1), 2000 slots, and its high-water mark at RVA 0x3DEFBF0 (L-E-P2): the slots in use are logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while the numbers change.`, the measurement (section 6.6, thread `blip pool summary`) reads the mark and, after `IsReadable` over the pool, the slots below it in one pass, 8 bytes at a time, without a lock - nothing is written or called - and logs `DIAGNOSTIC: blips: 162 of the pool's 2000 slots in use, the high-water mark at 170; blip_controller makes blips up to 1900 (ACTIVE_BLIP_LIMIT).`, its end saying what the guard held after the last placement: another threshold left as it is, `blip_controller's threshold was not raised at its last placement (see above)`, `blip_controller has not been placed yet`, `blip_controller's threshold is not raised this session (see above)` when the hook could not be made, `DRY_RUN: blip_controller's threshold is not raised` in a dry run.
The old plugin is rootBrz's `BlipLimitFix.asi` (section 4.3).

### 11.10 WeaponLimits (`InitWeaponInfos`, `InitWeaponComponents`)

Two halves under one tag, each a module of its own with its own key under `[WEAPON_LIMITS]` and its own guard: the weapon infos' (`WEAPON_INFO_FILES`, the `weapons.meta` files) and the weapon components' (`WEAPON_COMPONENTS_SIZE`, the weapon component definitions of all `weaponcomponents.meta` files together).
The part's entry of the feature table starts them in turn, each inside its own `try` there: an exception ends only that half, what it has written or hooked by then staying as it is, and is reported under the part's tag with the half's name - `The weapon infos' half is not done this session: an unexpected exception ended it.`, `The weapon components' half is not done this session: an unexpected exception ended it.` - and the other half still starts.
Each guard is a MinHook hook on the game's loader of one file - of a `weapons.meta` file, of a `weaponcomponents.meta` file - that refuses a file once its half's room is used up, before the game reads it, and reports it as a finding in a box of its own (section 6.4), a job of its own beside its half's raise; in a test build with `TWEAKSV_TEST_GUARD_ROOM=1` it guards the game's own room instead, which the half reads from the game's code (`GuardRoom`, section 13.1).

#### 11.10.1 Weapon infos (`InitWeaponInfos`)

The game parses each `weapons.meta` file - each data file of the type 0x4E - into one weapon info blob of 248 bytes and keeps the blobs in one block on its heap, held by the weapon info manager's array in its data: the block's pointer at +0, the count at +8 and the capacity at +0xA, both 16-bit (PATTERNS.md, "Weapon infos").
A C++ static initializer of the game sets the array up once with two immediates - `mov edx, 160` hands the number to the allocation, which allocates and constructs that many blobs, and `mov word [capacity], 160` gives the room, 160 on 1.0.1158.16 - and the game's loader adds a blob per file without a check, so that a file past the capacity is parsed into the memory after the block.
