`InitWeaponInfos`, under the part's tag `WeaponLimits`, does two jobs from `DllMain`, each whole or not at all and each left undone alone: the raise of the two immediates to `WEAPON_INFO_FILES` before the initializer runs, and the guard, a hook on the loader that refuses a file past the room; with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` it measures, phase A's measurement kept as the part's own.
`WEAPON_INFO_FILES` (160..16384, 1024 by default) is the room: 248 bytes of the game's heap a file, 254 KB at 1024; the count's type would allow 65,535.

First the sites both jobs build on, checked as phase A's measurement checked them and each logged with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`: `W-E-I1`, the initializer's allocation, and `W-E-I2`, the loader's head, both looked for through `Scanner::FindOnce`, so that the log names each one missing and a pattern the game holds twice is said to be; the imm32 of `mov edx` at +11 and the imm16 of the capacity's store at +34 equal - the exit destructor destroys as many blobs as the capacity says - and not 0 (`DIAGNOSTIC: limit at RVA 0x1897A81 holds 160 (W-E-I1): the blobs the game allocates`, `DIAGNOSTIC: limit at RVA 0x1897A98 holds 160 (W-E-I1): the array's capacity`); the call at +15 into the game, its target readable and beginning with the allocation's 14 bytes `56 57 48 83 EC 28 89 D6 48 63 FA 48 69 CF`, which take the number from `edx` alone, the element size the imm32 after them (`DIAGNOSTIC: allocation call at RVA 0x1897A85 -> RVA 0x1893AC0 (W-E-I1): 56 57 48 83 EC 28 89 D6 48 63 FA 48 69 CF F8 00 00 00`); the block's store, whose disp32 at +23 gives the array, inside the game with its 12 bytes, and the capacity's store, whose disp32 at +30 counts from the instruction's end, after the imm16, and must lead to the array + 0xA (`DIAGNOSTIC: reference at RVA 0x1897A8D -> RVA 0x47CE480 (W-E-I1): the block's store - the weapon info blob array`, `DIAGNOSTIC: reference at RVA 0x1897A94 -> RVA 0x47CE48A (W-E-I1): the capacity's store`); `W-E-I2`'s count read at +3 and written at +13, both at the array + 8, and its block read at +27, at the array (`DIAGNOSTIC: reference at RVA 0x1863B5B -> RVA 0x47CE488 (W-E-I2): the loader's read of the count`, `... (W-E-I2): the loader's write of the count`, `... (W-E-I2): the loader's read of the block`), its blob size at +20 the allocation's element size and not 0 (`DIAGNOSTIC: blob size at RVA 0x1863B6C holds 0xF8 (W-E-I2)`).
The first that fails ends both jobs with one problem of the start-up summary: `The weapon info blob limit is neither raised nor guarded this session: W-E-I1 was not found.`, or with the reason `the game code holds W-E-I1 2 or more times, not the expected 1.`, `the two limits of the weapon info blob array disagree: 160 at RVA ... and 161 at RVA ... (W-E-I1; another mod may have changed one).`, `the limits of the weapon info blob array hold 0 (W-E-I1).`, `the allocation call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already) (W-E-I1).`, `the allocation at RVA ... does not begin with the bytes it is expected to (W-E-I1).` (`cannot be read` where it cannot), `the weapon info blob array at 0x... (outside the game) (W-E-I1) is not inside the game.`, `the capacity's store at RVA ... leads to RVA ..., not to the array's capacity at RVA ... (W-E-I1).`, `the loader's read of the count at RVA ... leads to RVA ..., not to RVA ... (W-E-I2).` (its write of the count and its read of the block alike), `the loader's blob size, 0x100 at RVA ..., is not the allocation's element size, 0xF8 (W-E-I2).` and `the blob size of the loader and the allocation is 0 (W-E-I2).`.
Then the guard's own check, before anything is written: the loader's start, the match - 0x18, readable game code and a function start - where the game's unwind data covers the match, the function it names must begin there (`DIAGNOSTIC: the loader at RVA 0x1863B40 (W-E-I2 - 0x18): a function start (unwind data)`), so that a shorter prologue after a game update cannot put the hook into the padding between functions; code without unwind data, the fake game's, is taken by the byte before it, as `IsFunctionStart` takes it (section 9).

The raise: `Scanner::FindRipRelativeReferences(array, 3)` exactly 2 - the block's store, which gave the array and so is always among them, and the initializer's `lea` of the array, each logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: reference at RVA 0x1897A21 -> RVA 0x47CE480 (scan)` - so that a second store of a block would show; then the array, read once after `IsReadable`, still empty - no block and capacity 0, the game's data as it was loaded - which proves on every boot that the initializer runs after TweaksV's `DllMain`, and so with the immediates the part writes (`DIAGNOSTIC: the weapon info blob array at RVA 0x47CE480 is still empty at TweaksV's start (no block, 0 loaded, capacity 0): its initializer runs after TweaksV's DllMain, so its limit can be raised in time.`).
The room is `WEAPON_INFO_FILES`; immediates that hold as much or more already - another mod's raise, or a later game build's own - are left as they are and logged, not reported: `The weapon info blob limit is 2048 already (WEAPON_INFO_FILES=1024) - nothing to patch.` Otherwise both go in one `WriteCode`, the imm32 and the imm16 to the room (`what` = `weapon info blob array size (160 -> 1024)` and `weapon info blob array capacity (160 -> 1024)`; with `DRY_RUN` its would lines alone), then `The weapon info blob limit is raised from 160 to 1024 weapons.meta files.` A raise not done is a problem of the start-up summary, and the guard goes on: `The weapon info blob limit is not raised this session: the game code holds 3 or more references to the weapon info blob array, not the expected 2 (another mod may have changed one, or the game has changed).`, `... the weapon info blob array at RVA ... cannot be read.`, `... the weapon info blob array at RVA 0x47CE480 was set up before TweaksV started (a block, 152 loaded, capacity 160): its initializer ran before TweaksV's DllMain.` and `... the game code could not be made writable (details in TweaksV.log).`

The guard keeps the count below its room: the room the raise gives the array - the game's own when the raise was not done or not needed - as `GuardRoom` hands it back, in a test build with `TWEAKSV_TEST_GUARD_ROOM=1` the game's own (section 13.1; `TEST BUILD: the weapon infos guard's room is the game's own 160 instead of 1024 (TWEAKSV_TEST_GUARD_ROOM=1).`).
A start that begins with a jump already is logged and hooked on top (`The weapon info loader at RVA ... already starts with a jump - another mod may have hooked it; the guard goes on top and chains through.`); with `DRY_RUN` nothing is hooked: `DRY_RUN: would hook the weapon info loader at RVA 0x1863B40 so that a weapons.meta file past the room, 1024, is refused.` Otherwise an event for the report is made, the loader's start hooked (`HookFunctions`, section 9) and the report's thread started (`StartMeasurement`, thread `weapon infos guard report`, which waits for the event), all or nothing - a thread that cannot start switches the hook off again, and the hook refuses nothing until the thread runs - and `The weapon info blob limit is guarded: the weapon info loader at RVA 0x1863B40 is hooked, and a weapons.meta file past the room, 1024, is refused instead of being written past the array's end.` The hook, on the game's loading thread, reads the count and the capacity - 16 bits each, aligned, in the game's data - and the imm32 of `mov edx`, and refuses a file once the count has reached the room, the capacity or the blobs the initializer allocated by that imm32, whichever is least, so that an array another mod gave less room, or fewer blobs than its capacity, after TweaksV still holds: it returns 0 without calling the game's loader, counts the refusal (an atomic), and the first time keeps the file's name - copied with `CopyMemorySafely` from the data file entry the loader got in `rcx`, at most 127 bytes, printable ASCII and `?` for any other byte, `(no name)` for an empty one and `(a name that cannot be read)` where it cannot - and the room it met, logs it at once - `The weapons.meta file dlc_wi01:/common/data/ai/weapons.meta is refused: the game's room for weapons.meta files, 1024, is full - the files refused are reported 10 seconds after this first one.`, so that a game that dies before the report keeps the line - and sets the event; below the room it calls the game's loader, last, with its arguments as they came (`rcx` to `r9`), and hands its result back.
Neither path changes the game's last error.
Ten seconds after the first refusal the report's thread reports it once, a finding in a box of its own (section 6.4): `The weapons.meta file dlc_wi01:/common/data/ai/weapons.meta and 13 more were not loaded: the game's room for weapons.meta files, 1024, is full - raise WEAPON_INFO_FILES in TweaksV.ini above 1024 (at most 16384).` - `was not loaded` for one file; `, the most WEAPON_INFO_FILES allows.` at 16,384; ` - the test build's room (TWEAKSV_TEST_GUARD_ROOM=1).` in a test build alone, where `GuardRoom` says the switch gave the room, since raising the key changes nothing then - the shipped file names no test switch; `: its limit was not raised this session (see above).` when the raise was not done, also under the switch; ` - the array has less room than the 1024 TweaksV gave it (another mod may have changed its limit after TweaksV).` when the capacity the file met was below the guard's room - and every minute while more come, a line: `9 weapons.meta files in all were not loaded this session: the game's room for weapons.meta files, 160, is full.` A guard not set up is a problem of the start-up summary, the raise standing: `The weapon info blob limit is not guarded this session: the loader at RVA ... (W-E-I2 - 0x18) is not a function start: W-E-I2 lies in the function at RVA ... (unwind data).`, `... the loader at RVA ... (W-E-I2 - 0x18) is not a function start (no unwind data, byte before it 48).`, `... the loader at RVA ... (W-E-I2 - 0x18) is not readable game code.`, `... no event for its report could be made (Windows error ...).`, `... the weapon info loader at RVA ... could not be hooked (details in TweaksV.log).` and `... the thread that reports the refused files could not be started (details in TweaksV.log).` With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` the hook writes a line per file before the game's loader runs, so that the file that passed a limit shows even when the game dies on it: `DIAGNOSTIC: weapons.meta file 153: dlc_wi01:/common/data/ai/weapons.meta`, with ` (past the game's own 160)` after a number past it and ` refused (the room, 160, is full)` after a file refused - 20,000 such lines at most, then `DIAGNOSTIC: 20000 lines of weapons.meta files written - from now on they are only counted; the summary every minute has the numbers.`

With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` the measurement follows, phase A's kept as the part's own: announced by `DIAGNOSTIC: the weapon info blob array at RVA 0x47CE480 (W-E-I1), blobs of 248 bytes: the weapons.meta files loaded and its room are logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while the numbers change.`, its thread `weapon infos summary` (section 6.6) reads the count and the capacity back - after `IsReadable`, 16 bits each, aligned, without a lock - and the refusals, 10 seconds in and then every minute while any changes (the capacity changes once, when the initializer sets the array up), and logs `DIAGNOSTIC: 152 weapons.meta files loaded (the game's own array holds 160, this one 1024).`: the game's own the number `W-E-I1`'s immediates held before the raise, this one the capacity read back - the proof that the initializer ran with the number the part wrote.
Its end: ` - the game's own array would have overflowed by 6 of them` once the count passes the game's own number within the array's room; then ` - the guard's room, 1024, is full: 6 files were refused` once the guard refused files, ` - the guard's room, 1024, is full: the next file is refused` at its room, ` - it is full: the next file would be written past its end` at the capacity without the guard (`DRY_RUN`, or a guard not set up), ` - past its end: the game's memory is already damaged` past it; `, and the array is not set up yet` in the place of `, this one 1024` while the capacity reads 0; and `DIAGNOSTIC: the weapon info blob array at RVA ... cannot be read.` is the whole line when it cannot be read.
A dry run measures the game's own array; nothing is hooked, so it has no lines per file.

#### 11.10.2 Weapon components (`InitWeaponComponents`)

The game keeps every weapon component definition of all `weaponcomponents.meta` files in one sorted list, a static array of 470 pointers with its count, an int32, right after it - the list's size is the distance between them, and no instruction carries it - and rebuilds it after every such file without a bound; every definition also takes a slot of a pool, 470 unless the game's configuration names another size, whose trap ends the game when it is full (PATTERNS.md, "Weapon components").
`WEAPON_COMPONENTS_SIZE`, 470..16384, default 1024, is the room the part gives: the list moved on the core to one of 16854 entries - the largest room and 470 more - the pool given the room and 470 more, the most one file brings, and the guard refusing the files that come once the room is used up, so that the last file let in always fits and the pool's trap always comes before the list could be overrun.
The 470 definitions one file may hold are the game's own and stay.

On the core: spec `{ "WeaponLimits", "weapon component list", "weapon component list size", "The weapon components limit is not raised", 8 bytes, 16854 entries, WEAPON_COMPONENTS_SIZE, 3 anchors, 17 scanned references, "48 4C" }`; the anchors, in this order: `W-E-C1` a reference - the rebuild's `lea r10`, its rel32 at +34 - and a follower - its `mov dword [rip+disp32], 0`, the disp32 at +2 and the instruction ending at +10: the count, whose distance from the list is the game's own size, 470 - then `W-E-C2` a reference, the sort's `lea rcx` at +14, and `W-E-C3` a site the part reads.
The core finds the 17 loads by the list's address, and its line on the key names the key's own range, whose maximum stops 470 short of the moved list's entries (`TweaksV.ini: WEAPON_COMPONENTS_SIZE=500 is outside 600..16384 - using 600.`).
The part's own checks after `FindRelocation`: the room and 470 more within the moved list, so that the pool can never pass the list, whatever size the core takes from the game - the key's maximum fits, checked when TweaksV is compiled, and a game's own list past it does not (`the game's own weapon component list holds 16500 entries: a room of 16500 and the 470 of the last file let in would pass the moved list's 16854.`); the sort's read of the count (`W-E-C2` +3) and the update's read and write (`W-E-C3` +3 and +12) must lead to the count the core found (`the references to the weapon component list's count do not all point to one count: RVA ... and RVA ... (W-E-C3).`), an int32 inside the game that can be read (`the weapon component list's count at RVA ... cannot be read.`); the pool's size call, `W-E-C4` +18, comes from `FindPoolSizeCall`, which looks for `W-E-C4` (`W-E-C4 was not found.`, `the game code holds W-E-C4 2 or more times, not the expected 1.`) and checks that the call leads into the game (`the weapon components' pool size call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already) (W-E-C4).`), and the pool's default at +14 must be 1 to 16,854, the moved list's entries (`the pool's default size at RVA ... holds 0, which is no plausible size (W-E-C4).`); with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` each is logged (`DIAGNOSTIC: count reference at RVA ... -> RVA ... (W-E-C2)`, `DIAGNOSTIC: entry size at RVA ... holds 8 (W-E-C2)`, `DIAGNOSTIC: files' count reference at RVA ... -> RVA ... (W-E-C1)`, `DIAGNOSTIC: pool size call at RVA 0x1855FD8 -> RVA 0x149060 for the pool 0xBAE33CCF, its default at RVA 0x1855FD4 holds 470, the game's configuration at RVA 0x2E96FB0 (W-E-C4)`).
Every opcode the part reads and the entry size 8 are bytes of their patterns, and so are `W-E-C4`'s opcodes and the pool's name hash, checked when TweaksV is compiled (`PatternByte`, section 4.2).
`ApplyRelocation` then takes the size call as a redirect, in the same all-or-nothing write as the 17 references, to `PoolSize(configuration, hash, defaultSize)`: it calls the game's lookup with the game's arguments, so that the configuration is read as it would be, and returns the room and 470 more exactly - also when the configuration names more, since a larger pool would let definitions past the list - and the first call logs `The pool size the game's configuration gives for the weapon components, 470, is replaced by 1494.` (nothing may throw there: the line is in a `try`, and the last error the lookup left is kept around it), a later one - should the game make the pool anew - nothing.
The status lines: the core's, `The weapon component list moved and its limit raised from 470 to 1024 entries.`, and `The pool size call at RVA ... is redirected: the weapon components' pool gets 1494 slots, the room of 1024 and 470 for the last file let in.`; the dry-run line `DRY_RUN: would move the weapon component list (17 references) and raise its limit from 470 to 1024 and redirect the pool size call at RVA ...` names no sites, since no limit is written.

The measurement, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`: nothing is hooked; announced by `DIAGNOSTIC: the weapon component definitions in the list and the weaponcomponents.meta files are logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while the numbers change, and the list's 17 loads are checked at each reading to lead to the moved list still.` - with `DRY_RUN` `... while the numbers change (DRY_RUN: the game's own list is in use).` - the measurement (section 6.6, thread `weapon components summary`) reads the list's count and the files' count - `W-E-C1` +14, a uint16 inside the game, or the measurement does not start: `DIAGNOSTIC: the weapon components are not measured: the count of weaponcomponents.meta files is not inside the game: ... (W-E-C1).` - each after `IsReadable`, without a lock, 10 seconds in and then every minute while they change, and logs `DIAGNOSTIC: 412 weapon component definitions from 23 weaponcomponents.meta files (the game's own list holds 470, this one 1024).` - ` and is in use)` in the place of `, this one 1024)` with `DRY_RUN` - with ` - the game's own list would have overflowed by 86` once the count passes 470, at the room ` - the room is used up: the guard refuses every further file` (` - the room is used up, and no guard refuses further files: past 1494 the game crashes while it loads` without the guard), at the room and 470 ` - the pool is full: one more definition crashes the game while it loads`, in the game's own list with `DRY_RUN` ` - it is full: one more definition crashes the game while it loads` at 470; a count outside 0..470 in the game's own list is its overflow (`DIAGNOSTIC: the weapon component list's count reads -1547607936, from 25 weaponcomponents.meta files - outside the game's own list of 470: the list has overflowed, a definition past its end written over the count and the memory after it, and the game is likely to crash.`), one outside 0..16854 in the moved list damaged data (`... - outside the moved list of 16854: the game's data is damaged, and the game is likely to crash.`), and counts that cannot be read `DIAGNOSTIC: weapon components: the list's count at ... or the files' count at ... cannot be read.` At every reading the 17 loads are read back, each after `IsReadable`: one that no longer leads to the moved list - 6 of them lie in the game's second, protected `.text`, which might put its bytes back - is reported once, a problem after the start-up summary in a box of its own (section 6.4), `The weapon components limit is not kept this session: the load of the weapon component list at RVA ... leads to the game's own list at RVA ... again, not to the moved list at ... - the game has put it back, and a search through it reads the game's own list, empty since the move: the game is likely to crash.` - a load that leads anywhere else `... leads to RVA ..., not to the moved list at ... - the game or another mod has changed it, and a search through it no longer reads the moved list: the game is likely to crash.` - one that cannot be read any more `The weapon components limit may not be kept this session: the load of the weapon component list at RVA ... cannot be read any more, so whether it still leads to the moved list at ... cannot be told.` - and the summary written then ends ` - a load of the list has changed, reported above.`

The guard (D12; the loader research's findings, by D-a to D-e of 2026-10-30): `W-E-C5` finds the loader of one `weaponcomponents.meta` (`W-E-C5 was not found.`, `the game code holds W-E-C5 2 or more times, not the expected 1.`); its head, the match - 0x13, must be a function start (`IsFunctionStart`) not starting with a jump (`the loader at RVA ... starts with a jump (another mod may have hooked it already).`), the blob array's count `W-E-C5` reads at +0x26 the one `W-E-C1` reads at +14, its capacity (+0x2D) 2 bytes after it and its pointer (+0x3E) 8 bytes before it, inside the game, and the blob's size at +0x07 and +0x15, 0xED8, is pinned by the pattern; then the head is hooked with `HookFunctions` (MinHook, its trampoline writable and executable: T-HARDEN), and `The weapon components' loader at RVA ... is hooked: a weaponcomponents.meta file that comes once 1024 definitions are loaded is refused.` - with `DRY_RUN` `DRY_RUN: would hook the weapon components' loader at RVA ... to refuse the files that come once 1024 definitions are loaded.`.
A guard not made is a problem of its own, `The weapon components' guard is not made this session: <why>`, and leaves the move and the pool.
At each file the hook reads the file's name from its entry - the type at +0x90 0x4F first, then the bytes up to the first NUL within 0x90, any byte outside printable ASCII shown as `?`; a file of another type, or with no name, is `a weaponcomponents.meta whose name cannot be read` in the lines, refused by the sum alone with no name kept, and `not named` in the summary - and the definitions already loaded, the sum of the blobs' counts: the count at most the capacity, the pointers and each blob's int32 at +0xEC0 copied with `CopyMemorySafely`, each 0..470.
A file is refused when the sum has reached the room - `WEAPON_COMPONENTS_SIZE`, the game's own 470 with `TWEAKSV_TEST_GUARD_ROOM=1` in a test build (`GuardRoom`, section 13.1) - or when a file of its name was refused before in the session (its 64-bit FNV-1a hash kept, 1,024 at most; past them `The weapon components' guard keeps 1024 refused names at most: from now on a file is refused by the definitions loaded alone.`, once), so that a base loader's run, which queues every file before any is parsed, refuses again exactly the files refused before.
A refused file never reaches the game's loader: its callback, passed by value, is destroyed as the loader's end would - its callable through its vtable's +0x20, `dl` true when the callable lies outside the argument, the vtable and the destroy inside the game - and nothing else needs undoing.
Each refusal is a line, `Weapon components file <name>: 1024 definitions loaded, the room 1024 - refused.` (`refused, as before.` by its name), with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` each file let in too, `DIAGNOSTIC: weapon components file <name>: ... - let in.`, 20,000 lines at most; the first refusal is a finding in a box of its own (section 6.4), `Weapon component files are refused this session: <name>, the first, came once N definitions were loaded, the room being R - the weapon components of the files refused are missing in the game. Raise WEAPON_COMPONENTS_SIZE in TweaksV.ini (at most 16384); the log names every file refused.` - where raising the key changes nothing, `, the most WEAPON_COMPONENTS_SIZE allows` at 16,384, or `, the test build's (TWEAKSV_TEST_GUARD_ROOM=1)` in a test build alone, where `GuardRoom` says the switch gave the room, after R, and `The log names every file refused.` for the advice - and the summary adds ` - F files refused, the first <name>`.
A read that does not add up lets the file through and switches the guard off for the session, a problem in the box: `The weapon components' guard is off this session: <why> - <name> and the files after it go to the game unchecked, and past 470 definitions above the room the game crashes while it loads.`, with the reason `the blob array at RVA ... cannot be read`, `the blob array holds 23 blobs, past its capacity of 10`, `the blob array's pointers at ... cannot be read`, `blob 5 at ... cannot be read` or `blob 0 holds 500 definitions, outside 0..470`.
Definitions already loaded at a file - the sum above 0, so the pool exists - while the game has never asked the redirected size call mean that it made the pool without the redirect: it made the pool before TweaksV started, or asks for its size another way.
That is one problem in the part's box, once, and the guard goes on with its room: `The weapon components' pool size is not raised this session: 412 definitions are loaded, and the game has not asked the redirected call at RVA ... for the pool's size - it made the pool before TweaksV started, or asks for its size another way, and the pool keeps the game's size: past it the game crashes while it loads, whatever WEAPON_COMPONENTS_SIZE says.` The hook runs on the thread that applies the data file changes, lets nothing throw out of it and keeps the game's last error, which its reads and lines may change and which a refused file finds as the game left it; what it decides stands whatever its lines and problems throw, each in a `try` of its own, so that a name kept as refused is always a file refused, and a read that does not add up switches the guard off first.

### 11.11 PedProps (`InitPedProps`)

The part is experimental and ships off: its code is all there and the suites run it switched on, on the fake game, but the raise has never been tried in the game past 255 props on an anchor.
Off, it is as quiet as any part that is off; only its measurement goes on, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` alone (below).

`TweaksVPedProps` lifts the props per anchor of a ped model (PATTERNS.md, "PedProps").
A ped model's props hang on 13 anchors and come in variation files, its own and one per clothing pack; the game counts an anchor's props over all of a model's files in 32 bits, but narrows that total to 8 bits in the check the set-prop native asks (`A-E-T1`) and in the helmet code (`A-E-T2..T5`), and a worn prop's index to 8 bits before the metadata lookup (`A-E-G1`, twice, and `A-E-G3..G6`), so that from 256 props on one anchor in all the game refuses the props from (total mod 256) on.
The part makes each of the eleven `movzx r32, r/m8` (`0F B6`) a `movzx r32, r/m16` (`0F B7`) - the same length and the same registers, each narrowed register holding the whole value - so that an anchor holds 65535 props in all, and below 256 the widened reads give the game's own values.
`[PEDPROPS]` holds `ENABLED` alone, off by default: the width is fixed, there is no size to choose.
Left as they are: the random props' builder (the research's G7), whose list keeps an index in a byte by its own format, and the 41 narrowings of one file's count, a file holding 255 props on an anchor at most by its own data.
No old plugin: the original is Legacy only, and nothing on Enhanced does the part's job.

`InitPedProps()` checks everything first, and the first check that fails refuses the whole job with nothing written, reported as `The ped props' limit is not raised this session: <why>`.
First the ten patterns of the narrowings, each as often as it must match - `A-E-G1` exactly 2, the others exactly 1 - and `A-E-C1`, the per-anchor count's start, exactly 1, each through `Scanner::FindAll` asked for one match more (`FindAll(pattern, 3)` for `A-E-G1`, `FindAll(pattern, 2)` for the others), so that a match more is named so, not as missing; every one is looked for, so that the log names each one missing, and the first problem in the table's order is the reason: `A-E-T1 was not found.`, `the game code holds A-E-T1 2 or more times, not the expected 1.`, `the game code holds A-E-G1 3 or more times, not the expected 2.` (`1 time` for one).
A narrowing another mod has widened no longer matches its pattern, so for the first pattern the game holds too seldom the part looks for its widened twin - the pattern's text with that `B6` made `B7`, `<label> (widened)` in the scanner's lines - and when it is there the reason names the site: `the narrowing at RVA ... holds 0F B7 C0, not 0F B6 C0 (another mod may have widened it already) (A-E-T3).` Then the narrowings, from the table `kNarrowings` - per site its pattern, how often it matches, the `movzx`'s offset and length in the match (3 bytes, 4 with a REX prefix), what it narrows and the call in the match - which `NarrowingsArePinned()` holds against the patterns when TweaksV is compiled: `0F B6` at the offset, which the pattern pins, so that a match is the instruction the part widens, and `B6` in the pattern's text where the twin puts `B7`; a REX prefix where the instruction is 4 bytes long; a ModRM of mod 11 whose register, without a REX prefix, is never AH..BH, whose 16-bit form would be another register; `E8` at the call; eleven sites in all.
With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` each narrowing is logged with its bytes - `DIAGNOSTIC: narrowing at RVA 0x10F4E63 holds 0F B6 C3 (an anchor's total, A-E-T1)`, `... holds 44 0F B6 C6 (a worn prop's index, A-E-G1)` - and its call after it, read through `IsReadable`, since a call's rel32 can lie past the match: `DIAGNOSTIC: texture count call at RVA ... -> RVA ... (A-E-T1)`, `per-anchor count call` after `A-E-T2..T5`, `metadata lookup call` after the six.
Every call must lead into the game (`the texture count call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already) (A-E-T1).`), `A-E-T2..T5`'s to the start `A-E-C1` matches (`the per-anchor count call at RVA ... leads to RVA ..., not to the per-anchor count at RVA ... (A-E-C1) (A-E-T3).`), and the six all to one function, the metadata lookup (`the metadata lookup calls do not lead to the same function: RVA ... and RVA ... (A-E-G4).`).
Then the complete sets: `Scanner::FindCallsTo` (section 8) finds every call of the per-anchor count, up to 8, and of the metadata lookup, up to 18, each logged with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` as `DIAGNOSTIC: per-anchor count call at RVA ... -> RVA ... (call scan)`; there must be exactly 7 and 17 (`the game code calls the per-anchor count at RVA ... in 8 or more places, not the expected 7 (another mod may have redirected some of them, or the game has changed).`), so that a caller a game update adds, which would narrow again, refuses the job.

The write is one `WriteCode` of the eleven, each the whole `movzx` with its `B6` made `B7`, `what` being `narrowing of an anchor's total (A-E-T1)` or `narrowing of a worn prop's index (A-E-G1)` - with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, `DIAGNOSTIC: narrowing of an anchor's total (A-E-T1) at RVA 0x10F4E63: 0F B6 C3 -> 0F B7 C3` for each - and then `The ped props' limit is raised from 255 to 65535 per anchor (11 sites).`; a write that fails: `the game code could not be made writable (details in TweaksV.log).` With `DRY_RUN` the part checks everything and writes one line in the place of the eleven writes, as the relocation core does: `DRY_RUN: would widen the 11 narrowings of the ped props from 8 to 16 bits and raise their limit from 255 to 65535 per anchor.`

The measurement, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` only, follows the job whether it was done, refused or dry, and runs with the part off too, from the table of the parts measured first (`MeasurePedPropsWhileOff`, section 4.3), which looks for `A-E-C1` in the job's place and does nothing with the part on: an aid that needs only its own sites, inside a `try` of its own (`DIAGNOSTIC: unexpected exception - the ped props' measurement is skipped for this session.`), whose failures are logged, not reported (NOTES.md, T-DIAG), as `DIAGNOSTIC: the ped props are not measured: <why>`.
Its own patterns, `A-E-C2` and `A-E-S1`, are both looked for, each through `Scanner::FindAll(pattern, 2)`, so that the log names each one missing, and the first of `A-E-C1`, `A-E-C2` and `A-E-S1` the game does not hold exactly once is the reason (`A-E-S1 was not found.`, `the game code holds A-E-C2 2 or more times, not the expected 1.`).
The layout it reads is pinned by `A-E-C1` and `A-E-C2`, each offset a constant checked against its pattern's bytes when TweaksV is compiled - a model info's variation collection at +0x240, the collection's count of files at +8 and their pointers at +0 (`A-E-C1`); a file's anchors at +0x58, their count at +0x60, entries of 0x18 bytes, an entry's props at +8 (`A-E-C2`) - and logged, `DIAGNOSTIC: per-anchor count at RVA ...: a model info's variation collection at +0x240, its count of variation files at +0x8 (A-E-C1)` and `DIAGNOSTIC: its loop at RVA ...: a variation file's anchors at +0x58, their count at +0x60, entries of 0x18 bytes, an entry's props at +0x8 (A-E-C2)`.
`A-E-S1` holds the model info store's four globals, the disp32 at +2, +15, +39 and +59 - its count of model indices (a dword), the pointers to its bitmap (a bit per index) and to its array (an element per index, the model info's pointer its first 8 bytes), and its stride (a qword) - each inside the game and readable (`the model info store's array's pointer at 0x... (outside the game) (A-E-S1) is not inside the game.`), and a model info's type at +0x9D, 6 for a ped in its low 5 bits: `DIAGNOSTIC: model info store at RVA ...: its count at RVA ..., its bitmap's pointer at RVA ..., its array's pointer at RVA ..., its stride at RVA ...; a model info's type in the low 5 bits of +0x9D, 6 for a ped (A-E-S1)`.
The measurement is announced, `DIAGNOSTIC: the model info store at RVA ... (A-E-S1): the props per anchor of its ped models are logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while they change.`, and started through `StartMeasurement`, its thread `ped props summary` (section 6.6).

A pass, the measurement's `numbers`, walks the store without a lock, the game adding variation files while it loads its packs: the four globals read as they are; every model index below the count whose bit is set; the model info its element's first 8 bytes point to, its type byte; for a ped, its hash at +0x18 and its variation collection (none: a ped model without variation data); every file of the collection and every entry of its anchors, an entry's anchor at +0x10 (both offsets without a pattern) and its props, a word taken whole - in each file only an anchor's first entry, as the game's per-anchor count takes it.
Every read of the heap goes through `CopyMemorySafely` (section 9), within bounds: the count 0..1,000,000, the stride 8..4096 and a multiple of 8, a collection's files and a file's anchors at most their words' 65,535, and at most 4,000,000 files and anchor entries read in one pass, so that data the pass misreads cannot keep the thread busy; an entry whose anchor is not 0..12 is counted apart.
A bound broken or memory that cannot be read ends the pass with its line in the summary's place, `DIAGNOSTIC: the ped props are not counted: the model info store's count, 2000000, is not plausible.` (`... the model info store's stride, 12, is not plausible.`, `... a variation file of mp_f_freemode_01 at 0x... (outside the game) cannot be read.`), which a later pass can take back.
Per ped model and anchor the pass sums the props, the files that bring them and the most in one file; the number that decides whether a summary is written is an FNV-1a hash of everything the summary says.

The summary: `DIAGNOSTIC: ped props: 43 ped models with variation data in 99 files (the model info store's 60010 indices, its stride 16); the most props on one anchor: 212 (mp_m_freemode_01, anchor 0, from 30 files, at most 38 in one); no anchor and no file past 255.` - the two freemode models by name, their hashes checked when TweaksV is compiled against `Scripts::NameHash` of their names (0x705E61F2 and 0x9C9EFFD8), every other model by its hash, `model 0x2C000000`; `DIAGNOSTIC: ped props: no ped model with variation data yet (...)` before the game has loaded its models; `; 1 anchor entry names no anchor of 0..12` before its end where such entries were counted, the first one named once a session, `DIAGNOSTIC: ped props: an anchor entry of mp_m_freemode_01 names anchor 13, not one of 0..12 - such entries are counted apart (logged once).`; and `; past 255: 2 anchors and 1 file, each in a line of its own.` as its end where there are any, those lines after it in the walk's order.
An anchor's line says what the game makes of it this session: with the eleven widened, `DIAGNOSTIC: ped props: mp_m_freemode_01, anchor 0: 302 props from 45 files - past the game's own limit of 255, raised to 65535 this session.`, past 65535 `... model 0x2D000000, anchor 0: 65790 props from 258 files - past the limit of 65535 it is raised to this session: the game's own checks refuse its props from index 254 on.`, the total mod 65536; without them - a dry run, a job refused, the part off - `... - past the game's own limit of 255: the game's own checks refuse its props from index 46 on.`, the total mod 256; and for a file past its own bound `DIAGNOSTIC: ped props: mp_f_freemode_01, anchor 2: its file 29 of 29 holds 270 props - a file holds 255 at most, its props past 255 stay unusable with or without TweaksV.` A pass keeps 20,000 of them, and their lines stop at `kMaxLinesPerKind` a session, with `DIAGNOSTIC: 20000 lines of ped props past 255 written - from now on they are only counted; the summary every minute has the numbers.`

### 11.12 Resources (`InitTextureBudget`)

The game counts the video memory of the textures and buffers its streaming engine places in one object of its data, the counters (PATTERNS.md, "Resources"): the buffers' and the textures' bytes and numbers, the most bytes of both so far - the game's own high-water mark - the most objects allowed and four budgets, the last two written once at the start-up from `gameconfig.xml` (`MaxStreamingResources`, 98,304, and `MaxStreamingMemory_0..3_Base`, 1,500, 1,500, 3,000 and 3,000 MB, in the game's own file); an index the texture quality gives picks the budget in use.
A resource is placed only while the objects stay below the most and the bytes below the budget - otherwise the streaming engine evicts what it cannot place, and textures go missing however much memory the card has - and an update check evicts ahead of time past a factor of the budget.
The part, one module, `TweaksVTextureBudget`, raises the budgets under the tag `Resources`: `[RESOURCES] VIDEO_MEMORY_BUDGET`, in MB, 6000 by default, says to what, and `STREAMED_OBJECTS` the most objects (below).
Windows' own budget for the game's video memory is not read: the key's maximum, 16,384, is a documented cap in place of a query of it, since the update check evicts against Windows' budget anyway.

`InitTextureBudget()` reads the key once.
0 keeps the game's own budgets and is said in one line, `VIDEO_MEMORY_BUDGET=0 - the texture budget is not raised.`; with `STREAMED_OBJECTS` at 0 too and no `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` nothing more is done, nothing looked for.
A value from 1 to 1,499, below the lowest budget the game's own file gives, is raised to 1,500 and reported in the settings' wording, `TweaksV.ini: [RESOURCES] VIDEO_MEMORY_BUDGET=1000 is outside 0 or 1500..16384 - using 1500.`, the settings table taking 0 to 16,384.
Otherwise - and with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, the key at 0 too - it looks for `Q-E-T1` to `Q-E-T4`, and `Q-E-T5` with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, all of them, so that the scanner names each one missing, each search going on to a second match, so that a pattern the game holds twice is named as what it is, `the game code holds Q-E-T1 2 or more times, not the expected 1`, and never as not found, and then checks the sites the raise and the measurement share, in this order, the first that fails ending both, the raise with a problem, `PROBLEM: The texture budget is not raised this session: Q-E-T1 was not found.`, the measurement with a line, `DIAGNOSTIC: the texture budget is not measured: Q-E-T1 was not found.`, each with that reason: the most objects `Q-E-T1` stores inside the game; past its match, read after `IsReadable`, the shift and the store of budgets 0 and 1 - the 9 bytes `Q-E-T2` holds at +9, checked against that pattern when TweaksV is compiled (`the code after Q-E-T1 at RVA ... is not the store of budgets 0 and 1: <bytes>`) - and the budgets inside the game; `Q-E-T2` right after it, at +34 (`the store of budgets 2 and 3 at RVA ... (Q-E-T2) does not follow the store of budgets 0 and 1, which ends at RVA ...`), its store 16 bytes after budgets 0 and 1; the budgets' writers, by the scanner's search for stores of any width into their 32 bytes (section 8), which must find the start-up's two (`the scan for the budgets' writers does not find the start-up's store at RVA ... (Q-E-T1)`); the call after the stores at +40 of `Q-E-T2`, its rel32 past the match read after `IsReadable`, a call into the game (`the call after the budgets' stores at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already) (Q-E-T2)`) - the call the raise redirects; the index `Q-E-T3` loads inside the game; and the counters `Q-E-T4` loads inside the game, all 0x48 bytes, each of its five loads the field of the counters the research names (`the placement's check loads the textures' bytes from RVA ..., not from +0x8 of the counters at RVA ... (Q-E-T4)`), its most the one `Q-E-T1` stores and the budgets at the counters' +0x28.
An address inside the game that is not aligned to the size of its reads - the most objects and the index to 4 bytes, the budgets and the counters to 8 - is refused too, with its own ending in the place of `not inside the game` (`the index at RVA ... (Q-E-T3) is not aligned to 4 bytes`), since a read that straddles a cache line could tear.
The opcode bytes the module reads in each match are checked against the patterns when TweaksV is compiled.

A writer of the budgets besides the start-up's two stores refuses the raise alone - it could change the budgets again after the start-up and so undo the raise; the search counts a load whose bytes read as a store too (section 8), given by the byte that reading starts at, so the bytes before the RVA tell which it is: `PROBLEM: The texture budget is not raised this session: the budgets at RVA 0x481D0A8 have 1 possible writer besides the start-up's stores, the first at RVA ..., which could undo the raise later.`; the measurement still runs and its line of the writers names every one.
So do the most objects or a budget not 0 at TweaksV's start, read after `IsReadable`: the game's start-up has stored them before TweaksV's `DllMain` - as when a loader starts TweaksV late, or a game build stores them earlier - and made the call the raise redirects with them, so that a redirect would never run: `PROBLEM: The texture budget is not raised this session: the most objects at RVA 0x481D0A0 and the budgets at RVA 0x481D0A8 were set before TweaksV started (MaxStreamingResources: 98304, MaxStreamingMemory_0..3_Base: 1500, 1500, 3000 and 3000 MB): the game's start-up ran before TweaksV's DllMain, and the call after its stores with it.`, or `... cannot be read.`; and so does the index not readable at TweaksV's start, which the raise reads on the game's start-up thread: `PROBLEM: The texture budget is not raised this session: the index at RVA 0x44C3B68 (Q-E-T3) cannot be read.`; while they are 0 - as the first summary, 10 seconds in, has found them in my starts - `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` says so (below).
Then `RedirectCalls` (section 9) sends the call through a call stub, read+execute, to the part's function, which runs on the game's start-up thread right after the stores, before any streaming: the four the game had are kept for the measurement and a flag goes up before the first write, each budget below the key's size in bytes is raised to it and none lowered - compared unsigned, as the placement compares them - the flag goes down once the four are published, and the game's function is called with the four registers that can carry its arguments and its result handed back as it came.
The research did not read that function's arguments: the start-up sets none up between its stores and the call and gives it no stack arguments (`sub rsp, 0x20` right before it, PATTERNS.md).
Its line can throw nothing into the game, and the game's function is always called.
The lines: at the start `The call after the budgets' stores at RVA 0x509F9F is redirected: the texture budgets below 6000 MB are raised to it when the game's start-up sets them.`, and when the start-up sets them `The texture budgets of gameconfig.xml (MaxStreamingMemory_0..3_Base: 1500, 1500, 3000 and 3000 MB) are raised to 6000 MB where they are below it: 6000, 6000, 6000 and 6000 MB now; the texture quality's index 2 uses 6000 MB.` - with none below the key `... are at least 1500 MB each - left as they are; the texture quality's index 2 uses 3000 MB.`, an index outside 0 to 3 at that moment `...; the texture quality's index 7 has no budget.` A call that cannot be redirected is a problem, `The texture budget is not raised this session: the call after the budgets' stores at RVA ... could not be redirected (details in TweaksV.log).`, and with `DRY_RUN` nothing is redirected: `DRY_RUN: would redirect the call after the budgets' stores at RVA 0x509F9F.` and `DRY_RUN: the texture budgets below 6000 MB would be raised to it when the game's start-up sets them.` Nothing else is written: of the game's code the call's rel32 alone, of its data the four budgets alone.

With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` each site is logged once its checks have passed, but for the call after the stores, whose line comes before its check, so that a call another mod hooked shows where it leads; and the measurement follows the raise, as an aid - logged, not reported (NOTES.md, T-DIAG), an exception ending it alone (`DIAGNOSTIC: unexpected exception - the texture budget's measurement is skipped for this session.`): first `Q-E-T5`, its own pattern, each check that fails logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: the texture budget is not measured: <why>.` - the update check's switch, `Q-E-T5`'s disp32 plus one byte, the imm8 after it, inside the game with its three factors, 16 bytes, and aligned to the 4 bytes of their reads; `Q-E-T5` loading the counters and calling, its rel32 past the match, the getter `Q-E-T3` matches; and past its match, read after `IsReadable`, `cmp byte [rip+disp32], 0` of the flag at +0x4B, the flag inside the game, and the factors' `mulss xmm, [rip+disp32]` - `F3`, maybe a REX, `0F 59`, a ModRM of mod 00 and r/m 101 - at +0x54 and +0xA0, leading to the switch's +8 and +4; the announcement follows its lines.
On 1.0.1158.16, at the RVAs the research found, with `VIDEO_MEMORY_BUDGET=6000`:

```
DIAGNOSTIC: store of the most objects at RVA 0x509F5B -> RVA 0x481D0A0, from the configuration's +0x54C (Q-E-T1)
DIAGNOSTIC: store of budgets 0 and 1 at RVA 0x509F6F -> RVA 0x481D0A8, from the configuration's +0x550 in MB (Q-E-T1)
DIAGNOSTIC: store of budgets 2 and 3 at RVA 0x509F85 -> RVA 0x481D0B8, from the configuration's +0x558 in MB (Q-E-T2)
DIAGNOSTIC: writers of the budgets at RVA 0x481D0A8: the start-up's stores at RVA 0x509F6F and RVA 0x509F85, none besides them (Q-E-T1, Q-E-T2)
DIAGNOSTIC: call after the budgets' stores at RVA 0x509F9F -> RVA 0x781C90 (Q-E-T2)
DIAGNOSTIC: budget getter at RVA 0x1C38DB0: the index at RVA 0x44C3B68, the budgets at +0x28 of the counters (Q-E-T3)
DIAGNOSTIC: placement check at RVA 0x4DBCB0: the numbers at RVA 0x481D090 and RVA 0x481D094, the most at RVA 0x481D0A0, the bytes at RVA 0x481D080 and RVA 0x481D088 (Q-E-T4)
DIAGNOSTIC: the most objects at RVA 0x481D0A0 and the budgets at RVA 0x481D0A8 are still 0 at TweaksV's start: the game's start-up sets them after TweaksV's DllMain, so the call after its stores is still to come
DIAGNOSTIC: call after the budgets' stores at RVA 0x509F9F: E8 EC 7C 27 00 -> E8 <the rel32 of TweaksV's stub>
The call after the budgets' stores at RVA 0x509F9F is redirected: the texture budgets below 6000 MB are raised to it when the game's start-up sets them.
DIAGNOSTIC: update check at RVA 0xCEE8D6: its switch at RVA 0x28DB5B0, the counters at RVA 0x481D080, the getter's call at RVA 0xCEE8F8 -> RVA 0x1C38DB0 (Q-E-T5)
DIAGNOSTIC: update check's flag at RVA 0xCEE921 -> RVA 0x432AD60, its factors at RVA 0xCEE92A -> RVA 0x28DB5B8 and RVA 0xCEE976 -> RVA 0x28DB5B4 (Q-E-T5)
DIAGNOSTIC: the texture budget's counters at RVA 0x481D080 (Q-E-T4): the streamed textures and buffers are logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while their highs, the update check or the budget in use change.
```

The measurement (section 6.6, thread `texture budget summary`) reads the counters, the index, the flag and the update check's switch and factors after `IsReadable`, each read aligned, as the checks above hold it, so read whole; nothing is written or called.
A reading has something new to say when it is the first, when it brings a new high - of the bytes of both in whole MB, of the objects, or of the game's own mark in whole MB, TweaksV's own highs being the most any reading saw - when the update check's flag, its switch or a factor, the index, a budget or the most changed, when the raise has run since the last summary, and when the counters became readable or unreadable; the summary comes 10 seconds in and then every minute with something new.
Before the first summary, and again whenever they changed, the update check's switch and factors are logged - the research could not see their values, nor whether the game sets them only after its start - `DIAGNOSTIC: texture budget: the update check's switch holds 1 (on), its factors 0.9 of the budget to start evicting, 0.8 to stop, and 0.95 of the video memory Windows gives the game to start evicting too.`, ` (off: nothing is evicted ahead of time)` in the place of ` (on)` for 0; then the summary, here with the full fake game's numbers:

```
DIAGNOSTIC: texture budget: 2845 MB of the 3000 MB budget for the texture quality's index 2 (MaxStreamingMemory_0..3_Base: 1500, 1500, 3000 and 3000 MB) - textures 2456 MB, buffers 389 MB; 23456 of the 98304 objects allowed (MaxStreamingResources) - 22222 textures, 1234 buffers; the most so far 2900 MB by the game's own mark, 2845 MB and 23456 objects at TweaksV's readings; the update check's flag is down.
```

While the flag is raised it ends `the update check's flag is up: it evicts ahead of time`; once the bytes reach the budget in use ` - at the budget: the streaming engine evicts what it cannot place (texture loss)` follows, and ` - at the most objects: ...` once the objects reach the most; while the budgets and the most are all 0 - before the game's start-up has read `gameconfig.xml`, which can come after the first summary - ` - the budgets and the most are not set yet (the game sets them when it reads gameconfig.xml)` follows, and a limit of 0 is never named; an index outside 0 to 3 is named in the budget's place (` - the texture quality's index 7 has no budget`), and counters that cannot be read give `DIAGNOSTIC: texture budget: the counters at RVA ... cannot be read.` Once the part has raised a budget, the parenthesis gives the game's own four and the raised ones, ` (MaxStreamingMemory_0..3_Base: 1500, 1500, 3000 and 3000 MB, raised to 6000, 6000, 6000 and 6000 MB by VIDEO_MEMORY_BUDGET)`, the budget in use being the raised one: `2845 MB of the 6000 MB budget for the texture quality's index 2`.
Both come from one reading: the raise's record of the budgets the game had is read before the budgets, and the budgets again when the raise comes while they are read, so that a summary never pairs the game's budgets with budgets read before the raise; and again while the raise's flag is up, so that a half-raised set never passes as the game's own - the raise's writes are a few instructions, made once, and only the measurement's thread waits for them, for a second at most.
A flag still up after that says the raise did not end: that reading gives no summary, a first summary coming with the first reading that is used, and the log says so once, `DIAGNOSTIC: texture budget: the raise of the budgets has not ended after 1 second: no summary comes until it has.`

`[RESOURCES] STREAMED_OBJECTS`, 131072 by default, the settings table taking 0 to 147456, raises the most objects in the same redirected call, under the same flag; 0 is said in one line, `STREAMED_OBJECTS=0 - the most streamed objects are not raised.`, and 1 to 98,303 is raised to 98,304 and reported in the settings' wording.
The number sizes nothing and has one reader, the placement's check, but it is all that stands between a load and the game's CPU-side descriptor heap, which has no check at its end (PATTERNS.md, "Resources", NOTES.md, T-DESCRIPTORS).
It looks for `Q-E-T6` too, after the shared checks, and a failure of its own ends its raise alone with its own problem, `PROBLEM: The most streamed objects are not raised this session: <why>.`, the budgets still raised: `Q-E-T6`'s two dwords, inside the game, aligned and readable; the heap, inside the game and aligned; its maker's call, its rel32 past the match, into the game; and the scan of section 8 over the most's 4 bytes, the start-up's store its only writer (`the most objects at RVA ... have 1 possible writer besides the start-up's store, the first at RVA ..., which could undo the raise later`).
What the shared checks refuse, each key's own problem says, one each.
In the redirected function it reads the most and the two dwords, the heap's size their sum and the top 3/4 of that, and writes the key, or the top where that cuts it, as one aligned dword, only if the most is below it, compared unsigned, never lowering; on this game the size is 196,608 and the top 147,456.
Its lines: `The call after the budgets' stores at RVA 0x509F9F is redirected: the texture budgets below 6000 MB are raised to it and the most objects below 131072 to it when the game's start-up sets them.`; `The most streamed objects of gameconfig.xml (MaxStreamingResources: 98304) are raised to 131072 by STREAMED_OBJECTS; the game's descriptor heap holds 196608.`; `... are at least 131072 - left as they are; ...`; a top that cuts the key but stays above the game's own, `... are raised to 119808, 3/4 of the game's descriptor heap of 159744, below the 131072 of STREAMED_OBJECTS.`; and `DRY_RUN: the most streamed objects below 131072 would be raised to it when the game's start-up sets them.`
Three things write nothing, each with one problem, from the raise itself, so usually after the start-up summary: no size (`the game's descriptor heap's size at RVA ... and RVA ... (Q-E-T6) reads 0`), a top not above the game's own (`3/4 of the game's descriptor heap of 98304, 73728, is not above the game's own 98304`), and a `gameconfig.xml` most above the top, `PROBLEM: MaxStreamingResources of gameconfig.xml, 200000, is above 147456, 3/4 of the game's descriptor heap of 196608: that heap has no check at its end, and a load that reaches it crashes. It is left as it is.`
With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, the keys at 0 too, `Q-E-T6` and `Q-E-T7` give the walk, each failure logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: the descriptors are not measured: <why>.` and ending the walk alone: `Q-E-T7` lying 0x36 bytes into the maker `Q-E-T6`'s call leads to, past its match the store of the head.
Each reading, once the size at the heap's +0x2C is not 0, reads the list's pointer and, after `IsReadable` over the size's dwords, walks it from the head while the index is below the size and at most the size's steps; each read an aligned dword, nothing written or called, no lock, so a race ends the walk early or long by what is taken or freed meanwhile, the bound ends a cycle, an index past the size ends it early, and in use is the size less the steps.
The summary gains `98343 of the 131072 objects allowed (MaxStreamingResources: 98304, raised by STREAMED_OBJECTS) - ...; 112345 of the 196608 descriptors in use; ...`, or `the descriptors cannot be read; ` for a list that cannot be read, a size past 16,777,216 among them, ends ` - near the descriptors' end: the game has no check there` below 16,384 free, and a new high of them is something new to say.
### 11.13 RayTracing (`InitRayTracing`)

The game skins every moving object its ray tracing keeps into one global vertex buffer each frame, the output of its `bvh_skinning` compute effect, which the BLAS refits read before the TLASes are built: 4,194,304 vertices of 20 bytes on 1.0.1158.16, 80 MB, made once at the renderer's start, after `DllMain`, by the set-up's call of the buffer maker with the count as an immediate (`T-E-B1`).
Each frame's BVH build hands out ranges of it from 0, by a counter per frame slot, and two checks - every moving object's geometry's (`T-E-C1`) and the ropes' (`T-E-C2`) - refuse a range whose end would pass the same immediate: the object then has no ray traced shadow, reflection or occlusion in that frame, and as the order of the objects changes they come and go (PATTERNS.md, "RayTracing").
`DYNAMIC_BVH_VERTICES` under `[RAYTRACING]` is 0, the game's own and the default, or 4,194,304 to 26,843,545 vertices; the settings' range is 0..26843545, so a value above the cap is clamped and reported by them (section 4.1).
The cap keeps a raw view of the buffer within 2^27 32-bit elements, far below the 26 bits the ray tracing shaders read an object's first vertex in and the game's 32-bit sizes; a `static_assert` ties it to the setting's maximum.
With the key at 0 the part logs `DYNAMIC_BVH_VERTICES=0 - the ray tracing buffer of moving geometry is not raised.` and, without `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, looks for nothing and writes nothing.

Otherwise it looks for its three patterns, each through `Scanner::FindOnce`, so that a second match shows - each one missing logged by the scanner, the first that does not match once the reason (`T-E-B1 was not found.`, or for one that matches twice or more `the game code holds T-E-B1 2 or more times, not the expected 1.`, as the relocation core says it) - and checks, in this order, the first failure ending the job with nothing written: the set-up's call at +24 of `T-E-B1` leads into the game (`the buffer maker's call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already) (T-E-B1).`), to a function start by the game's unwind data alone, as the siren count checks find-bone-by-name (`the buffer maker's call at RVA ... leads to RVA ..., which is not a function start (inside the function at RVA ... (unwind data)) (T-E-B1).`, or `(no unwind data)`); the buffer object's global, the disp32 at +32, inside the game, aligned to its 8 bytes - the game's threads write these data while the part's read them, and only an aligned value is read whole - and readable (`the buffer object's global at RVA ... (T-E-B1) is not inside the game.`, `... is not aligned to 8 bytes.`, `... cannot be read.`); each check's frame slot (its disp32 at +2) and counters (+11) inside the game, aligned to the 4 bytes of the dword each is read as, and readable, 4 and 8 bytes (`the frame slot at RVA ... (T-E-C1) is not inside the game.`, `the counters at RVA ... (T-E-C1) are not inside the game.`, each also `... not aligned to 4 bytes.` and `... cannot be read.`), and both checks loading the same ones (`the two checks load different frame slots: RVA ... (T-E-C1) and RVA ... (T-E-C2).`, `the two checks load different counters: ...`); each check's refusal jump, its rel32 right past the match read after `IsReadable`, leading into the game (`the geometry's refusal jump at RVA ... cannot be read (T-E-C1).`, `the geometry's refusal jump at RVA ... does not lead into the game (another mod may have hooked it already) (T-E-C1).`, the ropes' alike); the counters referenced by exactly three `lea r64, [rip+disp32]` or `mov [rip+disp32], r64` in the game's code (`Scanner::FindRipRelativeReferences`, section 8) - the two checks' and the frame's reset - and by no fourth (`the game's code references the counters at RVA ... in 4 places or more, not 3 - the two checks and the frame's reset, as the research found them.`); and the buffer's count (+9 of `T-E-B1`) and the checks' capacities (+44 of `T-E-C1`, +41 of `T-E-C2`) holding one value (`the buffer's count and its two checks disagree: 4194304 at RVA ... (T-E-B1), 4194304 at RVA ... (T-E-C1) and 8388608 at RVA ... (T-E-C2).`).
The bytes the part relies on - the count's `mov ecx`, the stride 20, the maker's call, the object's store, the slots' loads and the counters' `lea` with their ModRM, the `lea` that adds ecx - which the stubs' `mov eax, ecx` rests on - with its ModRM and its SIB naming rcx, the `cmp` of that sum and the `ja` with its rel32 right past the match - are checked against the patterns when TweaksV is compiled (`PatternByte`).
With the key not 0 a failure is the part's problem, `The ray tracing buffer of moving geometry is not raised this session: <why>`; with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` it is also logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: the ray tracing buffer of moving geometry is not measured: <why>`, the only line with the key at 0.

With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` every site is logged as it passes: `DIAGNOSTIC: buffer's count at RVA ... holds 4194304, the buffer maker's call at RVA ... -> RVA ..., a function start (unwind data) (T-E-B1)`, `DIAGNOSTIC: buffer object's store at RVA ... -> RVA ..., which holds 0: the buffer is not made yet (T-E-B1)` (or `which holds 0x...: the buffer is made already`), `DIAGNOSTIC: geometry's check at RVA ... holds 4194304, its frame slot at RVA ..., its counters at RVA ..., its refusal jump at RVA ... -> RVA ...: 31 C0 ... (T-E-C1)` with the refusal's first 8 bytes, the ropes' alike (`ropes' check`, `T-E-C2`), and `DIAGNOSTIC: the counters at RVA ... are referenced in 3 places of the game's code: RVA ..., RVA ... and RVA ...`.

The raise, with the key not 0.
The three immediates holding another value than the game's own 4,194,304 are another mod's raise, left as it is and logged, not a problem: `The ray tracing buffer of moving geometry is not raised this session: another mod already set it to 8388608 vertices - left as it is.` A key below the game's own is raised to it and reported, as the texture budget reports its key, `TweaksV.ini: [RAYTRACING] DYNAMIC_BVH_VERTICES=1000 is outside 0 or 4194304..26843545 - using 4194304.`, and the game's own changes nothing: `The ray tracing buffer of moving geometry holds 4194304 vertices already (DYNAMIC_BVH_VERTICES=1000) - nothing to patch.` A buffer object's global that is not 0 at the start - a build that makes the buffer before TweaksV's `DllMain` - refuses the raise, since checks raised alone would let the game's GPU animation write past the buffer: `the game has made the buffer already - its object at RVA ... is set (T-E-B1) - and a raise of its checks alone would let the game write past it.` Otherwise one `WriteCode` writes the three immediates, all or nothing - `ray tracing buffer's count`, `geometry's capacity check` and `ropes' capacity check` - and the part logs `The ray tracing buffer of moving geometry is raised from 4194304 to 8388608 vertices (80 to 160 MB).`, the MB rounded; a write that fails is `the game code could not be changed (details in TweaksV.log).` With `DRY_RUN` the three writes' lines come, then `DRY_RUN: would raise the ray tracing buffer of moving geometry from 4194304 to 8388608 vertices (RVA ..., RVA ... and RVA ...).`

The measurement, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` only - also with the key at 0 - of the buffer as the part leaves it.
Two counting stubs, one per check, 32 bytes each on the first page of a block of two pages near the game (`AllocateNearGame`), their counters - the geometries refused and their vertices, two 64-bit values per stub - on the second: `lock inc qword [rip+disp32]`, `mov eax, ecx` - the vertex count the check added, zero-extended - `lock add qword [rip+disp32], rax` and `jmp [rip+0]` on to the game's refusal, whose address follows.
They run on the game's worker threads, which take their ranges with `lock cmpxchg`, so they count with lock instructions; they keep every register but rax and the flags, which both refusals overwrite or ignore, and use no stack.
The first page is made read+execute once written - never writable and executable at once - and the second stays read/write.
Then the summary's thread starts (section 6.6, `ray tracing buffer summary`; one that cannot start: `its summary thread could not be started.`, the block freed and nothing more done), then the sampling thread (`ray tracing buffer sampling`, through `StartModuleThread`; `DIAGNOSTIC: the ray tracing buffer's frames are not sampled: their thread could not be started.`), and only then are both jumps pointed at their stubs, one `WriteCode` of the two 6-byte `ja`, both or neither, so that a measurement that cannot sum up redirects nothing: `DIAGNOSTIC: the refusal jumps at RVA ... (T-E-C1) and RVA ... (T-E-C2) are redirected to counting stubs: every geometry and rope the full buffer refuses is counted.` A buffer the game has made already - its object's global set at the start - gets no stubs, since the game's threads may run the checks then and a jump's rel32 can cross a cache line, so that a check could read it half written: `DIAGNOSTIC: the ray tracing buffer's refusals are not counted: the game has made the buffer already - its threads may run the checks while their jumps are rewritten.` A block that cannot be had, a stub out of reach of its jump, a block that cannot be made read+execute (Windows' dynamic code policy, for one) and a write that fails are logged, `DIAGNOSTIC: the ray tracing buffer's refusals are not counted: <why>`, and the block freed; with `DRY_RUN` nothing is redirected: `DRY_RUN: would redirect the refusal jumps at RVA ... (T-E-C1) and RVA ... (T-E-C2) to counting stubs - the refusals are not counted.` The announcement: `DIAGNOSTIC: the ray tracing buffer of moving geometry is measured: the last finished frame's vertices are sampled every 50 ms and logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while the most taken or the refusals change.`, and with the sampling thread refused `DIAGNOSTIC: the ray tracing buffer of moving geometry is measured, its frames not sampled: the rest is logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while it changes.`

The sampling thread, every 50 ms - a frame takes 10 to 20 ms on a fast PC, so a sample catches one in three to five - reads the frame slot, the other slot's counter and the slot again, each an aligned dword after `IsReadable`, and writes nothing: the frame under way counts into its own slot's counter, and the other keeps the last finished frame's total until its own frame starts, so when both reads of the slot agree that counter is the last finished frame's total; it keeps the latest and the most.
At the first refusal the stubs count it writes, once, `DIAGNOSTIC: ray tracing buffer of moving geometry: the first geometry refused - the buffer is full, and an object it refuses has no ray traced shadow, reflection or occlusion in that frame.` Each pass inside a `try`; the thread never ends.
The summary, 10 seconds in and then every minute while the most, the refusals or the buffer object change, reads the buffer object's global and, under `CopyMemorySafely`, the object's count at +0x08 and its stride, the low 12 bits of the word at +0x0C, as the buffer maker keeps them (PATTERNS.md): `DIAGNOSTIC: ray tracing buffer of moving geometry, made with 8388608 vertices of 20 bytes: the last frame took 2811203 of 8388608 vertices (54 of 160 MB), at most 3987120 so far; no geometry refused.` In the buffer's place `not made yet`, `made, its object at 0x... cannot be read` or `its object's global at RVA ... cannot be read`; in the frames' `its frames are not sampled (see above)` or `no frame sampled yet`; for the refusals `1234 geometries refused (5612345 vertices in all), 12 of them ropes - the buffer is full: those objects had no ray traced shadow, reflection or occlusion in those frames`, `the refusals are not counted (DRY_RUN)` or `the refusals are not counted (see above)`.
Nothing of the measurement is reported: an aid (section 12).
The plugin that stays is `ResourceExtensions.asi` (section 4.3), whose page names extra capacity for the ray tracing of moving geometry; the part has no old plugin.

### 11.14 SirenSettings (`InitSirenIds`)

A siren setting of `carcols.meta` and the vehicle variation that names it carry its ID in a byte on the whole load path, and a table of 256 bytes maps an ID to the setting's position, a byte too, in which 0 means no sirens: 256 IDs and 255 settings a vehicle can use, bound silently - a pack's setting whose ID is in use is dropped, a clash within one file moves the later setting to the first free ID, an ID above 255 in a file keeps its low byte (PATTERNS.md, "Siren IDs").
`TweaksVSirenIds` widens the ID to 16 bits on that whole path; the position stays a byte, so 255 settings stay the most.
`[SIRENSETTINGS]` holds `ENABLED` and `LARGE_SIREN_IDS`, on by default, 0 or 1: there is no size to choose, since the table must cover every 16-bit value.
255 stays the game's "none", so a pack may use 0 to 65535 but 255; README asks packs to keep 0 and 65535 free too.
No old plugin: `SirenSetting_Limit_Adjuster.asi` is a Legacy plugin (D9).
With `LARGE_SIREN_IDS=0`: `LARGE_SIREN_IDS=0 - the siren setting IDs' limit is not raised.`, and nothing is looked for but with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` (section 11.16.1).

The checks, all before anything is written, a failure `PROBLEM: The siren setting IDs' limit is not raised this session: <why>`:

1. The core (section 10.2), with the 14 patterns as anchors, in the order of the table: `I-E-T1` to `T4`'s `lea` of the table, their opcodes `4C 8D 05`, `48 8D 1D`, `4C 8D 05` and `48 8D 2D`, must lead to one address inside the game, `FindRipRelativeLeas` must find exactly those 4 with a `48` or `4C` prefix, and the four free-ID searches' limits, `48 81 FE 00 01 00 00` at +0x85 and +0xCD of `I-E-T2` and at +0x81 and +0xC9 of `I-E-T4`, must each be that instruction and say 256: four that agree on another size are another mod's, `the siren setting ID table's limits say 512 entries, not the game's 256 (another mod may have raised them already).`
   There is no setting: the size to use is the table's fixed 65,536.
2. Every other site, in the order of the patterns, as section 11.16.1 has them, each line with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` alone, and for the part more strictly: an ID member's type other than 0x01 or 0x02 refuses the job (`the variation's siren setting ID member's type at RVA ... is 0x3, not 0x1 or 0x2, which store a byte (another mod may have widened it already) (I-E-P2).`), so does a type byte outside the section the game's section table names `.data` (`IsInsideGameData`, section 7; `the siren setting's ID member's type at RVA ... does not lie in the game's .data (I-E-P1).`), since that byte is the one write outside the game's code, and so does an offset field whose low dword is neither 0 - not registered yet, since the game may register its structures after TweaksV has started - nor the member's offset (`the siren setting's ID member's offset field at RVA ... holds 0x8, not +0x0 (I-E-P1).`); no boot has shown what its upper dword holds, so it counts for nothing, and the line of `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` gives the whole field.

The one write, all or nothing (`ApplyRelocation`, with `RedirectCalls`), each with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` as `DIAGNOSTIC: <what> at RVA ...: <old> -> <new>`:

- **The table:** 65,536 entries of a byte, read/write, near the game, the game's own 256 copied, the rest 0xFF, free; its 4 `lea` and its 4 limits, as the core writes them.
- **The IDs' code:** the nine byte loads made word loads (`0F B6` -> `0F B7`: `I-E-T1` +0x93 and +0xC4, `I-E-T2` +0x30 and +0x4B, `I-E-T3` +0x8D and +0xBE, `I-E-T4` +0x28 and +0x42, `I-E-D1` +0x0B) and the two byte stores of a new ID word stores (`40 88 31` -> `66 89 31` at `I-E-T2` +0x29, `40 88 33` -> `66 89 33` at `I-E-T4` +0x21).
- **The dedupe's compare loop,** `I-E-D2`: as a word compare it is one byte longer, so it is written from +2, over the 10-byte NOP before it, `66 39 54 35 00`, `0F 84` with its rel32 recomputed to the same target, `48 81 C6 E8 05 00 00`, `48 39 F3`, `75 E9` back to its new start, and a 9-byte NOP up to where it ended; nothing else jumps into those bytes (the findings).
- **The three constructors' defaults,** the high byte of an ID no file gives: `I-E-C1` +8's dead store at +0x1C becomes `66 C7 01 FF 00 66 90`, `mov word [rcx], 0x00FF`, which the byte store `C6 06 FF` then leaves as it is; the variation's two, 30 and 34 bytes from 15 before `I-E-C2` and `I-E-C3`, write their zeros in shorter stores and +0x4C = 0 in the room that gives, in the same bytes.
- **The two parser members' types** (`I-E-P1`'s member 0, `I-E-P2`'s member 6): 0x01 or 0x02 made 0x03 or 0x04, in the game's `.data` - TweaksV's first and only write to the game's data, the code and TweaksV's own memory aside; `WriteCode` finds the page writable and leaves its protection as it is.
- **The base loader's call,** `I-E-R1` +13, redirected: the vehicle metadata's init fills its own table, which nothing reads any more, and calls the base loader; the stub fills the moved table with 0xFF and calls the loader, so that the table is free whenever the game frees its own - the registers go on as they came and the result comes back, the init setting up no argument the bytes before the call show.

What is checked and left as it is: the four compares with 255, now on the 16-bit value; the two `40 B6 FF` when none is free, since a search that ends with `rsi` 0x10000 leaves `si` 0x00FF, "none"; the dedupe's `80 FA FF` on the low byte, so that a pack's ID `n*256+255` skips the dedupe, is appended and gets a free ID when its own is taken - the earlier setting keeps the ID, one array slot more; the two `88 .. ..` stores into a variation, which nothing reads afterwards.

The lines: `The siren setting ID table moved and its limit raised from 256 to 65536 entries.`, then `The siren setting IDs' limit is raised to 65535: their 11 loads and stores, a pack's dedupe's loop, the 3 constructors' defaults and the 2 parser members' types are written, and the base loader call at RVA ... is redirected, so that the moved table is filled whenever the game fills its own.`
With `DRY_RUN` one line, as the core's other parts have it: `DRY_RUN: would move the siren setting ID table (4 references) and raise its limit from 256 to 65536 (4 sites) and widen the IDs' 11 loads and stores, a pack's dedupe's loop, the 3 constructors' defaults and the 2 parser members' types, and redirect the base loader call at RVA ....`

With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, the part at work (with `DRY_RUN` only `DRY_RUN: would hook a pack's carcols callback at RVA ... and the base builder at RVA ... and take a pack's dedupe load through a stub, to count the settings dropped and moved.` and `DRY_RUN: would redirect the file opener's call at RVA ..., to name a pack's carcols file.` and `DRY_RUN: would hook a pack's carvariations callback at RVA ..., to count the vehicles whose siren setting ID finds no setting.`):

- **The measurement over the moved table,** as section 11.16.1 has it, its numbers words: `DIAGNOSTIC: siren settings loaded: 31 (vehicles can use 255 at most); siren setting IDs in use: 31 of 65536, 4 of them past 255; settings past position 255, which no vehicle can reach: 0; IDs given to more than one setting: 0; settings the game dropped so far: 2, moved: 1.`
- **The settings dropped and moved, per file,** an aid (section 12): `I-E-H1` and `I-E-H2`, each found once and at a function's start (`DIAGNOSTIC: a pack's carcols callback at RVA ..., a function start (unwind data) (I-E-H1)`), hooked with `HookFunctions`, and `I-E-D1`'s load of a pack's ID through a stub of its own, `E9 rel32 90` at +0x0B.
  A pack's file is read inside the callback, so at the callback's start its settings are not in memory, and a dropped setting never reaches the game's array: the dedupe's load is the one place that sees all of a pack's IDs.
  The stub loads the word, keeps it in a record of 4,096 IDs behind it, counting any past that, keeps every register but `rdx`, which the load writes, and jumps back; the flags are free, since what `I-E-D1` pins after the load is a `movzx` and the compare; it lies on a page near the game, read+execute once written, its record on a read/write page behind it.
  The callback's hook reads the settings' IDs before and after the game's function and, by the dedupe's rule over the record, says `DIAGNOSTIC: a pack's carcols, dlcpacks:/sipack1/data/carcols.meta, brought 6 siren settings: 4 added, 2 dropped, their IDs taken (5, 300), 0 moved.`; at most 16 IDs named; a file the game could not read or parse, the callback's byte false, says `DIAGNOSTIC: a pack's carcols, <name>, could not be read or parsed: the game takes no siren setting from it.` The callback never gets the name: `I-E-H3`'s call of the game's file opener is redirected with the hooks, and copies the text at +0 of the data file's entry, 127 bytes, since an entry moves, with the parsed object it is handed, under a lock; the line takes it only if the object is the one the callback finds at its third argument, else `(no name)`, so it can never be a wrong file's, and an aid to an aid, it cannot stop the rest: `DIAGNOSTIC: a pack's carcols files are not named: I-E-H3 was not found.` Printable ASCII; the log writes a profile folder as `%USERPROFILE%`.
- **The vehicles whose siren setting ID finds no setting,** an aid of its own, at load, nothing per frame, and whatever `SIRENS_PER_VEHICLE` says: `I-E-H4` hooked with `HookFunctions`; after the game's function, through `CopyMemorySafely`, each variation of the parsed object whose position is 0xFF named, 16 to a line: `DIAGNOSTIC: a pack's carvariations brought 5 vehicles: 2 name a siren setting ID that finds no setting: sicar1, sicar4.`, with none `none names`; the base game's own go through the base loop and are not seen, and a variation that names no ID keeps its index and does not show.
  The base builder's hook compares the settings' IDs before and after it: `DIAGNOSTIC: the base carcols brought 24 siren settings: 1 moved (7 to 0), 1 without an ID given one.`
  These lines stop at `kMaxLinesPerKind` (`DIAGNOSTIC: 20000 lines of carcols files written - from now on they are only counted; the summary every minute has the numbers.`).
  What cannot be set up is logged, not reported, and the part stands, its load a plain word load: `DIAGNOSTIC: the siren settings dropped and moved are not counted: I-E-H1 was not found.`
  Both hooks keep the game's last error and its result, and let nothing throw into the game.

**For another module:** `SirenSettingsNow()` gives where the siren settings lie this moment - their array, their count and the 0x5E8 bytes from one to the next, through the carcols pointer of `I-E-T4` - or nothing while the game has none loaded or they cannot be read; the lighting capacity's measurement counts through it.
It works once this module has looked for its sites, with the part at work and with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` while the part or the key is off; `I-E-T4` found is all it takes, whatever the sites after it hold, though the part goes through its patterns in order and stops at the first missing or not as the findings have it, so one before `I-E-T4` leaves nothing.
