A read of the array goes through `CopyMemorySafely`, since it grows while packs load.
In test builds the module logs what it gives at its start and at each summary: `TEST BUILD: the siren settings' accessor gives 24 at 0x... (outside the game), 0x5E8 bytes apart.`, or `... gives nothing.`, since no code of the module reads it.

The risks, each with what settles it:

1. What the game's PSO loader makes of a member wider than its binary file's - the base game's `carcols.ymt` and `carvariations.ymt` and any binary pack - is answered: it loads member by member by name hash, each value read by the file's type and stored by the member's (PATTERNS.md, "Siren IDs"), and my boots compared the base game's settings and IDs with `LARGE_SIREN_IDS=0` and `=1` (section 11.16.1 logs them): the same 27 settings, the base game's IDs the same, the packs' IDs whole.
   The same numbers, and the design stands; not the same, and the two members are widened only after the base loader has returned, in the stub, binary packs keeping their 8-bit IDs; the default follows that comparison.
2. TweaksV's first write to the game's data: the two members' type bytes, checked first, at their fixed places from the registrations' tables.
3. Packs that rely on the cut low byte - a setting's 372 named as 116 by its vehicles, or the reverse - lose their link once IDs are 16 bits, as with the original.
   A variation that names a setting which is not there finds a free entry, so its index 0xFF goes to the model, the has-sirens bit is set, and the game's getter, with no upper bound, returns a place past the array at two calls on each pass while the sirens are on, the renderer's and the vehicle lights', which reads four dwords of it: the game's own hazard for a missing ID. With `SIRENS_PER_VEHICLE` above 20 both calls are guarded (section 11.14.1) and such a vehicle gets the empty setting, no lights; at 20 the game's calls stand.
   With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` the count at load names such vehicles (above).
4. More than 255 settings still wrap: the index stays a byte (the findings' 4.4 has what widening it would take).
5. Other mods that read the IDs or the table as bytes see 16-bit values; no plugin on Enhanced is known to.
6. A game update moves the sites or changes the facts PATTERNS.md's "When Game Updates" item 5 lists; the scan refuses then.
7. The protection reads gap bytes between functions, at RVA 0x135D21A; no site writes a gap byte, the variations' constructors keeping their padding.

#### 11.14.1 The sirens per vehicle's lights (`InitSirensPerVehicle`, `FindSirenLights`)

`SIRENS_PER_VEHICLE` under `[SIRENSETTINGS]`, 20 to 32, 32 by default (D38), clamped and reported as other sizes; 20 is the game's own: `SIRENS_PER_VEHICLE=20 - the sirens per vehicle are not raised.`, nothing looked for, nothing written.
Above 20 every structure of the game stays at 20: the lights past 20 by a second call of the game's own renderer on stand-ins of the part's own, the damage by the game's own code raised (`FindSirenDamage`).
The checks, all before anything is written, each failure `PROBLEM: The sirens per vehicle are not raised this session: <why>`, the vehicles at 20: the game's build, 1.0.1158.16 alone (D40: `this game build is 1.0.1013.17, and TweaksV has read the siren lights' renderer of 1.0.1158.16 only. Vehicles keep 20 sirens until a new TweaksV.`); `LightStoresRaised()` (D39); `FindSirenLights`, then `FindSirenDamage`.
`FindSirenLights` looks for `I-E-B1`, `I-E-Z1` to `Z7` and, through `FindSirenSettingSites` of `TweaksVSirenIds`, which keeps the matches the IDs' writes would hide, `I-E-C1`, `I-E-P1` and `I-E-M1`.
It checks the vehicle lights' two calls lead into the game, the getter's to `I-E-M1`'s match (by the match, as the getter has no unwind data), and the carcols pointer it reads inside the game; `I-E-Z1`, `Z4`, `Z5` and `Z6` inside the function the renderer's call leads to, `I-E-Z7` inside the one `I-E-Z3` starts, by the unwind data; `I-E-Z1` +24 leading to `I-E-B1` +24's getter; the constructor a function start; and the lights' member, entry 19 of `I-E-P1`'s tables: at +0x3C, its data in the game's `.data`, its offset field 0 or 0x3C, type 0x0D, subtype 1, 20 elements of 0x48 bytes, its element's data, if there yet, in `.data`, type 0x0C, which the hook asks again of the element's member object.
The one write: the hook on `I-E-Z3` first, since it can be taken back, and taken back it is when the rest is refused or throws, the problem saying if it stays on; then one `RedirectCalls` inside a `try` with the three calls, `I-E-B1` +13 and both of the getter's, `I-E-B1` +24 and `I-E-Z1` +24, and the state's size, `I-E-Z2` +1, 0x188 -> 0x16A8, with the damage's writes in it: `The sirens per vehicle are raised to 32.` An exception from a line after the writes finds them in, by the renderer's call, and they stand.
With `DRY_RUN` the lines that it would, `DRY_RUN: would hook the array member's tree reader at RVA ....` among them.

**The settings.** The hook runs after the game's reader: one compare of the member's data per array read, and only the lights go further.
The key is read at the structure, r8, plus the offset in the member data at the call.
A node of more than 20 children has up to `SIRENS_PER_VEHICLE` - 20 more read by the element's own slot 1 into a siren setting made by the game's constructor in zeroed memory, its head and tail lights zero, read with no lock held and under a guard against an access violation.
Each light's start is corrected once, forward start + 20 x delta, reversed start - 20 x delta, rotation and flashiness alike, so that the renderer's delta x i gives its true index.
Its key is a 64-bit hash of the 20 lights the game kept; a table of 255, allocated at the first, keeps it: a twin is dropped, one with the same 20 lights and others past them replaces the first, the old object staying, since a renderer may be reading it; each entry whole before the count or the pointer leads to it, with a release store, and no lock on the renderer's path.
A table that is full or a read that faults is reported once: `Some siren settings' lights past 20 are not kept this session: ...`, and that setting keeps 20 lights; memory that cannot be had the same, `The lights past 20 of the siren settings are not kept this session: ...`.
A light's group past the renderer's 4 becomes 0, as an index it does not check.

**The second call.** `I-E-Z2`'s allocation holds the state, a second state at +0x190 and a stand-in vehicle of 0x1388 bytes at +0x320, 16-aligned by `operator new`, freed whole by the game.
The wrapper calls the game's renderer, then looks the model up in the table of 1,024 (section 11.16.2); a model that does not fit keeps 20 sirens, reported once: `Some vehicle models' lights past 20 are not lit this session: ...`.
On a model's first pass, with the last error kept: a block is made when the room behind its bone structure (`TweaksVSirenRoom.h`) names bones past `siren20` and its setting's 20 lights hash to an own setting, whose scalars +0x04 to +0x23 and +0x38 take the real one's, the last model's where two with the same 20 lights differ, a limit logged; the block stands in for the model info: +0xB0 leads to bytes whose +0x99 + k is `siren(21 + k)`'s bone, the rest -1.
For a model with a block: a first state with another start time than the second's has been made anew, or the marker at +0x318 does not name the second as made yet, the heap's bytes being what they may, so the second and the stand-in are zeroed and the beat at +0x08 set to -1; the stand-in's +0x48, +0x6C, +0x80 (16 bytes), +0xDC and +0x986 are the vehicle's, +0x860 the vehicle's >> 20, +0x1380 the second state; the renderer again with the stand-in, the block and the same floats, flag, skeleton, dword and list, the first call's result and last error going back.
Both calls of the getter lead to one plain function (D41), the game's getter left out: a block, which is no model info, gets its own setting, by one range check; position 0 gets none; a position below the array's count that entry, the count read before the pointer; any other, or no carcols data, the empty setting, 0x5E8 bytes of TweaksV's `.rdata`, zero but for `sequencerBpm` 200, which lights nothing and leaves the head and tail lights as with none.
A model's first pass takes the same bound, and a model past it gets no block: `not lit past siren20: its siren setting's position lies past the game's settings.`
`SIRENS_PER_VEHICLE=20` redirects neither call. The IDs' measurement walks the array by its count and reads no model's position, so it needs no bound.
Lights past 20 are per pass, so up to 8 merged lights a vehicle (`useRealLights` off), not 4; a pack's binary file with lights past 20 stays dark if it does not reach the tree reader (HINT: it does).
With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` every site is logged, each setting read has its line, capped at `kMaxLinesPerKind`, and section 11.16.2 says which models are lit.
**The damage** (`FindSirenDamage`, `TweaksVSirenDamage`) is the half of the part's one write behind the lights', with `SIRENS_PER_VEHICLE` above 20 only; it writes nothing itself, and `InitSirensPerVehicle` makes the write, so `DRY_RUN` and `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` are the patch module's.
Its 19 patterns are each looked for, so that the log names every one missing, `I-E-L08` and `I-E-N09` three times each; the first missing or held too often is the problem, `The sirens per vehicle are not raised this session: I-E-L03 was not found. Vehicles keep 20 sirens.`, in `NotAsOften`'s words, and a wrong count of those two behind it gets a line in the same words, since the scanner logs only a miss.
Every operand it writes is the game's own value, pinned by its pattern, checked when TweaksV is compiled, so a site another mod changed is a pattern not found.
Then, each failing alone with nothing written: each site of a loop lies behind the one before it, at most 0x200 bytes (`I-E-N03 at RVA ... does not lie behind I-E-L03 at RVA ... in one loop.`), the matches of the three classes paired in address order; the call at +3 of `I-E-Y3` leads into the game (`the bone structure maker's call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already) (I-E-Y3).`), to the function `I-E-Y2` lies in by the unwind data (`I-E-Y2 at RVA ... does not lie in the bone structure maker at RVA ...: the game's unwind data puts it in the function at RVA ....`, or `names no function there.`); the pool is not made yet (`the bone structures' pool at RVA ... is made already, so TweaksV has started too late to give its structures their room (I-E-Y2).`), read through the `mov rcx, [rip+disp32]` right before `I-E-Y2` by its shape, since no pattern pins it, another shape or a load outside the game refused (`the bone structures' pool cannot be read: no load of it before the allocation at RVA ... (I-E-Y2).`); `I-E-Y4` is a function start by the unwind data (`the bone structures' fill at RVA ... is not a function start (inside the function at RVA ... (unwind data)) (I-E-Y4).`, or `(no unwind data)`).
It adds 21 writes and one redirected call: the bounds to N and `I-E-L08`'s -20 to -N, the reads to the room, the hit's counter (PATTERNS.md, "Siren damage"), the pool's stride and the allocation to 0x38C, whatever N is.
The sync loop stays at 20: it reads 20 bytes of sync data, and nothing writes its sites.

The wrapper, `MadeBones`, runs at a model's load, never per frame: it hands the game's function the four register arguments, keeps its rax and the last error around its own work, and fills the room with no lock and nothing thrown into the game.
The model's structure through the two pointers from +0xB0, each read guarded; a null holder or structure, which the game's own readers allow, leaves nothing to fill.
Under an access-violation guard: the room to 0xFF, the game's 40 bytes copied in as they stand, a plane's gear doors in their slots, then the game's fill once more, flag 1, on a table of TweaksV's own: 24 entries, `siren21` to `siren32` at IDs 0x350 and on and `siren_glass21` to `siren_glass32` at 0x370 and on, names and tags made when TweaksV is compiled and pinned by `static_assert`s to the tags the game's table was read to hold.
A byte past `siren20` below 0, a bone past index 127, becomes -1, so that no such byte reaches a raised loop, and is counted for the room's line; a bone it names has its bit of the set at +0x00 cleared, for parity with RVA 0x1350360 (HINT, PATTERNS.md).
A structure that cannot be read or written, or a fault in the fill, is said once, the first model named, the game going on and that model's first 20 standing where its structure could be read, its bytes past `siren20` all -1: `The bones past siren20 are not looked up for every vehicle model this session: vehicle model 0x0BAD0003 faulted while its room was filled (an access violation), so its sirens past 20 stay dark. Only the first such model is named.`
With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`: the three site lines, each write's line, and for each model the room gives bones past `siren20` or that loses some past bone 127: `DIAGNOSTIC: the room of vehicle model 0xA1B2C3D4 holds 12 siren and 12 glass bones past siren20, up to siren32 and siren_glass32.`, with `; lost past bone 127: 1 siren and 1 glass` where they apply, capped (`kMaxLinesPerKind`).
### 11.15 StaticBounds (`InitStaticBounds`)

The game keeps the static collision bounds of the map in one store, named `StaticBounds`, and a full store ends the game at a trap.
Its size is read once, from one call (PATTERNS.md, "StaticBounds"): the store's init asks the game's pool size lookup, and the pool, the name map, the module's count, the streaming range and the box streamer's arrays all follow what comes back.
So a write to the pool's capacity alone cannot carry a load past the game's size, as one of my boots showed, and nothing has to be written beside the call.
The part gives the store at least `STATICBOUNDS_SIZE`, 1..32767, 16000 by default: 32767 is the largest size whose every slot is sound.
The key's name, its default and the rule, the larger of the two, are Nochala's Static Bounds Limit Adjuster v1.0.2's (CREDITS.md; its notice in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md), the route TweaksV's own, in the way of WeaponComponents' `W-E-C4`.
`InitStaticBounds()`:

1. Both patterns are looked for with `Scanner::FindOnce`, so that the log names each one missing.
   `C-E-L1` missing or held twice gives `C-E-L1 was not found.` or `the game code holds C-E-L1 2 or more times, not the expected 1.` (section 8), with nothing written.
   `C-E-D1` is only the read-back's: anything wrong with it is a line, the raise going on, `The static bounds store's read-back is not done this session: C-E-D1 was not found.`, its reason also the store's: `the store at ... is not inside the game (C-E-D1).` or `is not aligned to 8 bytes`, by the one data check (section 6.6).
   With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`: `DIAGNOSTIC: the static bounds store at RVA ... (C-E-D1)` and `DIAGNOSTIC: the static bounds store's size call at RVA ... -> RVA ... (C-E-L1)`.
2. The call must lead into the game: else `the static bounds store's size call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already) (C-E-L1).`, with nothing written.
   A mod's jump at the lookup's start is wrapped, the larger winning.
3. `RedirectCalls`, its original set before the rewrite, the check's first event made before, so that the game's first asking finds it.
   A dry run ends here: `DRY_RUN: would redirect the static bounds store's size call at RVA ...`.
   A failure gives `the game code could not be changed (details in TweaksV.log).`
   Made: `The static bounds store's size call at RVA ... is redirected: the store gets at least 16000 slots (STATICBOUNDS_SIZE), more where the game gives it more.`
4. The budget.
   The raise spends the pool memory budget, a slot's 17 bytes, which only HeapAdjuster raises, and past it the game writes through a null pointer with nothing on screen (PATTERNS.md, "A pool's memory").
   So a size above the key's default while that budget is not raised is one problem of the start-up summary, the raise made all the same: `The static bounds pool may not fit the pool memory budget this session: STATICBOUNDS_SIZE=32767 is above its default of 16000, and the HeapAdjuster part, which raises that budget, is off. A pool past the budget crashes the game: switch the part on, or lower STATICBOUNDS_SIZE.`, for a part off by the key or a plugin found, or `..., and POOL_MEMORY=136 leaves that budget at the game's own. A pool past the budget crashes the game: raise POOL_MEMORY, or lower STATICBOUNDS_SIZE.` At the default nothing is said, since a box at every start would say little, nor in a dry run or with nothing redirected.
5. The check, from the session's start, with the redirect made: the raise holds only if the game asks after TweaksV's `DllMain`, about 15.5 s in on 1.0.1158.16, and nothing else would say when it does not.
   The clock starts with the session, the game placing `blip_controller`, which the script programs' hook sees (section 13.3), told by its hash as the Blips part reads it, the program found readable first.
   The first program of all comes about 24 seconds earlier in the one boot that logged both.
   The part uses that hook (`Scripts::AddUser`, its `before` alone): while the Blips part is on it joins the hook Blips has made, and while `TweaksV.ini` switches Blips off it makes the hook itself, under its own tag.
   While a plugin that does Blips' job is installed, `BlipLimitFix.asi` or `ResourceExtensions.asi`, whatever `[BLIPS] ENABLED` says (`IsPluginInstalledFor`, section 7), the part makes no hook and no thread, logged: `The static bounds check and its read-back are not made this session: they start with the session, which TweaksV sees through the script programs' hook, and a plugin that does the Blips part's job on the script programs is installed (named above) - TweaksV leaves the script programs to it.`
   A check that cannot be made otherwise is reported, the redirect staying: `The check that the game asks for the static bounds store's size is not done this session: <why>`, the reason `its events could not be made.`, the hook's own (`Y-E-H1 was not found.`, `the natives' resolution call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already).`), or `its thread could not be started (details in TweaksV.log).`
   Made, a thread of its own (`StartModuleThread`, `static bounds check`) is announced: `The static bounds check waits for the session's start - the game placing blip_controller - and then 60 seconds for the game to ask for the store's size.`
   It waits for the session's start, then until the game's first asking sets its event or 60 seconds (`kCheckSeconds`), and if the game has not asked reports `the game did not ask for the store's size within 60 seconds of the session's start - it asked before TweaksV started, or makes the store another way, and the store keeps the game's size.`, in a box of its own after the start-up summary (section 6.4).
   A session that never starts leaves it waiting.
   The asking and the check's end take one atomic state, so the two cannot both win; an asking after the report is said once: `The game's lookup of the static bounds store's size came after all, after the check had reported it missing.`
   With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`: `DIAGNOSTIC: the session started 31250 ms after the part's start (blip_controller placed): the static bounds check's clock runs.`
   A test build takes the check's time, 1..60 seconds, from `TWEAKSV_TEST_STATICBOUNDS_CHECK`, and does without the event `TWEAKSV_TEST_STATICBOUNDS_NO_EVENT` names, `asked` or `session`, each said once.
6. The read-back, on every boot with a check, once, by that thread, when the game has asked, a second on (`kSettleMs`), since the init runs on: the store is copied through `CopyMemorySafely`, and a store whose +0x98 is not set yet is a line, no more.
   `The static bounds store's read-back: count 16000, pool 16000, name map 4621 buckets with 10406 names, streamer 16000 (its word 16000), 10406 slots in use, its streaming base 123456.`
   Count, pool and streamer differing is a problem: `The static bounds store's size is not raised this session: the store's count 16000, its pool 8000 and its streamer 16000 differ, and the load would end at the smallest (another mod may have changed one).`
   More than 32767 in use is one, which only a `gameconfig.xml` above that can bring: `The static bounds store holds 32768 slots in use this session, past the 32767 whose every slot is sound: gameconfig.xml gives it 40000, and an interior whose slot lies past them can lose its collision.` A `gameconfig.xml` above 32767 is otherwise kept, as any size above the key.

Every failure of the job is `The static bounds store's size is not raised this session: <why>`, a problem of the start-up summary, after it in a box of its own when it comes later.

**The redirect** (`StoreSize`) runs on the game's thread for every store of that init, 10 of 0x7740's and three others, and the lookup goes first, with the game's three arguments; a hash other than 0x2CDFE406 goes back as it came.
For the store's, a size from 0 up to below the key becomes the key, its first asking ends the check, and the sizes are said once, before the game goes on: `The size the game gives the static bounds store, 12000, is raised to 16000 (STATICBOUNDS_SIZE).` or `..., 24500, is kept: it is not below STATICBOUNDS_SIZE=16000.` With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, `DIAGNOSTIC: the game's lookup of the static bounds store's size came 15500 ms after the part's start.` A size below 0 is left and reported once, `the game gives the store the size -2, which is none - left as the game gave it.` Nothing throws out of it, and the last error is the game's.

The old plugins are `StaticBoundLimitAdjuster.asi` and `StaticBoundsLimitAdjuster.asi`, the original's two names (section 4.3).

### 11.16 The parts measured first (`kMeasuredFirst`)

A part to come lifts a limit of the game - SirenSettings the sirens per vehicle, and the lighting capacity, the two model budgets, the ped pool and the render queues if their numbers ask (ROADMAP.md) - and goes in only once a mod load reaches it, and the same part's siren setting IDs are measured with their job off too, for a start with it and one without.
So the limits are measured, each by a module of its part, `TweaksV<Limit>.cpp` and `.h`, with its header comment, its part's log tag (`kTag`: `SirenSettings`, which the two modules share, or one of its own where no part stands behind it yet) and one entry function, which `kMeasuredFirst` starts with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` (section 4.3); the subsections below are in that table's order.
A module finds every pattern of its limit - its measurement's and those its part's writing half will use - as often as each must match, checks each site as the writing half will (its bytes, offsets and targets, the complete sets by a scan), logs every site in the wording of section 6.3 and starts its measurement through `StartMeasurement` (section 6.6).
It writes nothing to the game - no `WriteCode`, no `RedirectCalls`, no hook - but the siren count's, which redirects one call (11.16.2), and the lighting capacity's, which redirects two jumps and two calls.
Every read of the game's memory is checked first and bounded (section 12): `IsReadable` for the game's own data, `CopyMemorySafely` (section 9) for heap data the game may change meanwhile.
A pattern missing, a site not as expected, an address outside the game or a thread that cannot start is logged and not reported, a diagnostic aid (NOTES.md, T-DIAG), and that module measures nothing more.
Without `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` nothing is looked for, so a normal boot does what it did without them; `DRY_RUN` changes nothing in them but those two, which then redirect nothing.
They have no section in `TweaksV.ini`, no entry of `kFeatures` and no old plugin: those come with a part's writing half, which the siren IDs have (section 11.14) and the lighting capacity (section 11.17).

#### 11.16.1 Siren IDs (`MeasureSirenIdsWhileOff`)

`MeasureSirenIdsWhileOff` does nothing while `InitSirenIds` is at its job: it measures with `LARGE_SIREN_IDS=0` and with the part off, nothing written, over the game's own table and IDs of a byte, as the ped props' measurement does while that part is off (section 11.11), so that a start with the job off and one with it can be compared.
The checks and their lines below are also the part's (section 11.14), which with `DRY_RUN` and `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` measures in the same way.

A siren setting of `carcols.meta` - the lights of an emergency vehicle's sirens - and the vehicle variation that names it carry its ID in a byte on the whole load path, and a table of 256 bytes maps an ID to the setting's position in the game's array, a byte too, in which 0 means no sirens: 256 IDs and 255 settings a vehicle can use, bound silently - a pack's setting whose ID is in use is dropped, a clash within one file moves the later setting to the first free ID, an ID above 255 in a file keeps its low byte, a setting past position 255 is reached as another one (PATTERNS.md, "Siren IDs").
`MeasureSirenIdsWhileOff` looks for its 14 patterns, `I-E-P1` to `I-E-M1`, each once - every one, so that the log names each one missing, and its line the first: `DIAGNOSTIC: the siren settings are not measured: I-E-P1 was not found.` - and checks, in the order of the patterns, every site the part changes or relies on, each logged; the first that is not as the findings have it ends the checks with `DIAGNOSTIC: the siren settings are not measured: <why>.`, and nothing is measured:

- **The two parser registrations**, `I-E-P1` (the siren settings) and `I-E-P2` (the vehicle variations): the tables their two `lea` lead to - the member data pointers (rel32 at +23) and the members' offsets (+37, past the match) - inside the game and readable; the ID member and its neighbour at their offsets, member 0 at +0x00 and member 2 at +0x04, member 6 at +0x4B and member 5 at +0x4A, and the variations' 7 members, the eighth pointer null; the ID member's data inside the game, its offset field (+0x08) and its type (+0x10) read - never written - and logged, at the start and again at the first summary, since the game may register its structures after TweaksV has started; a type other than 0x01 or 0x02, which store a byte, is a finding, logged, and stops nothing here: `DIAGNOSTIC: the variation's siren setting ID member's type at RVA ... is 0x3, not 0x1 or 0x2, which store a byte - a finding for the part (I-E-P2).` Logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: siren settings' parser registration at RVA ...: name hash 0x84EB9B0D, member data table at RVA ..., offsets table at RVA ... (I-E-P1)` and `DIAGNOSTIC: variation's siren setting ID member: member 6 of 7 at +0x4B, member 5 at +0x4A; its data at RVA ..., offset field 0x4B, type 0x1 (I-E-P2)` - the offset field, +0x08 of the member's data, is what the game writes there when it registers the structure.
  The reasons: `the siren settings' members 0 and 2 are at +0x0 and +0x8, not +0x0 and +0x4 (I-E-P1).`, `the vehicle variations' parser registration has 8 or more members, not 7 (I-E-P2).`, `the siren setting's ID member's data is not inside the game: <address> (I-E-P1).`
- **The simple member's XML reader**, `I-E-P3`, which its pattern pins: `DIAGNOSTIC: simple member's XML reader at RVA ...: the member data at +0x8 of the member, its type at +0x10, types up to 0x21 (I-E-P3)`.
- **The ID table:** the `lea` of `I-E-T1` to `T4` (their rel32 at +10, +23, +10 and +16, the second and the fourth past the match), each logged as the relocation core logs a reference, `DIAGNOSTIC: reference at RVA ... -> RVA ... (I-E-T1)`; the first leads inside the game with the table's 256 bytes (`the siren setting ID table is not inside the game: <address> (another mod may have moved it already).`), the others to the same (`the references to the siren setting ID table do not all point to the game's own table: <a> and <b> (I-E-T4).`); then `DIAGNOSTIC: the siren setting ID table is at RVA ... - 256 entries of 1 byte` and `FindRipRelativeLeas`, each load logged `(lea scan)`, which must find exactly those 4: `the game code loads the siren setting ID table in 5 or more places, not the expected 4 (another mod may have moved some of them, or the game has changed).` Before `I-E-T4`'s `lea`, its carcols pointer (the rel32 at +3) inside the game: `DIAGNOSTIC: carcols pointer load at RVA ... -> RVA ... (I-E-T4)`.
- **The bytes at fixed offsets from the matches**, from a table of the module whose bytes, where they lie inside a match, are checked against its pattern when TweaksV is compiled, and outside one are read only after `IsReadable`: the nine loads of an ID as a byte (`0F B6 ...`), the two byte stores of a new ID (`40 88 31`, `40 88 33`), the four checks for none (`48 81 FD FF 00 00 00` and the like), the two IDs given when none is free (`40 B6 FF`), the two IDs stored into a variation (`88 1C 0F`, `88 14 03`), the dedupe's load and its check for none on the low byte (`80 FA FF`), the siren setting's constructor's store at +0x1C that the part replaces, its ID default `C6 06 FF` at +0xA0 and its second store at +0x1C, `89 46 1C` at +0xC9, and the stores of the variation's two constructors from 15 bytes before `I-E-C2` and `I-E-C3`, 30 and 34 bytes - each logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: <what> at RVA ...: <bytes> (<label>)` (`DIAGNOSTIC: siren setting ID load at RVA ...: 0F B6 2C 0F (I-E-T1)`); and the four free-ID searches' limits, `48 81 FE 00 01 00 00` at +0x85 and +0xCD of `I-E-T2` and at +0x81 and +0xC9 of `I-E-T4`, logged as the core logs a limit: `DIAGNOSTIC: limit at RVA ... holds 256 (N, I-E-T2) -> game size 256`.
  A site that holds other bytes: `the siren setting ID load at RVA ... holds 0F B7 2C 0F, not 0F B6 2C 0F (I-E-T1).`
- **The dedupe's compare loop**, `I-E-D2`: its `jz` (the rel32 at +18) leads into the game - `DIAGNOSTIC: pack dedupe's compare loop at RVA ..., its jz -> RVA ... (I-E-D2)`; else `the jz of the pack dedupe's compare loop at RVA ... does not lead into the game (another mod may have changed it) (I-E-D2).`
- **The vehicle metadata's init**, `I-E-R1`: its `movdqa` stores the table's last 16 bytes (the disp32 at +4 leads to the table + 0xF0), and its call at +13 leads into the game - `DIAGNOSTIC: base loader call at RVA ... -> RVA ..., after the siren setting ID table's last fill at RVA ... -> RVA ... (I-E-R1)`; else `the store before the base loader call at RVA ... fills RVA ..., not the siren setting ID table's last 16 bytes at RVA ... (I-E-R1).` or `the base loader call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already) (I-E-R1).`
- **The getter**, `I-E-M1`, which its pattern pins - a model's siren setting index at +0x53B, the settings at +0x48 of the carcols data, 0x5E8 bytes each, the last two checked against the module's constants when TweaksV is compiled - and its carcols pointer, the rel32 at +15, which must be `I-E-T4`'s: `DIAGNOSTIC: siren settings getter at RVA ...: a model's siren setting index at +0x53B, the settings at +0x48 of the carcols data, 0x5E8 bytes each; the carcols pointer at RVA ... (I-E-M1)`; else `the carcols pointer the siren settings getter reads, <a>, is not the one a pack's builder reads, <b> (I-E-M1).`

Everything checked, the measurement is announced - `DIAGNOSTIC: the siren settings, through the carcols pointer at RVA ... (I-E-M1), and the siren setting ID table at RVA ..., 256 IDs (I-E-T1 to I-E-T4): the settings loaded and the IDs in use are logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while the numbers change.` - and started (section 6.6, thread `siren settings summary`).
At its first summary the two ID members are logged once more, before the summary, `DIAGNOSTIC: variation's siren setting ID member, at the first summary: its data at RVA ..., offset field 0x4B, type 0x1 (I-E-P2)` - a type other than a byte's with its line again, and data that cannot be read then with `... at the first summary: its data at RVA ... cannot be read (I-E-P2)`.
It reads without a lock and writes nothing: the carcols pointer after `IsReadable` - while it is null, `DIAGNOSTIC: no siren settings loaded yet - the game has not read carcols.ymt.`; the settings' array, count and capacity at +0x48 of the carcols data through `CopyMemorySafely`, a count above the capacity taken for data the game was changing; each setting's ID, a byte at its +0x00 or, once the part has written, a word, through `CopyMemorySafely`, into two bitmaps of 65,536 bits of its own, nothing allocated; the array and the count read again, since the array grows while packs load; then the ID table after `IsReadable`, its entries that are not 0xFF, as the game tells a free ID.
A read that fails, or settings that changed meanwhile: `DIAGNOSTIC: the siren settings at <address> could not be read this time (the game may have been changing them).`, and they are counted at the next.
The summary: `DIAGNOSTIC: siren settings loaded: 24 (vehicles can use 255 at most); siren setting IDs in use: 24 of 256; settings past position 255, which no vehicle can reach: 0; IDs given to more than one setting: 0.` - the settings past position 255 being those beyond the 256 positions an index reaches, the IDs given to more than one setting those that two or more settings carry (with IDs of a byte, 255 when the table is full).
Its two bitmaps are of 65,536 bits, a setting's ID read as a word once the part has written, a byte before; the numbers the thread compares are a hash of all it says, the IDs listed among them, so a summary comes when any of them changes.
With the part at work the summary's line is that of section 11.14.
After each summary the IDs themselves, in their settings' order, at most the first 1,024 settings, 128 a line - `DIAGNOSTIC: siren setting IDs of settings 0 to 23 of 24: 1, 2, 3, ...` - which is what a start with `LARGE_SIREN_IDS=0` and one with `=1` compare, the base game's coming first.

#### 11.16.2 Siren count (`MeasureSirenCount`)

The game lights 20 sirens of a vehicle, `siren1` to `siren20`: its renderer runs over 20 lights, and a model's bone structure names the bones of `siren1` to `siren20` and of their glass by signed bytes, so a model made for more sirens keeps the rest dark and a bone past index 127 cannot be named at all (PATTERNS.md, "Siren count").
With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` the module looks for `I-E-B1` to `I-E-B4`, once each - each one missing logged by the scanner, the first of them named: `DIAGNOSTIC: the siren count is not measured: I-E-B1 was not found.` - and checks them; every line starts with `DIAGNOSTIC: the siren count is not measured: ` when a check fails.
The call at +13 of `I-E-B1`, the renderer's, and the one at +24, the siren settings getter's, whose rel32 lies past the match and is read after `IsReadable`, must lead into the game (`the siren lights' renderer call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already) (I-E-B1).`, and the same of `the siren settings getter call`), logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: siren lights' renderer call at RVA ... -> RVA ..., siren settings getter call at RVA ... -> RVA ... (I-E-B1)`; `I-E-B2` must lie in the function the renderer's call leads to by the game's unwind data (`RtlLookupFunctionEntry`; `I-E-B2 at RVA ... does not lie in the siren lights' renderer at RVA ...: the game's unwind data puts it in the function at RVA ....`, or `... names no function there.`), logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: bone structure offset at RVA ... holds 0xB0, siren1's bone offset at RVA ... holds 0x99, in the function at RVA ... (unwind data) (I-E-B2)`; `I-E-B3` is logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: siren_glass1's bone offset at RVA ... holds 0xAD (I-E-B3)`; and `I-E-B4`'s match must be a function start by the game's unwind data, as the wrapper calls it - not by the byte before it, which `IsFunctionStart` (section 9) takes for code without unwind data (`find-bone-by-name at RVA ... is not a function start (inside the function at RVA ... (unwind data)) (I-E-B4).`, or `(no unwind data)`), logged as `DIAGNOSTIC: find-bone-by-name at RVA ..., a function start (unwind data), bone count offset at RVA ... holds 0x5E (I-E-B4)`.
The four offsets the module reads by - 0xB0, 0x99, 0xAD and 0x5E - are checked against the bytes their patterns pin when TweaksV is compiled (`PatternByte`).
With `DRY_RUN` the module logs the line that would redirect the call, `DRY_RUN: would redirect the siren lights' renderer call at RVA ....`, then `... not measured: DRY_RUN - the siren lights' renderer call is not redirected, so nothing is counted.`, and stops.
Otherwise it makes the measurement's event, starts its thread (section 6.6, `siren count summary`; `its summary's event could not be made (...).`, `its summary thread could not be started.`) and only then redirects the call (`RedirectCalls`, through a read+execute stub; a call that cannot be redirected - Windows' dynamic code policy, for one - is logged by the patch module and `the siren lights' renderer call at RVA ... could not be redirected (details above).`), so that a measurement that cannot sum up redirects nothing; after a failed redirect the thread waits for a first model that never comes.
Nothing of it is reported - the module's lines are under `[SirenSettings]`, the scanner's under `[Scanner]`, a thread that cannot start under `[TweaksV]` (section 6.6) - no problem, no box.
A call redirected is announced: `DIAGNOSTIC: the siren lights' renderer call at RVA ... is redirected (I-E-B1): the vehicle models with sirens on, each counted once with its siren bones looked up by name up to siren32, are logged 10 seconds after the first, then every minute while they change.`

The wrapper, `CountedSirenLights`, takes the renderer's nine arguments - the three floats as floats, the stack's five as 64-bit values, so that each slot reaches the renderer as the game's call left it - calls the game's renderer first and returns its rax, then counts, on the game's thread, with no lock, no allocation and nothing that can throw: one lookup of the model info in a fixed table of 1,024 slots, from a Fibonacci hash of its address over 32 slots at most.
A free slot is claimed by compare-exchange, so a new model drawn on two threads at once is counted once, and a model that finds neither its slot nor a free one among the 32 counts its pass as not held.
On a model's first pass, with the caller's last error kept around it: the hash of its name at +0x18; its bone structure through the two pointers from +0xB0 and the structure's 20 siren and 20 glass bytes; the skeleton data, the skeleton's first field, and its bone count at +0x5E - every pointer through `IsReadable`, the first that cannot be read making it a model that could not be read - then, unless the bone count is 0, 64 calls of the game's find-bone-by-name, `siren1` to `siren32` and `siren_glass1` to `siren_glass32` (names made when TweaksV is compiled), each guarded as `CopyMemorySafely` guards a read, since no pattern pins what the renderer's 7th argument holds: an access violation in it is caught, and the model is one that could not be read, looked at no more; and for each bone found among the first 20 its index at +0x42 - one its bone structure does not name counts as lost past bone 127 when that index is past 127, and below 128 as a disagreement of its own.
The first model counted sets the event.
The summary (section 6.6), 10 seconds after it and then every minute while the models counted change, walks the table: `DIAGNOSTIC: 4 vehicle models with sirens seen in 800 passes of the siren lights' renderer - the most sirens a model's bone structure names: 20 of 20 (model 0x79FBB0C5); 1 with siren bones past siren20, which the game never lights; 1 losing siren bones past bone 127, which a bone structure cannot name; 0 that could not be read.` - the model of the most sirens the one with the lower hash of two, `none read` in its place when no model could be read, and `; the table full, N passes of models it could not hold not counted` before the end when there are such passes.
Then a line, once, for each model the limit binds, that could not be read, or whose bones by name and bone structure disagree - which says the measurement is not right for it - its numbers in pairs, the sirens' first: `DIAGNOSTIC: vehicle model 0xA1B2C3D4, 100 bones - siren and glass bones by name: 32 and 32, up to siren32 and siren_glass32; named by its bone structure: 20 and 20; past siren20, never lit: 12 and 12.`, with `; lost past bone 127, found by name but not named by its bone structure: 2 and 0 (the highest bone 129)`, `; found by name below bone 128 but not named by its bone structure: 1 and 0`, `; named by its bone structure but not found by name: 0 and 1` and `; found by name at another bone than its bone structure names: 1 and 0` where they apply; `DIAGNOSTIC: vehicle model 0x0BAD0001 could not be read: its bone structure (the model info's +0xB0).`, `... its skeleton data (the renderer's 7th argument, its first field).` or `... find-bone-by-name faulted on its skeleton data (an access violation).` The lines per model are bounded by the table's 1,024 slots, below `kMaxLinesPerKind` (a `static_assert`), so they need no cap.
With the raise the wrapper is the raise's, `SirenLightsTwice`, and the call redirected once; the module checks and logs its sites as ever, starts its measurement and says the same announcement.
The summary's words change: `; 6 with siren bones past siren20, 6 lit past siren20 by the second call; ...`, a second line follows, `DIAGNOSTIC: the sirens per vehicle at 32: 8 siren settings with lights past 20 in the table of 255, 1 twins dropped, 1 replaced by a later one with the same 20 lights, 0 that did not fit or could not be read; 1 models that changed the scalars of a setting another model shares; 1206 second calls of the renderer.`, and a model's line says `; past siren20: 12 and 12; lit past siren20 by the second call: 12 bones, the 12 lights past 20 of a setting of its own.`, or `; not lit past siren20: <why>.` A refused raise leaves the measurement as it was.

#### 11.16.3 Lighting capacity (`MeasureLightCapacity`)

A frame's lights pass through layers of fixed room, and many vehicles with real siren lights are where a load may reach them (PATTERNS.md, "LightCapacity"): a job's list of 80 lights and 200 coronas for 5 vehicles, silent past it; the frame's stores of 400 and 928, unchecked, over their own write pointers; 300 vehicles a frame, unchecked; the scene store's 1024, whose byte index replaces a wrong slot; the ray traced list's 1024 and its upload's 512; the reflection lists' 768; the corona store's 1088.
Its tag is `LightCapacity`.
It looks for all 22 patterns, those its part writes through with them, unless the part has done so before it, which hands over what it found and looks for nothing again (section 11.17), `G-E-G1` exactly twice through `Scanner::FindAll` and the others through `FindOnce`, each one missing logged by the scanner and the first of them named: `DIAGNOSTIC: the lighting capacity is not measured: G-E-A1 was not found.`, the same words for each failure below.
The constants it counts by are compared with the bytes their patterns pin when TweaksV is compiled.
Then it checks and logs, the first failure ending it with nothing written: `G-E-A1`'s stores and write pointers inside the game, the pointers aligned and readable, the lights' store 928 coronas past the coronas', the coronas' pointer 400 lights past the lights' store and the lights' pointer right behind it (`the vehicle lights' store at RVA ... does not lie 928 coronas past the vehicle coronas' at RVA ... (G-E-A1).`); `G-E-A2` and `G-E-A3` naming the same; the drain's call past `G-E-A3`'s match read after `IsReadable` and leading into the game; each store's references by `Scanner::FindRipRelativeReferences`, the reset's, the drain's and the constructor's, logged (`DIAGNOSTIC: the vehicle lights' store at RVA ... is referenced in 3 places of the game's code: RVA ..., RVA ... and RVA ...`), a fourth ending it (`the game's code references the vehicle lights' store at RVA ... in 4 places or more, not 3 - the frame's reset, the drain and the constructor, as the research found them.`) and fewer than three refusing nothing, a fact for the boot: the scan sees a `lea` or a store of a pointer, and the findings give the constructor's two by their addresses, not their instructions; `G-E-B1`'s count, its disp32 counted from past the instruction's imm32; the jump of `G-E-C1` leading into the game, to the 12 bytes of the function's way out (`..., which holds ..., not the function's way out (G-E-C1).`), with where the unwind data puts the check as a fact for the boot; `G-E-C4`'s start values 0, 0, 200, 80; `G-E-D1`'s count, the sort list's count right behind its 1,024 entries, read and stored at one address, its mark the byte at +4; `G-E-E1`'s and `G-E-E2`'s one store, each of `G-E-E1`'s five other addresses inside the game, its last past the match, and both sets of counts; `G-E-E3`'s lists and `G-E-F1`'s one pointer; `G-E-F2`'s call past the match; `G-E-G1`'s one count.
A limit is logged in section 6.3's form: `DIAGNOSTIC: the scene store's limit at RVA ... holds 1023 (N-1, G-E-D1) -> game size 1024: its count at RVA ...`.
Each value the measurement reads is inside the game, aligned to its size and readable when it starts, and read plainly from then on.
Beside those it looks for `G-E-K1` and `G-E-K2`, which nothing else does, and checks them and the jump at +18 of `G-E-F1`: that jump's rel32 past the match readable and leading into the game, to the 14 bytes of its function's way out; the two calls' rel32 past their matches readable and leading into the game, to one function.
Each is logged (`DIAGNOSTIC: the jump when the ray traced list is full at RVA ... (G-E-F1) -> RVA ...: 48 83 C4 28 5B 5F 5E 41 5C 41 5E 41 5F C3`, `DIAGNOSTIC: a job's coronas' copy at RVA ...: its call at RVA ... -> RVA ... (G-E-K1)`).
One not as the findings have it costs its own number and a line, no more: `DIAGNOSTIC: the ray traced list's refused lights are not counted: the jump when the ray traced list is full, at RVA ..., leads to RVA ..., which holds ..., not the function's way out (G-E-F1).`, `DIAGNOSTIC: a job's most is not counted: G-E-K1 was not found.`, `DIAGNOSTIC: a job's most is not counted: a job's two copies call different functions: RVA ... and RVA ... (G-E-K2).`
A write pointer not 0 at the start means the game's frames have begun, as RayTracing's buffer made already does (section 11.13): its threads may run the jumps and a rel32 being written can be read half written, so no stub, `DIAGNOSTIC: the counting stubs are not made: the game's frames run already - its threads may run their sites while they are rewritten.`, and the sampling goes on.
With `DRY_RUN`: `DRY_RUN: would redirect the jump at RVA ... (G-E-C1) to a counting stub - a job's refused lights are not counted.`, `DRY_RUN: would redirect the jump at RVA ... (G-E-F1) to a counting stub - the ray traced list's refused lights are not counted.`, `DRY_RUN: would redirect the calls at RVA ... (G-E-K1) and RVA ... (G-E-K2) to stubs - a job's most is not counted.`, and the sampling goes on.
Otherwise the stubs go on the first page of a block of two near the game (`AllocateNearGame`), each in a room of 64 bytes, their counters on the second, read/write.
A jump's, 22 bytes: `lock inc qword [rip+disp32]`, `jmp [rip+0]` and the way out's address; no register and no stack, and only the flags change, which the way out's first instruction writes anew before anything reads them.
A copy's, 37 bytes: `mov rax, [rip+disp32]`, `cmp r8, rax`, `jbe` on, `lock cmpxchg [rip+disp32], r8`, `jne` back to the compare, on: `jmp [rip+0]` and the copy's address; rax is free at that call (PATTERNS.md), the arguments and rsp stay, and the mark is exact however many jobs end at once, at most 120 times a frame.
The first page is read+execute once written; stubs that cannot be made are logged, `DIAGNOSTIC: the counting stubs are not made: they could not be made read+execute (...).`, and the sampling goes on (`no memory near the game could be had for them.`, `the <what>'s stub is out of reach of it, at RVA ....`, `the game code could not be changed (details above).`).
The summary's thread starts, then the sampling thread, then one `WriteCode` of the two jumps' 6 bytes and the two calls' 5, in `DllMain`, where no other thread runs them; a summary thread that cannot start redirects nothing (`... not measured: its summary thread could not be started.`), and a sampling thread that cannot start is a line, `DIAGNOSTIC: the lighting capacity's numbers are not sampled: their thread could not be started.`, the summary saying `its numbers are not sampled (see above)`.
The lines: `DIAGNOSTIC: jump when a job's list is full at RVA ...: 0F 83 D0 01 00 00 -> 0F 83 ...`, `DIAGNOSTIC: jump when the ray traced list is full at RVA ...: 0F 8F 33 02 00 00 -> 0F 8F ...`, `DIAGNOSTIC: call of a job's coronas' copy at RVA ...: E8 51 44 66 01 -> E8 ...`, `DIAGNOSTIC: call of a job's lights' copy at RVA ...: E8 29 44 66 01 -> E8 ...`, `DIAGNOSTIC: the jump at RVA ... (G-E-C1) is redirected to a counting stub: every light a job's full list refuses is counted.`, `DIAGNOSTIC: the jump at RVA ... (G-E-F1) is redirected to a counting stub: every light the full ray traced list refuses is counted.`, `DIAGNOSTIC: the calls at RVA ... (G-E-K1) and RVA ... (G-E-K2) are redirected to stubs: a job's most coronas and lights are kept.`, and last `DIAGNOSTIC: the lighting capacity is measured: its numbers are sampled every 10 ms and logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while they change.`, or with no sampling thread `DIAGNOSTIC: the lighting capacity is measured, its numbers not sampled: the rest is logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while it changes.`
The sampling thread, every 10 ms, which sees every frame up to 100 fps: the vehicles' count; each store's write pointer, its number the distance from the store, 0 meaning the reset has not run; the larger of the scene store's render count and count; the larger of the two ray traced lists' counts; the largest of the six reflection lists' counts; the corona store's count; whether the store is at its room with the sort list marked; each number's most kept since the last reading.
A pointer outside its store and a number past its room are a line at once, once each, and no mark, no box (D30): `DIAGNOSTIC: vehicle lights' write pointer RVA ... is outside its store - the game has overwritten memory.`, `DIAGNOSTIC: vehicles seen at 301, past the 300 the game has room for - the game has overwritten memory.`, `DIAGNOSTIC: scene lights seen at 1025, past the 1024 the game has room for.`
At the first light refused, once: `DIAGNOSTIC: the first light a job's list refused - the list is full, and the job's later vehicles lose lights in that frame.`
The summary (section 6.6), 10 seconds in and then every minute with something new, a most other than the last, lights refused, the store marked at its room or the upload passed where they were not, the settings read anew: the first `DIAGNOSTIC: the most so far: vehicles 87 of 300, vehicle lights 143 of 400, vehicle coronas 512 of 928, scene lights 811 of 1024 (at its room with the sort list marked in 0 of 38211 samples), ray traced list 640 of 1024 (past 512 in 5120 samples, by 128 at most, 0 refused), reflection lists 212 of 768, coronas 900 of 1088, a job's most 78 of 80 lights and 150 of 200 coronas, a job's refused lights 0; siren settings 33, real lights in 12, up to 20 lights.`; each later one first the same numbers of the minute alone, `DIAGNOSTIC: the most this minute: ...`, without the settings, which is what a scene reached.
`at its room with the sort list marked` counts the samples with the store at its 1024 and the byte at +4 of the sort list's count not 0: past the 1024 a nearer light takes a wrong slot and a farther one is dropped.
Who sets that byte, and when, the findings do not say, so it may mark a list sorted or one that needs a sort: the line is true on either reading, and my boot reads what it shows.
The refusals say `are not counted (DRY_RUN)` or `(see above)` where they are not.
The ray traced list's `refused` and a job's most stand only where their stubs were made; a job's most is what its stub kept, in bytes, taken by `xchg` at each reading, so the minute's and the session's stand apart.
`The coronas' room is `CoronaStoreNow().room`, read when the measurement starts, and with `.counted` their number reads `coronas 1088 of 4096, refused 0 (LOD's own 1088: 0), LOD lights 640`, the refusals the minute's and the session's, the LOD lights' batch sampled as a most.
`With the part's one write done the numbers go over the moved stores and lists, `vehicle lights 143 of 10800, vehicle coronas 512 of 18000`, `a job's most 78 of 180 lights and 150 of 300 coronas`, and a pointer outside one of those stores is the line above; with the part off, refused or in a `DRY_RUN` they go over the game's own.
The settings come through `SirenSettingsNow()`, each through `CopyMemorySafely`, again if the array or the count changed meanwhile, the last good numbers kept: how many, how many with the byte at +0x38 set, and among those the most lights with the flag at +0x45; `siren settings not read yet` before any.
Nothing of it is reported: an aid (section 12).

#### 11.16.4 The model budgets (`MeasurePopulation`)

The game keeps a budget in bytes for the vehicle models and one for the ped models it holds in memory: an entry of a table by a level, times the Population Variety's multiplier in story mode, an entry alone in a network game (PATTERNS.md, "Population").
Short of it by a margin the game asks for no further model and releases what nothing uses, so a load with many models repeats those it holds, without an error.
One module measures both, since they share an object, a set-up and the crash context's line that leads to them, under its own tag, `Population`, and it writes nothing.
It looks for its 16 patterns, `O-E-C1`, `O-E-V1` to `V7` and `O-E-P1` to `P8`, each once - every one, so that the log names each one missing, and its line the first: `DIAGNOSTIC: the model budgets are not measured: O-E-C1 was not found.` - and checks that what it reads past a match can be read (`the code past O-E-P2's match at RVA ... cannot be read.`); then every site, each logged, in this order, the first that is not as the findings have it ending the checks in the same line, and nothing is measured:
- **`O-E-C1`, the crash context's line:** its text, which must read `Ped Memory Use/Budget : %d/%d\n`, the object inside the game with its 0x62C0 bytes, aligned; and at each call's target a getter, 62 bytes checked against the module's mask, since the two are the same bytes and no pattern can tell them: its network table right behind its story table, its level, flag, tables and variety inside the game, aligned and readable, both reading one flag.
- **`O-E-V1`, `V2`:** the check before a vehicle model's request reads what the vehicle getter reads, and its request is a call into the game.
- **`O-E-V3`, `V5`:** `VehicleStruct`'s pointer, and the ambient cars' count, whose read lies right before `O-E-C1`.
- **`O-E-V4`, `V6`, the writing half's check W1:** the one call leads to the set-up, a function start 7 bytes before `O-E-V4`, the only call of it (`FindCallsTo`), and `O-E-P5` to `P7` and `O-E-V7` lie in its blocks of 0x38 bytes.
- **`O-E-V7`, `O-E-P5` to `P7`, check W2:** each store leads to its entry of the story table.
- **`O-E-P1`, `P2`, `P3`, `P4`, `P8`:** the peds' sum stores the display `O-E-C1` reads; the gate reads the getter's level, holds the population's list at +0x7A and the floor at +0xEA, and calls into the game at +0x102; the model count, the model info table and its stride inside the game; the variety's store is the getter's, and its factor, sum and most are logged as they read, `the setting x 5.00 + 1.00, at most 10.00`.
- **Check W3:** the scanner's search for stores of any width into each story table (section 8) is a line, `DIAGNOSTIC: writers of the vehicle story table at RVA ...: the set-up's stores at RVA ..., RVA ... and RVA ..., none besides them (O-E-V7)`, `..., 1 besides them: RVA ... (O-E-V7)`, and a writer besides them stops nothing: it could undo a raise, not a measurement.
  The search skips what it cannot read, so that one which misses a store the patterns pin proves nothing, and says so: `...: the scan finds 2 of the set-up's 3 stores, not the one at RVA ..., so it proves nothing about other writers (O-E-V7)`.

The measurement is started (section 6.6, threads `population summary` and `population sampling`).
A sample reads the game's data after `IsReadable` and aligned, the pool and the model infos through `CopyMemorySafely`, nothing allocated, one lock around it: it is nothing while the object's +0x6208 is 0, the game's init not run, the tables holding `.data`'s other numbers, or while one of the nine lists it counts holds nothing but zeros, which would count 256 entries of model 0 in highs nothing lowers, a laid-out list holding an entry not 0 whether or not it starts with model 0; and the fill counts from a second sample running that finds both, half a sampling period or more after the first, since the research gives the order of none of the init's stores; a level above 3 makes no sample of that budget.
Each entry of the population's list is read once, with its end test, since the game may release a model meanwhile.
The budget comes as the game's getter has it without its doubling, the cast's overflow included; the stop is the budget less 1.5 MB for the vehicles, less 1.5 MB + (n >> 1) x 1.5 MB for the peds, n the override +0x6208 or else +0x6204, and wraps as the game's add does.
The numbers of a summary are a hash of the highs in whole MB or as counted, the samples at the stop, the level and the tables, so that a summary comes when one moves, a budget that binds so bringing one a minute, and a use that only falls none.
Each budget's line ends with `; no new high for N s`, the seconds since one of its highs last moved, or since its first sample - the use in whole MB and the models, the pool's and the ambient cars' for the vehicles, the peds of a model and the streamed for the peds - and those last words are what the proof's drive reads: it goes on while N stays small.
So that the last line says it where no budget binds and nothing else brings one, N's passing two minutes is among the summary's numbers, one more line for each budget.
The lines, on the boot's numbers of 2026-11-02, `DIAGNOSTIC: the model budgets are not filled yet - the game has not started its population.` before the fill and `DIAGNOSTIC: the model budgets at RVA ... could not be read this time.` for a read that fails:
```
DIAGNOSTIC: vehicle model budget's tables: story 1500000, 33500000, 57500000 and 71500000 bytes, network 1500000, 33500000, 57500000 and 71500000 bytes; the configuration's story Level1Kb to Level3Kb: 32000, 56000 and 70000, the game's defaults - the story entries are theirs.
DIAGNOSTIC: vehicle model budget: 428.7 of 429.0 MB in use at level 3 (the level's 71.5 MB x variety 6.00), the requests stop at 427.5 MB, over the budget by 0.0 MB, at most 428.7 MB so far; vehicle models loaded: 94 for traffic, 0 outside the pop cycle, 0 trains and helicopters, 0 boats, 8 dropped, 0 in flight, at most 102 at once, about 4.1 MB each; at the stop in 170 of 171 samples; VehicleStruct 102 of 200 slots, at most 102 so far, unused models released past 180; of the bytes: weapons 10.1 MB, mods 0.0 and 0.0 MB; ambient cars on the map 57, at most 57 so far; no new high for 95 s.
DIAGNOSTIC: ped model budget: 305.1 of 561.0 MB in use at level 3 (the level's 93.5 MB x variety 6.00), the requests stop at 553.5 MB, over the budget by 0.0 MB, at most 305.1 MB so far; ped models loaded: 19 for the population, 1 of them streamed, 5 outside the schedule, 4 in no group, 3 asked for directly, at most 31 at once, about 9.8 MB each; at the stop in 0 of 171 samples; at most 9 peds of one model; at most 1 streamed at once; no new high for 32 s.
```
The tables' line comes at the first summary after the fill and whenever they change, and is the only proof that a raise in `gameconfig.xml` has taken: `..., not the defaults of 1.0.1158.16, 32000, 56000 and 70000 - the story entries are theirs: gameconfig.xml, or a game update, has changed them.`, the defaults being the module's own, which no pattern pins, and for an entry the configuration does not give `... - level 3's entry holds 120000000 bytes, not the 71500000 they give: a tunable or another mod has changed it, or the game's set-up has not run yet.`; `; the configuration at RVA ... could not be read this time.` when it cannot be read, and then the line comes again at the next summary, one a minute, until it could.
A network game's lines say `(the network table's 72.0 MB, no variety)`; a budget the cast overflows, `(the level's 400.0 MB x variety 6.00, which the game's cast to int overflows: nothing stops its requests)`, with no stop; a level above 3 at every sample, `DIAGNOSTIC: vehicle model budget: no sample yet - its level reads 7, above the game's 3.`; once each, `DIAGNOSTIC: vehicle model budget: the variety has been 10.00, above the 6.00 of the game's slider - settings.xml or another mod has changed it.`; a lowest level below the one now, `; at level 1 at the lowest so far`; a model info that fails, `; 2 model infos could not be read this time`; `VehicleStruct not made yet` or `VehicleStruct could not be read this time`.
The measurement is announced once its threads are started, `DIAGNOSTIC: the model budgets, in the population object at RVA ... (O-E-C1): sampled every second, nothing written; ...`, and a sampling thread that cannot start leaves a sample a summary: `...: sampled once a summary alone, their sampling thread not started, nothing written; ...`.

#### 11.16.5 Ped pool (`MeasurePedPool`)

The game keeps its peds in one pool of 256 slots of 6,528 bytes, sized by `Peds` in `gameconfig.xml`, and a full pool is graceful; raising the number alone is a trap, since three of the game's arrays stay at 256 with no check (PATTERNS.md, "Ped pool").
With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` the module, tag `PedPool`, looks for its 18 patterns, `N-E-A1` to `N-E-D6`, and for `H-E2` through its part (section 11.1), each once - every one, so that the log names each one missing, and its line the first: `DIAGNOSTIC: the ped pool is not measured: N-E-A1 was not found.`
Then it checks, in that order, each logged, the first thing not as the findings have it ending the checks with `DIAGNOSTIC: the ped pool is not measured: <why>.`: the accessor's flag, value and keys inside the game, aligned and readable (`N-E-A1`), and likewise every field below; the pools' proxy inside the game (`N-E-M1`); the pool's lookup call into the game (`N-E-M2`), and `N-E-C1`'s and `N-E-I1`'s to the same function; the setter's call into the game and its bytes, `89 0D`, a disp32 and `C3`, which give the number (`N-E-C1`); the spatial array's store `48 89 1D` at +63, past the match, and its pointer (`N-E-S1`); the budget's site, which only says what it holds; the four counts and three caps (`N-E-K1`, `N-E-K2`); the copy's two getter calls into the game, to one function (`N-E-R1`); the timeslice list's count (`N-E-R2`), its call to the filler `N-E-R3` matches 5 bytes into, and the filler's call into the game; the priority function's restore `48 81 C4 48 10 00 00` at +0x308 and its pointer, which must be `N-E-S1`'s (`N-E-R4`); the six defaults, which their patterns pin.
What the code reads by - the decode's rotation and addend, the stride 0x1980, every instruction it takes an operand from - is checked against the bytes its pattern pins when TweaksV is compiled.
No complete set is scanned: the findings' section 7 lists none, and no read of it depends on one.
It writes nothing, and `DRY_RUN` changes nothing in it.
Everything checked, its measurement is announced - `DIAGNOSTIC: the ped pool is measured, through its pointer at RVA ... (N-E-A1): the slots in use and the spatial array are sampled every second and logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while they change.` - and started (section 6.6, thread `ped pool summary`), with `ped pool sampling` beside it, which keeps the most slots in use and the most in the spatial array; a sampling thread that cannot start is a line, the most are then the summaries' own, and the announcement says so in its second wording: `DIAGNOSTIC: the ped pool is measured, through its pointer at RVA ... (N-E-A1), its numbers not sampled: the slots in use and the spatial array are logged after 10 seconds, then every minute while they change.`
It reads without a lock: the flag, value and keys after `IsReadable`, through `DecodePoolPointer`; the pool's head and the spatial array's through `CopyMemorySafely`; the number, the counts and the caps after `IsReadable`.
The summary, a walk of the slots added for it alone: `DIAGNOSTIC: ped pool: 143 of 256 slots in use, at most 171 so far; mission 12, reuse 4; scenario 40 on foot and 2 in cars, cap 40; ambient 61 on foot and 18 in cars, caps 64 and 16 (which is which a hint); spatial array 139 of 256, at most 165.`, with ` - the pool has been full: the game makes no more peds until one goes` and ` - past the 256 of the game's fixed arrays: expect memory corruption` before its end where they apply - lines of the log, no box.
The first time the sampling thread sees the spatial array past 256 it says so at once, as what follows may end the game before the next summary: `DIAGNOSTIC: the ped pool's spatial array holds 300 of 300 - past the 256 of the game's fixed arrays: expect memory corruption.`, a line of the log, no box.
A reading a second can still miss it.
The ambient caps stand in the counts' order, on foot and then in cars, by the research's hint of which store is which, so that the line says it is one.
The walk copies the flag bytes, the pool's own count of them, into a fixed buffer of 16,384, and of each live slot reads the ped's three bytes through `CopyMemorySafely`, a ped with +0x1091 bit 4 not counted, as every loop of the game skips it; a read that fails gives `mission and reuse not counted this time`.
The other lines: `DIAGNOSTIC: no ped pool yet - the game has not made it.`; `DIAGNOSTIC: the ped pool at ... could not be read this time (the game may have been changing it).`, also for more in use than it has slots; `... could not be read this time: its slots, 20000, are outside 1..16384 (the game may have been changing it).`; `DIAGNOSTIC: the ped pool at ... has slots of 0x1000 bytes, not 0x1980: it is not read (another mod may have made it, or the game has changed).`; `DIAGNOSTIC: the ped pool's pointer at RVA ... cannot be read.`; `spatial array not made yet` or `spatial array not read this time` in the summary's place; and before the summary `DIAGNOSTIC: ped pool: the game's number of peds, 300, is not the pool's 256 slots - a mod changed one of them, and a pool larger than the number overruns two of the game's arrays.`

#### 11.16.6 Render queues (`MeasureRenderQueues`)

What a streamed ped, its props or a vehicle with mods draws in a frame the game keeps in three families of four arrays, one per frame, the render data behind them in six pools: past the peds' and the props' 120 entries the game writes past a heap block with nothing said, a full pool stops the game or takes a vehicle's mods, and past the draw list's 10,368 references, on the same path, a streaming reference is never released (PATTERNS.md, "Render queues").
The module's tag is its own, `RenderQueues`, since no part stands behind it yet.
With `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` it looks for its 24 patterns, `U-E-S1` to `U-E-D3`, each once - each one missing logged by the scanner, the first of them named: `DIAGNOSTIC: the render queues are not measured: U-E-S1 was not found.` - and checks, each logged at its RVA, the first that fails ending them with that line, nothing started:

- each pool's make: the rol and the add it holds, 1 to 63 and 0 to 63, read and never assumed, but `U-E-S1`'s add, 1 alone, since that code shifts by c + s and c ^ 63, a rotation at 1 and at nothing else, which is checked against the pattern when TweaksV is compiled; its flag, value and key 8 bytes apart, in `.data`, aligned and readable;
- `U-E-S2`'s jump to a CC past the match, `U-E-V2`'s inside its function by the game's unwind data;
- each family's arrays in `.data`, aligned and readable, as its add or walk loads them and as its init stores them, the four capacities at each array's +0xA; the vehicles' add and walk reading one index, their second size stored where their init reads it;
- the eight size lookups, `U-E-V5`'s two and `U-E-N1` to `U-E-N6`, calls into the game, all to one function, their rel32 read only after `IsReadable` where it lies past the match;
- the frame's three calls of the flips, each into the game, to a flip's head 6 or 7 bytes on, each told by the index it reads, since no pattern says their order, and taking the family's lock: the walk's for the vehicles, 8 bytes past the index for the others;
- the draw list manager's global in `.data`, aligned and readable.

No complete set is scanned: the findings' 3.2 names none, its 3.3 being their evidence, and no read of the measurement depends on one; they are for the writing half, on its findings.
It writes nothing and `DRY_RUN` changes nothing in it; a thread that cannot start is logged and ends it, the sampling thread being started first and ended by a flag it reads when the summary's cannot start.
`render queues sampling` reads every 20 ms, inside a `try`, through `CopyMemorySafely` alone, so that no page can fault the game: each array's count and capacity words, each pool's three fields, decoded by `DecodePoolPointer`, and its 0x30 bytes, the manager's global and its 0x40 bytes at +0x5D0.
A pool's flag with bit 0 clear reads as not made yet.
A read that fails, and any other stride than a pool's code hands it - the game's allocator need not zero, and a teardown may read freed memory before the flag clears - is believed in nothing, and 50 of them in a row, about a second, end that reading, since each read that fails is an access violation through every handler in the process: said once, an address as `DescribeAddress` gives it, `DIAGNOSTIC: the pool of StreamPed render data at 0x... (outside the game) has stride 737, not 736: it is not read further.`, `... cannot be read: it is not read further.`, and in the same words `the pool of ...'s fields at RVA ...`, `the ped props' arrays at RVA ... cannot be read: they are not read further.`, `the draw list manager's global at RVA ... cannot be read: the references are not read further.`, `the draw list's references at 0x... (outside the game) cannot be read: they are not read further.`
The first time a family's array is seen past its capacity it is said at once, `DIAGNOSTIC: streamed peds: a frame's array at 130 entries of 120 - PAST ITS CAPACITY: the game wrote past the array's end, a crash may follow.`, a line of the log and no box of D30, since what it does may be a crash long before the next summary.
At a family's new most it copies that array's entries, at most 4,096, and counts the different pointers, those of 0 left out, by a sort in a static buffer: that gives the entries per ped per frame, the one number the dump cannot give.
The summary (section 6.6, `render queues summary`), 10 seconds in and then every minute while a most, a capacity or a state changes, the latest numbers alone never:

`DIAGNOSTIC: streamed peds: render data 14 in use of 120, at most 31; requests 0 of 55, at most 9; a frame's array at most 63 entries of 120, 21 different in that frame.`, the same for `props of peds` and `streamed vehicles`, and `DIAGNOSTIC: the draw list's references: at most 4210 of 10368 in a frame.`

In a pool's, an array's or the references' place `not made yet`, or `not read further (see above)` with `, until then at most 8 of 120` where something was read.
At a most equal to a pool's capacity ` - FULL: one more stops the game` (` - FULL: one more loses its mods` for the vehicles' render data); past an array's capacity ` - PAST ITS CAPACITY: the game wrote past the array's end, a crash may follow`, as the line that said it at once; `, the entries could not be read`, and `31 different among the first 4096` past that many; at the references' capacity ` - FULL: past it a streaming reference is taken and never released, a silent leak`.

### 11.17 LightCapacity (`InitLightCapacity`)

The frame's two stores take what a job hands them with no check, over their own write pointers, a job's lists drop what does not fit, and past its 1024 the scene store replaces a wrong slot (PATTERNS.md, "LightCapacity"); 32 sirens a vehicle bring the overflow three eighths nearer (D23, D30, D39).
The part is `[LIGHTCAPACITY]`, on by default and off beside `ResourceExtensions.asi`, with no key for these sizes, since they stand at the top of what the worker's scratch allows; its tag is `LightCapacity`.
It looks for the 22 patterns and checks every site as section 11.16.3 has it, with or without `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, `G-E-C4`'s 16 bytes 16-aligned for their `movaps`; those sites' lines come with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1`, and the measurement reads the addresses from it and looks for nothing again.
Each failure is a problem in the same words, with nothing changed: `PROBLEM: The light stores are not raised this session: G-E-A1 was not found.`
A write pointer not 0 means the game's frames have begun, and a thread may be reading a rel32: `... the game's frames run already - its threads may run the sites while they are rewritten.`
Then `FindCoronaStore` with `G-E-G1`'s two matches, whose false is the same problem and whose writes join the one write (section 11.17.1).
Its memory is one block near the game (`AllocateNearGame`): the start values 0, 0, 300, 180 on a page of their own, read-only once written, as the game's in its `.rdata`, then the 18,000 coronas of 0x30 bytes and the 10,800 lights of 0x1C0, 5.7 MB, each store on pages of its own, read/write and zero, since the records are copied whole and read by value.
All that the 60 jobs can hand them fits, so no overflow can happen by construction, and the check the game lacks is not needed.
The one write, 16 places in one `WriteCode` with the corona store's, in `DllMain`: `G-E-A1` +3 and +24, `G-E-A2` +3 and `G-E-A3` +3 to the stores; `G-E-C4` +8 to the start values; `G-E-C2` +17, +22, +32, +42, +47, `G-E-C3` +2, +9, `G-E-B2` +4 and `G-E-B3` +5 for a job's scratch of 0x17340 bytes, 300 coronas and 180 lights, inside the worker's 0x18008; `G-E-D1` +42 `89 04 CA` and `G-E-D2` `8B 1C C1 90` for the index, so that past 1024 the farthest light goes.
The lines: `The vehicle coronas' store moved and its limit raised from 928 to 18000 entries.`, `The vehicle lights' store moved and its limit raised from 400 to 10800 entries.`, `The corona store moved and its limit raised from 1088 to 4096 entries.`, `A job's lists patched: 80 lights and 200 coronas -> 180 lights and 300 coronas.`, `Scene store's index patched: 88 04 CA -> 89 04 CA, 0F B6 1C C1 -> 8B 1C C1 90.`; with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` each write, and `DIAGNOSTIC: the stores moved: the vehicle coronas' at 0x... (outside the game), the vehicle lights' at 0x... (outside the game), a job's lists' start values at 0x... (outside the game)`.
Refused: `no memory near the game could be had for the stores.`, `a job's lists' start values could not be made read-only (...).`, `the part's memory at 0x... (outside the game) is out of reach of the <what> at RVA ....`, `the game code could not be changed (details in TweaksV.log).`
`DRY_RUN` makes no memory and writes nothing: `DRY_RUN: would move the vehicle coronas' store (2 references) and raise its limit from 928 to 18000.`, the same for the lights, `DRY_RUN: would lead a job's lists' start values at RVA ... (G-E-C4) to TweaksV's own: 0, 0, 300, 180.` and each constant's `DRY_RUN: would patch the <what>: <old> -> <new>`.
`LightStoresRaised()` is true once the one write returned true, a `DRY_RUN`'s would-write included, so that the sirens' dry run goes on; `InitSirensPerVehicle` asks it, after this part's start.
#### 11.17.1 The corona store (`FindCoronaStore`)

The game's store holds 1088 coronas a frame and drops the rest without a word, the vehicles' first, since they come last in a frame (PATTERNS.md, "The corona store"): 1088 of 1088 in 1 of the 32 minutes of my proof, and 32 sirens send it more (D35).
`CORONA_STORE` under `[LIGHTCAPACITY]` is the room, 4096 by default, 1088 to 8192; a `static_assert` ties the maximum to what a draw's stack of 0x24 bytes a corona, with 64 KB to spare of the render threads' 0x80000, and its word of an index take.
At 1088 `FindCoronaStore` looks for nothing and writes nothing.

Above it, called by the part after its own checks and with `G-E-G1`'s two matches, it looks for its 14 patterns, `G-E-I1` to `I9` and `J1` to `J5`, through `Scanner::FindOnce`, each one missing logged by the scanner and the first named, and checks, the first failure its reason, which the part reports as its own problem, with nothing written: the store `G-E-I2` names inside the game, its count's four bytes too (`the corona store at RVA ... (G-E-I2) is not inside the game (another mod may have moved it already).`); `G-E-I3`, `I5` and `I6` naming the same (`the second inlined add names the corona store at RVA ..., the first inlined add the one at RVA ... (G-E-I3).`); no more than the 19 references of the research by `Scanner::FindRipRelativeReferences`, since nothing gates the raise by the game's build and a 20th may be a new batch with a clamp of its own, fewer refusing nothing, a fact for the boot (`the game's code references the corona store at RVA ... in 20 places or more, not 19, as the research found them.`, `DIAGNOSTIC: the corona store at RVA ... is referenced in 19 places of the game's code: RVA ..., ... and RVA ...`); both `G-E-G1`, the part's, holding what the compile-time checks cannot name, their read, their limit and their jump (`the first inlined add at RVA ... holds ..., not its read of the count, its limit of 1087 and its jump when full (G-E-G1).`); `G-E-I4` and both `G-E-G1` reading the count at the store's +0xCC00 (`the copy to the draw list reads the count at RVA ..., not the one right behind the 1088 coronas, at RVA ... (G-E-I4).`); `G-E-I6`'s call leading to `G-E-I7` (`the LOD lights' job's call at RVA ... leads to RVA ..., not to what hands the store out, at RVA ... (another mod may have hooked it already) (G-E-I6).`), and no second caller by `Scanner::FindCallsTo`, which would get its count from the old store (`the game's code calls what hands the corona store out, at RVA ..., in 2 places or more, not 1 (G-E-I7).`); `G-E-J2`'s and `G-E-J3`'s rel32 past their matches readable (`... cannot be read (G-E-J2).`); `G-E-J2`'s three addresses and `G-E-J3`'s mark inside the game; and `G-E-J3` reading the render count `G-E-J2` stores (`the draw reads the render count at RVA ..., the draw list's command stores it at RVA ... (G-E-J3).`).
The literals the patterns pin, 0x43F, 0x440, 0xCC00, 0x180 and the job's scratch, and every instruction around a field are compared when TweaksV is compiled.
The new store is N x 0x30 + 0x180 bytes through `AllocateNearGame`, since the copy always reads 8 records past the count; 16-aligned, as `VirtualAlloc` gives memory, since the adds write with `movaps` (`no memory near the game could be had for the new corona store of 4096 coronas.`, `the new corona store at 0x...
