(outside the game) is out of reach of its count at RVA ....`, `... of the game code at RVA ....`).
Then it adds the 13 writes to the part's one write and writes nothing itself: the `lea` in place of `G-E-I1`'s nop, `corona store's lea in its general add`; the count's three, `corona store's count in its general add's load`, `... exchange` and `corona store's count in what hands it out`; the limits, `corona store's limit in its general add`, `... in the first inlined add` and `... in the second inlined add`, N - 1, and `corona store's clamp in the LOD lights' batch` and `corona store's comparison in the LOD lights' batch`, N; and the store's four rel32, `corona store in the first inlined add`, `... in the second inlined add`, `... in the copy to the draw list` and `... in the LOD lights' job's parameters`.
Its sites are logged as they pass with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, each at its RVA: `DIAGNOSTIC: the corona store at RVA ..., its count at RVA ...: the inlined adds' stores at RVA ... (G-E-I2) and RVA ... (G-E-I3)`, `DIAGNOSTIC: the general add at RVA ...: its count at 0xCC00 of the store at RVA ... and RVA ..., its limit at RVA ... holds 1087 (N-1, G-E-I1) -> game size 1088`, and the new store: `DIAGNOSTIC: the new corona store at 0x... (outside the game): 4096 coronas of 0x30 bytes and 0x180 past them, 196992 bytes`.
With `DRY_RUN` the store is made for the rel32 of the lines and freed again: `DRY_RUN: would patch the corona store's limit in its general add: 3F 04 00 00 -> FF 0F 00 00` and the others, and `DRY_RUN: would move the corona store (5 references) and raise its limit from 1088 to 4096 (5 sites), with 3 offsets of its count.`
A write of the part's that fails leaves the store's memory and the stubs', about 200 KB, unused: the module hears no more of it.
The part says the raise among its lines, by `CoronaStoreNow().room`: `The corona store moved and its limit raised from 1088 to 4096 entries.`

The counters, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` and not with `DRY_RUN`, an aid (section 12), whose failure is logged while the raise goes on: `DIAGNOSTIC: the corona store's refused coronas are not counted: <why>`.
Each of the four jumps, `G-E-I1` +30, each `G-E-G1` +15, read after `IsReadable`, and `G-E-I9` +13, must lead into the game, to the bytes the research found up to the first instruction that writes the flags (PATTERNS.md; `the jump when the corona store is full in its general add at RVA ... leads to RVA ..., which holds ..., not what the research found there (G-E-I1).`).
The stubs lie on the first page of a block of two near the game, the counters on the second, read/write; the first page is read+execute once written (`its stubs could not be made read+execute (...).`, `no memory near the game could be had for its stubs.`, `its stubs are out of reach of the game code.`).
A jump's, 22 bytes as `G-E-C1`'s: `lock inc qword [rip+disp32]`, `jmp [rip+0]` and where it led; all three adds' count into one counter, the job's own limit's into another.
The batch's, 40 bytes, in place of the six bytes at `G-E-I8` +56, `E9 rel32` and `90`: `mov eax, esi`, `sub eax, edx`, `add eax, ecx`, `lock add qword [rip+disp32], rax`, `mov dword [rip+disp32], esi`, the six bytes, and `jmp [rip+0]` past them; rax is free, since the moved `lea` writes it, and the flags are as ever, since the moved `sub` writes them last.
Their rel32 and the six bytes join the 13 in the one write: `jump when the corona store is full in its general add` and the others, `LOD lights' batch's way to its counting stub`, and `DIAGNOSTIC: the four jumps when the corona store is full and the LOD lights' batch at RVA ... (G-E-I8) are redirected to counting stubs once the part's one write is done.` With `DRY_RUN`: `DRY_RUN: would redirect the four jumps when the corona store is full and the LOD lights' batch at RVA ... (G-E-I8) to counting stubs - the refused coronas are not counted.`
`CoronaStoreNow()` gives the part's line, on any thread, what it says: the game's own 1088 and nothing counted, on a dry run too, until `LightStoresRaised()` says the write is done; then N, and with the stubs made the refused coronas, the LOD lights' job's own refused and its last batch, plain reads of aligned values.
## 12. The rules every part follows

- **Nothing is written before everything is found and checked.**
  Every site is found by pattern, every instruction's bytes are the expected ones, every address inside the game, every value plausible and consistent; the first thing not as expected ends the part's job with nothing changed.
- **All or nothing.**
  A job's writes go in one `WriteCode` or `RedirectCalls`: every page made writable first, every page given its own protection back, on an exception too; a part either does its whole job or leaves the game as it was.
  Hooks of one group are created and switched on together or removed again.
  The one write outside the game's code and TweaksV's own memory is SirenSettings' two parser members' type bytes in the game's `.data`, in the part's one write (section 11.14).
  The writes in `DllMain` happen while the game is still single-threaded; RageOpenV's start-up writes its few bytes - the cache check's `C3 90`, the console's NOPs, the five `E8 rel32` - on the game's thread at the first time request without freezing the other threads, as MinHook does for the hooks: an accepted window, since no other thread runs that code then (NOTES.md, T-FREEZE).
  The Blips part writes into a script program on the game's resource placement thread, after `DllMain`, but before the game enters the program in its registry of programs, where no script thread can run it (section 13.3).
- **What another mod has already changed, TweaksV does not change again.**
  A heap another adjuster set, a cache check already skipped, a function already hooked (hooked on top, chained through) are left and logged; an array already moved, a call already redirected, a pattern that matches more often than it must are refused with the reason, because what TweaksV would write over cannot be known.
  The conflict check switches a part off entirely when its old plugin is installed, or a plugin that does its job too and stays (section 7).
- **Never silent.**
  Every job not done is a `PROBLEM` line in the log with the reason and an item of the start-up summary, which is a message box with `ERROR_POPUP=1`; a part that stops working after the start-up gets a box of its own.
  A switch that is off is logged in one line.
  DirectStorage's job is its `NtCreateFile` and `NtSetInformationFile` hooks: one that cannot be made is a problem of the start-up summary.
  A job whose effect comes later is checked for it: RageOpenV's start-up check reports a start-up that never ran (section 11.6), HeapAdjuster's pool memory budget at its write, against an allocator set up already (section 11.1), the static bounds' check a store the game did not ask for in the 60 seconds after the session's start, a store whose read-back differs, and a check of its own that cannot be made (section 11.15), and the weapon components' guard definitions loaded while the game has never asked the redirected size call (section 11.10.2); the files a guard of WeaponLimits refuses are reported as findings in a box of their own (section 11.10).
  An aid that cannot be set up - the console, DirectStorage's call logging (its factory and `OpenFile` hooks and the checks that the game asks for its factory), a measurement whose thread cannot start (section 6.6), the `UNLOCK_FILES` summary's among them, RayTracing's counting stubs (section 11.13), the lighting capacity's (section 11.16.3), the corona store's counters (section 11.17.1) and SirenSettings' counting of the settings dropped and moved (section 11.14) - is logged, not reported (NOTES.md, T-DIAG).
  `DRY_RUN` and `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` work the same for every part, with three exceptions: with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` the box streamer refuses its whole job when a tree-search call cannot be redirected, because the measurement is the reason the switch is on (section 11.4), and the ped props' and the siren IDs' measurements run while their job is off too (sections 11.11 and 11.16.1); and with `DRY_RUN=1` the Blips part checks its hook's sites but never sees `blip_controller`, which reaches it only through the hook a dry run does not make (section 11.9).
  The parts measured first are aids as a whole (section 11.16): without `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` they look for nothing, a failure of one is logged and not reported, and `DRY_RUN` changes nothing in them but the siren count's and the lighting capacity's measurements, which then redirect nothing.
- **Everything read is bounded and checked.**
  INI values are trimmed, limited to 255 characters, parsed and clamped; the mods list has limits on entries, depth and path length; paths from the game are read with a length limit, paths from DirectStorage copied with a length limit and a guard against unreadable memory, and the name of every `NtCreateFile` call read within its own length under the same guard, and every rename's buffer within the length its caller gives, its name copied only when it has 1024 characters at most; the entry table of an archive is walked only when its count is plausible, and the check for packs made for GTA V Legacy reads each archive's header, entry table and names within its bounds - 1,048,576 entries, the table inside its archive, 65,792 bytes of names, 8 levels of nesting, 4,096 archives and 64 MB per pack, 1 GB in all (section 11.6); the scanner reads the PE header and the section table only where they lie inside the image, and unwind data is asked of Windows (`RtlLookupFunctionEntry`); the measurements and the guards of WeaponLimits read the game's data only after `IsReadable` or through `CopyMemorySafely`, within bounds of their own (sections 11.10 to 11.16); the log cuts its lines and its file.
- **Nothing throws into the game.**
  Every hook puts what can throw - a log line, a table walk - inside a `try`, and so do the measurement threads, the two threads of `TweaksVSummary`, the static bounds check's thread and the thread of the check for packs made for GTA V Legacy, whose start never throws into RageOpenV's start-up; a lock is never left taken by an exception (`TweaksVLock.h`), nor a page of the game's code writable (the guard in `WriteCode`).
  The DirectStorage hooks, RageOpenV's ParseHeader hook and its OpenBulk hooks' lines, the script programs' hook around its users' work (section 13.3), the guards of WeaponLimits, which refuse a file without calling the game's loader (section 11.10), and the weapon components' pool size function, which calls the game's lookup first (section 11.10.2), keep the game's last error; the other hooks call the game's function last, so the game's own error stands.
  `DllMain` catches everything on attach and returns `TRUE`.
- **Nothing per frame beyond the game's own work.**
  The hooks on hot paths (the file functions, the tree searches, DirectStorage's `NtCreateFile`, which every file open of the game passes) do one path spelling and one hash lookup, one count with atomics, or one bounded comparison of the open's name with the game folder and its counts with atomics - no allocation, no lock of TweaksV's, no file system call, but for one query of a folder's identity per open relative to a folder handle (section 11.8); DirectStorage's `NtSetInformationFile` hook compares the class of every call and reads nothing more of one that is not a rename, and a replace-rename costs a copy of its name on the stack and, for a name relative to a folder handle or a bare name, one query of Windows; the `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` check costs one comparison, and with it on the log lines are capped (below); the measurements are read once a minute by threads of their own; the siren count's measurement, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` only, adds to the game's call that draws a vehicle's sirens one lookup in a fixed table of the vehicle models seen, the model's bone lookups only on its first pass (section 11.16.2); the sirens per vehicle add to that call one lookup in the same table, always, and a second renderer call for a model lit past 20, to each of the getter's two calls one range check, and to the reader of the lights one compare per array read in parsing (section 11.14.1); RayTracing's, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` only, adds a few instructions to each object the full ray tracing buffer refuses - none while the buffer fits - and its sampling thread reads three dwords every 50 ms (section 11.13); the lighting capacity's, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` only, adds two instructions to each light a job's full list or the full ray traced list refuses - none while they have room - and a load and a compare to each of a job's two copies at its end, at most 120 a frame, and its sampling thread reads 15 of the game's values every 10 ms (section 11.16.3); the model budgets' adds nothing to the game's calls, and its sampling thread reads the budgets' data once a second (section 11.16.4); the ped pool's sampling thread reads a few fields once a second, with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` only (section 11.16.5); the render queues' makes about 20 guarded reads of the game's data every 20 ms, on a thread of its own, and nothing on the game's threads (section 11.16.6); the guards of WeaponLimits cost a bounded check per file the game loads (section 11.10).
- **Memory TweaksV adds is never writable and executable at once.**
  The moved arrays are read/write data, and the start values the light stores lead `G-E-C4` to read-only (section 11.17); the call stubs are written once and then read+execute, and so are RayTracing's counting stubs, whose counters lie on a page of their own, read/write (section 11.13), the lighting capacity's stubs, their counters on a page of their own, read/write (section 11.16.3), the corona store's, their counters on a page of their own, read/write (section 11.17.1), and SirenSettings' stub of a pack's dedupe, its record on a page of its own, read/write (section 11.14); MinHook's trampolines are the one exception, accepted (NOTES.md, T-HARDEN).
  The build carries ASLR with high entropy, DEP, Control Flow Guard and CET compatibility, statically linked (section 15).
- **Per-event output is capped.**
  Every kind of line that can come without bound - file requests, files served, archive headers, OPEN archives left as they are, mods files that cannot be opened, folders of the mods folder that cannot be read, the packs the check for packs made for GTA V Legacy names or reads, the opens inside the game folder, DirectStorage's `OpenFile` calls and the renames it turns, the `weapons.meta` and `weaponcomponents.meta` files the WeaponLimits part logs, `the ped props past 255, the rooms of the sirens' bones - stops after 20,000 lines of its kind (`kMaxLinesPerKind`, section 6.6; the cap lines are in section 6.3); the events are then only counted, with a summary every minute where `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` gives one, so no boot fills the 64 MB log.
  The tables behind a summary are bounded too: DirectStorage's open summary takes 100,000 entries per table, its `UNLOCK_FILES` summary 8,192 files and 15 types.
- **One wording** (section 6.3) and one log tag per part, the feature name.

## 13. Test builds

### 13.1 The switches

A test build (`-p:TweaksVTestBuild=true`, `TWEAKSV_TEST_BUILD` defined, built into `object\Test\Release\TweaksV.asi`) reads six more keys under `[TWEAKSV]`, never written to the file, and says `TEST BUILD - not for release` in the header: `TEST_BREAK_PATTERN=<label>` treats that pattern as not found; `TEST_TWICE_PATTERN=<label>` treats that pattern as held once more than the game code holds it (section 8); `TEST_LOG_LIMIT_KB=<KB>` (1..65536) lowers the log's limit; `TEST_DUMP_CODE=<seconds>` (1..3600) saves the game's code; `TEST_DUMP_DATA=<seconds>,<rva>:<bytes>[,<rva>:<bytes>...]` saves named windows of the game's `.data` (section 13.2); `TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT=<name>` saves the script program of that name each time the game places it (section 13.3).
The environment variable `TWEAKSV_TEST_NO_BOXES=1` keeps every message box off the screen, `2` logging each box's whole text as well (section 6.4), and `TWEAKSV_TEST_FILE_INDEX=1` makes DirectStorage read the game folder's identity as on a file system without file IDs (section 11.8; `TEST BUILD: the game folder's identity is read without FileIdInformation (TWEAKSV_TEST_FILE_INDEX=1).`), and `TWEAKSV_TEST_NO_RENAME_EX=1` makes it answer every rename it turns as a file system that refuses POSIX semantics would, `STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER` without asking Windows, so that the fallback runs (section 11.8; `TEST BUILD: every rename turned is answered as on a file system that refuses POSIX semantics, without asking Windows (TWEAKSV_TEST_NO_RENAME_EX=1).`).
`TWEAKSV_TEST_SUMMARY_MS=<ms>` (100..60000) makes every measurement's summaries after its first come every <ms> milliseconds instead of every minute, so that a suite sees a summary come again within the minute a fake game runs (section 6.6; said once per measurement, `TEST BUILD: the weapon infos summary comes every 2000 ms after its first (TWEAKSV_TEST_SUMMARY_MS=2000).`).
`TWEAKSV_TEST_GUARD_ROOM=1` gives each guard of the WeaponLimits part the game's own room - 160 `weapons.meta` files and 470 weapon component definitions on 1.0.1158.16, as the half reads them from the game's code - in place of the room the part gave the game, which stays as it is, so that a load past the game's own limit reaches the guards while the game stays safe (section 11.10; `GuardRoom` in `TweaksVSettings`, asked once by each guard and said once under its tag, `TEST BUILD: the <guard>'s room is the game's own 160 instead of 1024 (TWEAKSV_TEST_GUARD_ROOM=1).`).
`TWEAKSV_TEST_LEGACY_FOLDER=<folder>` has RageOpenV's check for packs made for GTA V Legacy read that folder in place of the mods folder, so that a suite reaches a folder the check cannot list, which RageOpenV's own reading of the mods folder stops at first (section 11.6; said once, `TEST BUILD: the check for packs made for GTA V Legacy reads <folder> in place of the mods folder (TWEAKSV_TEST_LEGACY_FOLDER).`).
`TWEAKSV_TEST_MODS_ENTRIES=<n>` (1..200000) lowers the limit of the mods folder's walk, for RageOpenV's list and its check alike, as `TEST_LOG_LIMIT_KB` lowers the log's, so that a suite reaches the stop with a handful of files (section 11.6; `ModsEntryLimit` in `TweaksVModsFolder`, read once, in the walk's file like the variables around it rather than a key of the settings table, and said once, `TEST BUILD: the walk of the mods folder stops at 7 files and folders instead of 200000 (TWEAKSV_TEST_MODS_ENTRIES=7).`).
`TWEAKSV_TEST_MODS_FAIL_LISTING=<folder>` has the listing of that folder, by the path a walk gives it (`mods\half`, `mods`), fail after its first name but `.` and `..`, with Windows error 64, the error of a share that drops, for RageOpenV's list and its check alike, so that a suite reaches a listing that fails half-way (section 11.6; `NextName` in `TweaksVModsFolder`, read once and said once, `TEST BUILD: the listing of mods\half fails after its first name with Windows error 64 (TWEAKSV_TEST_MODS_FAIL_LISTING=mods\half).`).
`TWEAKSV_TEST_WEAPON_HALF_THROWS=<1 or 2>`, read in the WeaponLimits part's entry of the feature table, makes the weapon infos' half (1) or the weapon components' half (2) throw in place of starting, so that a suite sees that half named in its problem and the other half start (section 11.10; said once, under the part's tag, `TEST BUILD: the weapon infos' half throws in place of starting (TWEAKSV_TEST_WEAPON_HALF_THROWS=1).`).
`TWEAKSV_TEST_THROWS=<what>`, read in `StartFeatures`, makes what it names throw in place of starting, so that a suite reaches the catch-all around it: a part by its tag (`RageOpenV`), a measurement by its part's tag (`measurement LightCapacity`; `measurement SirenSettings` names its two), and the start of them all by `start-up`, which only `DllMain`'s `try` catches (sections 3 and 4.3; said under the tag, `TEST BUILD: measurement LightCapacity throws in place of starting (TWEAKSV_TEST_THROWS=measurement LightCapacity).`).
`TWEAKSV_TEST_STATICBOUNDS_CHECK=<seconds>` (1..60) makes the static bounds check come that many seconds after the session's start instead of 60, so that a suite sees it within the minute a fake game runs (section 11.15; said once, `TEST BUILD: the static bounds check comes 2 seconds after the session's start instead of 60 seconds (TWEAKSV_TEST_STATICBOUNDS_CHECK=2).`).
`TWEAKSV_TEST_STATICBOUNDS_NO_EVENT=asked` or `session` makes the static bounds check do without that one of its two events, as if Windows could not make it, so that a suite reaches a check that cannot be made (section 11.15; said once, `TEST BUILD: the static bounds check's session event is not made (TWEAKSV_TEST_STATICBOUNDS_NO_EVENT=session).`).
`TWEAKSV_TEST_REFUSE_THREAD=<n> <what>` (n 1..9) has Windows' refusal of a new thread come for the first n starts of the thread `StartModuleThread` names so - `start-up summary`, `late report`, a measurement's summary or sampling thread (section 6.6) - with Windows error 8, so that a suite reaches what comes of a thread that cannot start (section 6.4; `ThreadRefused` in `TweaksVSummary`, read once and said once, `TEST BUILD: Windows refuses the first 1 start(s) of the start-up summary thread (TWEAKSV_TEST_REFUSE_THREAD=1 start-up summary).`).
`TWEAKSV_TEST_LIGHT_STUB` reaches what comes of the lighting capacity's stubs or a jump Windows refuses (section 11.16.3), or of the light stores' memory (section 11.17), each value said once: `no memory` takes the near memory for none, `TEST BUILD: the near memory is taken for none (TWEAKSV_TEST_LIGHT_STUB).`; `left writable` leaves the stub as it is written, unrun, `TEST BUILD: the stub is left writable (TWEAKSV_TEST_LIGHT_STUB).`, so that under the fake's dynamic code policy the refusal comes at the jump's page.; `no stores`, `values refused` and `stores out of reach` take the part's memory for none, its first page for one that cannot be made read-only and it for out of reach, `TEST BUILD: the near memory is taken for none for the stores (TWEAKSV_TEST_LIGHT_STUB).`, `TEST BUILD: the start values' page is taken as refused (TWEAKSV_TEST_LIGHT_STUB).`, `TEST BUILD: the stores are taken as out of reach (TWEAKSV_TEST_LIGHT_STUB).
`TWEAKSV_TEST_CORONA_MEMORY` reaches what comes of memory the corona store cannot have (section 11.17.1), each value said once: `none` takes the near memory for the store for none, `TEST BUILD: the near memory for the corona store is taken for none (TWEAKSV_TEST_CORONA_MEMORY).`; `far` gives the store memory out of reach of the game's code, `TEST BUILD: the corona store is given memory out of reach (TWEAKSV_TEST_CORONA_MEMORY).`; `no stubs` takes the near memory for its stubs for none, `TEST BUILD: the near memory for the corona store's stubs is taken for none (TWEAKSV_TEST_CORONA_MEMORY).`; `far stubs` gives them memory out of reach, `TEST BUILD: the corona store's stubs are given memory out of reach (TWEAKSV_TEST_CORONA_MEMORY).` A test build also exports `TweaksVTestCoronaStoreNow`, five 64-bit numbers of `CoronaStoreNow()`, which the fake's report reads where the export is there, so that a suite sees the accessor whichever line of the part reads it.
`TWEAKSV_TEST_SIREN_WRITE_FAILS` reaches what comes of the sirens per vehicle's one write failing once the hook is on (section 11.14.1), each value said once: `1` it is refused, a write without an address in it, `TEST BUILD: the sirens' one write is refused (TWEAKSV_TEST_SIREN_WRITE_FAILS=1).`, and the hook is taken back; `2` an exception in its place, `... throws in its place ...`; `3` refused, with a hook that cannot then be switched off, `... is refused and its hook cannot be switched off ...`; `4` an exception once it is done, `... throws once it is done ...`, and the raise stands; `5` the hook refused, by a function that is none, `... has its hook refused ...`, since Windows' dynamic code policy stops the light stores before it.

`WriteLog` leaves a last error of its own, `0x54574B`, no error of Windows', after every line of a test build, so that a hook of the game's call that writes a line and does not hand back the game's last error fails the fake's `last error kept` fields wherever a test build runs it; a good write sets none, so that on the Release build those fields pin the outcome and not the restore.
### 13.2 The code dump and the data windows (`TweaksVDump`)

`StartCodeDump()` takes a copy of the game as it is before any part changes it, and a thread saves it as `GameCode-start.bin`, waits the seconds asked for, takes a second copy and saves it as `GameCode-later.bin`, both in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\TweaksV` through the own-file checks, never in the game folder.
A copy holds the PE headers and every section that is not writable - the code, the constants, the function table - and a writable one only when it is also executable; the plain data sections, which can hold the player's data, are never saved; bytes that cannot be read are saved as zeros and counted.
The file (format version 1, little-endian): a `DumpHeader` (`magic` "TWKVDUMP", `formatVersion` 1, `sectionCount`, `imageBase`, `imageSize`, `timeDateStamp`, `secondsAfterStart`, `reserved`; 40 bytes), one `DumpSection` per section (`name` as in the section table, "HEADERS" for the headers; `rva`, `size`, `characteristics`, `unreadable`, `fileOffset`; 32 bytes), then the bytes of each section at its `fileOffset`.
The log says what was saved: `TEST BUILD: GameCode-start.bin saved, the game as it was 0 s after TweaksV started, before anything was patched: 50.3 MB in <folder> - sections HEADERS .text ...; none unreadable; not saved (the game writes to them): .data ...`.
This is how patterns are found after a game update: a dump of the running game is rebuilt as a PE (the headers and the saved sections at their offsets, `.data` as zeros) and analysed with Ghidra's headless analyser, the `.pdata` table giving the functions (PATTERNS.md, "When Game Updates", item 3).

`StartDataDump()` reads `TEST_DUMP_DATA=<seconds>,<rva>:<bytes>[,<rva>:<bytes>...]` (D21), since the code dump leaves out `.data` and a research pass needs static data that lies there, the parser's member data first.
The seconds are `TEST_DUMP_CODE`'s, 1 to 3600 in decimal; the RVAs and the sizes are in hex, 1 to 8 digits, with `0x` in front or without.
It takes at most 16 windows of 1 byte to 64 KB each, each wholly inside the section the game's section table in memory names `.data`.
Anything else is one problem, `TweaksV.ini: [TWEAKSV] TEST_DUMP_DATA=<value>: <reason> - the game's data is not saved.`, and nothing is saved, the good windows of that value neither: text that is no number of seconds or no window, no window at all, a 17th, a size of nothing or past 64 KB, one RVA named twice, whose two windows would write one file, a window not wholly inside `.data`, a game whose section table names no `.data`, a value of more than 254 characters, which a key cannot hold whole.
The first copies are taken at once, in `DllMain`, before any part changes the game.
A thread saves them as `GameData-<rva>-start.bin`, waits the seconds asked for, takes the second copies, all at one moment, and saves them as `GameData-<rva>-later.bin`, the RVA in upper-case hex without `0x`, in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\TweaksV` through the own-file checks.
A file that fails those checks is a problem of its own, and the other files are still saved.
`.data` as a whole stays out, since it can hold the player's data; a window is named for static data that holds none.
The file (format version 1, little-endian): a `DataDumpHeader` (`magic` "TWKVDATA", `formatVersion` 1, `headerSize` 48, `imageBase`, `imageSize`, `timeDateStamp`, `secondsAfterStart`, `rva`, `size`, `unreadable`; 48 bytes), then the window's bytes.
`imageBase` is there because the game's address changes at every start and static data holds pointers: a pointer in a window less `imageBase` is an RVA.
Bytes that cannot be read are saved as zeros and counted, as the code dump does.
The log says each file: `TEST BUILD: GameData-28EE4C8-start.bin saved, 1024 bytes of the game's .data at RVA 0x28EE4C8 as they were 0 s after TweaksV started, before anything was patched, in <folder>; none unreadable.`
### 13.3 The script programs (`TweaksVScripts`) and their copy (`TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT`)

A script program is one of the game's compiled scripts - `blip_controller` and the others its archives store, encrypted - as the game holds it in memory: its code and its strings in pages of 16 KB, its table of natives (the game's functions it calls) and its name (PATTERNS.md, "Script programs").
The game makes one in the program's resource constructor whenever it streams the script in, on its resource placement thread; the constructor calls the game's function that resolves the natives (the call at +3 of `Y-E-H1`) and only afterwards enters the program in its registry of programs, where every script thread looks its program up each time it runs, so no script thread can run a program while that call is under way.

`TweaksVScripts` owns the one redirect of that call, for every part that works on a script program.
A user is a `Scripts::User { tag, before, after }`: `before(program, placed)` runs for every program the game places, before its natives are resolved - the native table still holds their hashes - with how many programs the hook has seen so far, this one included, and what it returns comes back to `after(program, resolved, context)`, which runs once the game's function has returned, with its byte (1 when every native was found).
`Scripts::AddUser(user, reason)`, from `DllMain` only, puts the user into a fixed table of four - no allocation and no lock on the game's thread, the count published after the entry - and the first user makes the hook: `Y-E-H1`, `Y-E-N1` and `Y-E-I1` are looked for, each one missing logged by the scanner and the first of them named in the reason (`Y-E-H1 was not found.`); the call at +3 must lead into the game (`the natives' resolution call at RVA ... is not a call into the game (another mod may have hooked it already).`); and `RedirectCalls` sends it through a call stub to the module's `ResolveHook` (with `DRY_RUN`, `DRY_RUN: would redirect the natives' resolution call at RVA ...` and nothing else; a write that fails gives `the game code could not be changed (details in TweaksV.log).`).
`DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` logs the three sites under the user's tag: `DIAGNOSTIC: natives' resolution call at RVA ... -> RVA ..., strings' length offset at RVA ... holds 0x70 (Y-E-H1)`, `DIAGNOSTIC: code length offset at RVA ... holds 0x1C, native table offset at RVA ... holds 0x40 (Y-E-N1)` and `DIAGNOSTIC: code page table offset at RVA ... holds 0x10 (Y-E-I1)`.
A hook made is logged, `The natives' resolution call at RVA ... is redirected: every script program the game places passes TweaksV, before and after the game resolves its natives.` The outcome is kept: a later user joins a hook that is made, and gets the first user's reason when it could not be made, which each user reports as its own problem.
`ResolveHook` calls every user's `before`, then the game's function, then every user's `after`, each user inside a `try`, with the game's last error kept around their work, and returns the game's byte.
The module gives its users the layout too: the fields as named constants (`kCodePagesOffset` 0x10, `kCodeLengthOffset` 0x1C, `kNativeCountOffset` 0x2C, `kNativesOffset` 0x40, `kNameHashOffset` 0x58, `kNameOffset` 0x60, `kStringPagesOffset` 0x68, `kStringLengthOffset` 0x70, `kProgramSize` 0x80), the four a pattern pins checked when TweaksV is compiled against the byte the pattern holds there (`PatternByte`, section 4.2) - `Y-E-H1` pins `kStringLengthOffset` at +18, in the constructor's loop over the strings' pages after the call; the page rule (`kPageSize`, `PageCount`, `PageLength`), its rounding, `kPageSize - 1`, pinned by `Y-E-H1` at +10 the same way; the bounds a program's fields are read within (`kMaxLength`, 16 MB for the code and for the strings - a call's target in the code is 24 bits - and `kMaxNatives`, 65536 - a native's index is 16 bits); `ReadName`, the name read within `kNameRead`, 64 bytes, a memory page at a time and only where it can be read; and the game's hash of a script's name (`NameHash`, checked against `blip_controller`'s 0x80CFE974 when TweaksV is compiled).

`TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT=<name>` (test builds) is its first user, started by `StartScriptDump()` at step 7 of the start-up, after `StartCodeDump()`, under the tag `TweaksV`; the Blips part is its second, joining at step 8 (section 11.9), so the switch's copy shows a program as the game placed it, before the part writes to it; the StaticBounds part joins after them, for the session's start (section 11.15), and makes the hook itself while `TweaksV.ini` switches Blips off, no plugin that does Blips' job is installed and the switch is not set.
The name is 1 to 63 letters, digits and `_`, compared without case; another value is reported (`TweaksV.ini: [TWEAKSV] TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT=../x is not a script's name of 1 to 63 letters, digits and _ - no script program is saved.`), and so are a `LOCALAPPDATA` that is not set (`TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT: LOCALAPPDATA is not set - no script program is saved.`) and a hook that cannot be made (`TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT: the script program blip_controller is not saved this session: Y-E-H1 was not found.`), and nothing is hooked.
Otherwise the log says what the switch waits for, `TEST BUILD: TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT=blip_controller: the script program blip_controller (hash 0x80CFE974) is saved each time the game places it, at most 16 times, as Script-blip_controller-<n>.bin in <folder>.` - with `DRY_RUN`, `TEST BUILD: TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT=blip_controller: DRY_RUN - the call is not redirected, so no script program is saved.`

At each placement, on the game's thread, every read of the game's memory is checked with `IsReadable` first - the name a memory page at a time, within 64 bytes.
The first program of all is logged, whatever its name - the line that proves the hook runs: `TEST BUILD: TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT: the first script program the game placed is <name> (hash 0x...), on thread N - the hook runs.` A program whose hash at +0x58 is the name's and whose name is that name is copied; one with only one of the two is logged once (`TEST BUILD: TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT: a script program has the hash of blip_controller, 0x80CFE974, but is named <name> - it is not saved (logged once).`) and not copied.
The bounds, the module's: the code and the strings 16 MB each and 65536 natives; a program beyond them is a problem (`TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT: Script-blip_controller-3.bin was not saved: its code length, 16777217 bytes, is beyond 16 MB.`, `... its native count, 65537, is beyond 65536.`) and not copied.
`before` allocates the file and copies the native table while it holds the hashes; `after` copies the program object, the code page after page, the table as the game left it and the strings, a page that cannot be read as zeros, counted, and writes the file right there, on the placement thread, through `OpenOwnFile` in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\TweaksV` (`FileFolder::Own`; the folder made if need be, a file of that name from an earlier session replaced), never in the game folder.
At most 16 copies a session: the 17th placement logs `TEST BUILD: 16 copies of blip_controller saved - from now on its placements are only counted.` once.
Memory that cannot be had and a file that cannot be written are problems (`TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT: Script-blip_controller-1.bin was not saved: it is a hard link: ...`), which after the start-up summary get a box of their own (section 6.4).
Each copy saved is logged: `TEST BUILD: Script-blip_controller-1.bin saved in <folder>: blip_controller placed the 1st time, as the game's 312th script program, on thread N - code 16675 bytes in 2 pages, 245 natives, every one resolved, strings 496 bytes; none unreadable.`

The file, `Script-<name>-<n>.bin`, the name in lower case and n counting this program's placements from 1 (format version 1, little-endian; a research reader is written against it, so a change of its layout needs a new format version): a `ScriptDumpHeader` of 176 bytes (`TweaksVDump.h`, its size checked by a `static_assert`) - `magic` "TWKVSCRP", `formatVersion` 1, `headerSize` 176, `name` (64 bytes: the program's name as the game holds it, zero-padded), `nameHash`, `codeLength`, `nativeCount`, `stringLength` (the program's fields at +0x58, +0x1C, +0x2C and +0x70), `placement`, `threadId` (the thread that placed it), `programsPlaced` (every program the hook saw so far, this one included), `resolved` (the game's byte), 3 bytes reserved, `imageBase`, `imageSize`, `timeDateStamp` (where the game was, its SizeOfImage and its PE timestamp - the game build), `unreadable` (the bytes saved as zeros), a dword reserved, and the offsets of the five blocks that follow in this order: `programOffset` (the program object's first 0x80 bytes, as they are), `codeOffset` (`codeLength` bytes, page after page), `nativesBeforeOffset` (`nativeCount` x 8: the table before the game's resolution - the hashes), `nativesAfterOffset` (the same after it - the handlers' addresses, as they are) and `stringsOffset` (`stringLength` bytes, page after page).
The reserved fields are 0.

## 14. The tests

### 14.1 The harness

`test\` holds everything, loose in one folder: `run_all.sh` builds and runs every suite from Git Bash, or with a suite's name that suite alone, and deletes its intermediates (its Release build replaces `x64\Release\TweaksV.asi`); `common.sh` has the helpers; `build_tweaksv.cmd` builds TweaksV's Release and test builds; `build.cmd` builds the fake programs into `object\TestRun\programs`, with warnings as errors (`/WX`); `vsenv.cmd` finds Visual Studio with vswhere; `deps.cmd`, `headers.cmd` and `junction.cmd` are the integration suite's helpers (a module's imports, the PE headers for the mitigations, a junction made and removed - the rageopenv suite makes one too); the fake programs are `fake_game.cpp`, `fake_game_full.cpp`, `fake_game_rov.cpp`, `fake_game_version.rc`, `fake_dstorage.cpp` and `fake_renamer.cpp`; `base.ini` is the file the game suites start from (layout 5 with `HEAP_SIZE=1024` and `PACKFILEARRAY_SIZE=12000`, two values that are not the defaults, which the part suites and the integration suite assert - `heap: 1024 MB` - and which prove the file is read, PedProps off, as the default file has it, which `fresh_ini` in `common.sh` switches on for the three suites that set `PEDPROPS_ON`, `pedprops`, `patterns` and `integration`), `expected_default.ini` the default file of section 5.4, `layout1.ini` the 1.6.0 default for the 1.x-file scenarios.
Each suite is one `run_<suite>.sh`; `run_all.sh` runs them side by side, as many at a time as the machine has threads, and prints each one's output whole and as it was in the order of its list, `SUITES`, one suite a line - `patterns ini_default story modkit packfile boxstreamer decals rageopenv legacypacks directstorage blips weaponinfos weaponcomponents pedprops texturebudget raytracing lightcapacity coronastore sirenids sirencount sirendamage staticbounds`, then the suites of the parts measured first in the order of section 11.16, `population`, `pedpool` and `renderqueues`, and `integration` last - reads each one's `N passed, M failed` line - a suite that prints none counts as one failure, with `no result line (the suite stopped early)` in its line of the RESULTS block - and prints the `all` line, whose count NOTES.md's "Tests:" bullet carries (2382).
Each line of the RESULTS block and the `all` line also say how many checks were skipped, `N passed, M failed, K skipped`, the K among the N, so that a green run names the checks it did not make.
Each suite's number of checks stands on one line of its script, `CHECKS=N`, just below its `source` of `common.sh`, and `run_all.sh` compares it in every run, a run of named suites too, so that a check lost shows in the suite it left, also beside another suite's new ones.
A difference, or a suite without the line, says ` - its CHECKS line gives N` in that suite's line of the block and fails the run.
Each suite has a folder of its own, `object\TestRun\<suite>`: its game folder `TestRun`, a name the suites' patterns of a path rely on, which `TWEAKSV_TEST_DIR` names and which holds its own copy of the fake programs, and its `TestTemp` next to it, so that what one suite's fakes hold, delete or deny never reaches another's.
`--serial` runs them one after another, and `TWEAKSV_TEST_JOBS` tells a suite how far it may go side by side itself, as `patterns` does with its runs, one with `--serial`; `story` starts its measurement's runs side by side whatever it says.
The full run takes about 9 minutes on my PC, its test build from scratch among them and its Release build incremental, since the four builds from scratch of section 15 cover a warning only Release's code gives, against about an hour with `--serial`, the same checks either way; the `time` line under the `all` line gives the run's seconds and the three slowest suites, so that it stays at about ten minutes at most.
The run fails when a check fails, when a suite stops early, when a suite makes another number of checks than its `CHECKS` line gives, when either build log holds a warning (`warning [A-Z]+[0-9]+`, by its code, so that `cl : Command line warning D9025` counts too; the warnings are listed with the log's last lines), and when the number of checks run is not the one NOTES.md's "Tests:" bullet gives - that bullet's, not the first number before "checks" anywhere in the file - so that a check that silently stops running shows.
It deletes `object\TestRun` and `object\Test` afterwards; `--keep` leaves them, and `--no-build` tests the TweaksV builds already there, which is why it works after a `--keep` run only.
A suite started by hand after a `--keep` run (`bash test/run_blips.sh`) finds its folder again.
A run stops at once when it cannot delete the last run's folder, which a stopped run's fakes may hold.
The suites are the arbiter: a change to a part passes the checks that pin its behaviour, or it is not done.
The fake game's copies of game code live in its `.fakesit` section, since TweaksV searches executable sections only: a site that must be found goes there, and the fake game reaches a moved array only through a pointer, never with an instruction a scan would count.
A pattern that must match more than once (`D-E-L12`, seven times, and `A-E-G1` and `G-E-G1`, twice each) goes through `Scanner::FindAll`, whose per-match DIAGNOSTIC lines name the pattern; the story and packfile suites count on `D-E-L12`'s and `G-E-G1`'s being the only per-match lines of a normal run, with `A-E-G1`'s part off.
The check count in NOTES.md is the `all` line of `run_all.sh`, updated with every test change; the patterns suite checks that PATTERNS.md and `TweaksVPatterns.h` list the same patterns.

The fake programs pretend to be the game, not TweaksV: they run as `GTA5_Enhanced.exe` (copied to that name for each run), load the real `TweaksV.asi` the way an ASI loader does, ask for the time once (as the game does early on, which runs RageOpenV's start-up), wait for the summary thread, and report.
`plain_game.exe` (`fake_game.cpp`) holds no game code: with it every part that uses a pattern reports it missing (DirectStorage only says that `dstorage.dll` is not loaded).
`full_game.exe` (`fake_game_full.cpp` with `fake_game_rov.cpp`) holds copies of every site TweaksV patches, laid out so that the patterns match, in a section of its own, `.fakesit`, which is executable (TweaksV searches only executable sections); the arrays those sites reference lie in the fake's ordinary data, and the fake's own code reaches them only through a pointer, so that no `lea` of them in its code is counted by TweaksV's scan.
RageOpenV's eight hooked file functions are real code in another section, `.fakefn`, so the hooks are called end to end; the five redirected call sites are only rewritten, and the report calls what they lead to - TweaksV's hooks, and through them the fake's own functions behind the calls.
The script programs' three sites lie in `.fakesit` too, and with an option `script...` the fake places a script program laid out at the game's offsets - `blip_controller`, its code over two pages of 16 KB, the second partial, three natives and its strings, each page a memory allocation of its own, its code decodable from end to end with the game's opcode lengths - instructions of every length and kind, a SWITCH, an ENTER whose name holds the guard's bytes - with the guard at 0x3FE as the game has it, its count native 1 and WAIT native 2, or as an option `blip...` asks for it (the fused ILT_JZ with the polarity the other way round, NOPs, too little room, 240, a native the program lacks, a second guard, a jump, a call or a SWITCH case into its span, a CATCH, a byte that is no instruction, its threshold changed while the natives are resolved), laid again before each placement as the game makes a new program; `other_script` and `blip_imposter` (`blip_controller`'s hash under another name) beside it - once TweaksV has started, as the game does: on a thread of its own, through the call at the hook site, to TweaksV's stub when the call is redirected and to the fake's own resolution function when it is not, which turns the natives' hashes into the addresses of handlers of its own (a hash it does not know into its "unknown" handler, and it then returns 0) and leaves a last error of its own; for each placement of `blip_controller` it writes what it placed, before the call, to `script_placed_<n>.bin`, which a copy must hold byte for byte after its header.
The blip pool's two sites lie in `.fakesit` too, the pool - 162 slots in use below its high-water mark at 170, two beyond it - and the mark in the fake's data.
It is built three more times with other version resources (`full_game_1158_13.exe`, `full_game_1013.exe`, `full_game_889.exe`) and once without one (`full_game_nover.exe`) for the packfile part's choice by build.
`dstorage.dll` (`fake_dstorage.cpp`) stands in for DirectStorage with its four exports, three of its own - how many calls reached it, the handle a file it opened holds, one that lets go of such a file - and an 8-entry factory whose function [4] is `OpenFile`, which opens the file for real, as DirectStorage does (`CreateFileW` for reading, sharing reading only), and holds it open in the file object it hands out; it opens no network path.
`renamer.exe` (`fake_renamer.cpp`) is a program other than the game, which replaces a file by the classic rename, `MoveFileExW` with `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING`, and exits with Windows' error; the full game starts it while the stand-in holds that file.
After TweaksV has started, the full game prints one report line that the suites read, field by field (`has`): the heap in MB, the three screens' bytes, the modkit references and limits, the packfile references, sizes and hook, the box streamer's references, capacities and calls (each search called with the capacity its site holds, and stopping 16 entries short of it, as the game's do), the decals' references, limits and count, RageOpenV's time hook, console bytes, cache check bytes, file hooks (called with a path the mods folder has and one it has not, the other spellings, a folder, `..` and an over-long path; with `rovask` and `rov1252`, each path the command line gives - in UTF-8, or in code page 1252 - asked of all eight functions, `ask N served S/8 passed P/8`; with `rovbadname`, whether the name with an unpaired surrogate could be made, `bad name made`) and encryption calls (called with OPEN and encrypted tags and a fake packfile), the stand-in DirectStorage's results (the configuration calls, the factory, the nineteen `OpenFile` results and how many calls reached the DLL, with `dsflood` how many of 20,001 more were made), what another program could do with the files the stand-in and the fake itself hold open (written, renamed, opened again: `dsshare`, `ownshare`), the file opens, a file the stand-in holds written the four ways from inside the game and replaced from `renamer.exe` - what each way gave, what the file's name holds afterwards and what the stand-in's handle reads (`dsreplace`) - and the renames TweaksV's rename hook sees, the bare and relative names and the classes' flags made through ntdll's `NtSetInformationFile` itself, the last error set before each call checked after it (`dsrenames`), the script programs it placed (how many, on which thread, through what the call at the hook site led to - TweaksV's stub or the fake's own resolution function - how many the resolution said resolved and how often the game's last error came back, and the game's base, size and timestamp for the copies' headers), `blip_controller`'s guard from its count through its branch after its last placement, how many of its placements kept their code as placed but where the part may write, and how many of the other programs' kept theirs whole, the call stubs (`stubs: N, all RX`) and whether every page got its own protection back (`protect: all restored (RX)`).
Options on its command line set up the scenarios: a pattern missing, a reference pointing elsewhere, an array already moved, a limit that disagrees, a call another mod hooked, a flood of requests, the game's dynamic code policy, and so on; each is listed at the top of `fake_game_full.cpp` and `fake_game_rov.cpp`.

The weapon infos' sites lie in `.fakesit` too: the initializer of the weapon info blob array - its `lea` of the array, then `W-E-I1` with `mov edx, 160`, the call to the allocation, the block's store and `mov word [capacity], 160` -, the allocation's first bytes with its element size 0xF8, and the loader, a NOP before its start - a CC there would add a line to the RageOpenV suite's count of its computed starts - and `W-E-I2` 0x18 bytes into it.
The loader is real code - its prologue, `W-E-I2`'s count read and written and its block read, then `mov eax, 1` and the epilogue - which the fake calls through its start with a data file entry of 0xA8 bytes, the file's name at +0 and the type 0x4E at +0x90, as the game calls its loader, so that TweaksV's guard, once it hooks the start, is called end to end: the loader counts the file, as the game's adds a blob, and returns 1.
The array - the block's pointer, the count at +8 and the capacity at +0xA - lies in the fake's data, empty while TweaksV starts; once TweaksV has started, the fake sets it up as the game's initializer does - a block on its heap of as many blobs of 248 bytes as its site's `mov edx` says, the room its capacity says - and its loader's count at 152, then loads the files its options ask for: `wifill` 872, `wifew` 14 as packs bring them - one with a name of 140 characters, one with a byte that is no printable ASCII, and with `wiunreadable` one whose name runs into memory that cannot be read - `wiflood` 20,001, and with `wilater` 3 more once the guard's report is in `TweaksV.log`; when the guard refused any, it waits for the report and the line after it in `TweaksV.log`, 20 and 5 seconds at most.
With `wipacks` it mounts packs by the count alone once `TweaksV.log` holds each summary - the count 160, then 165 - for the test build's `TWEAKSV_TEST_SUMMARY_MS`; its other options (`wi...`) set the array up before TweaksV starts, give both immediates 2048, give the array room for 512 after TweaksV's raise, rewrite the number of blobs alone to 512 after it (`wiedx`), start the loader with another mod's jump to a stub that runs the instructions the jump took the place of and jumps back (`wijump`), copy the loader into a function of the fake's own that its unwind data names - at its start (`wiunwind`) or 8 bytes into it (`wiunwindin`), W-E-I2 in `.fakesit` broken then - or break one site each.
The report's field, `weapon infos: limits 1024 1024, loader hooked, the rest as laid, array 152 of 1024, files 0 passed 0 refused, 0 of 0 as expected`, gives the two immediates as they are, the loader's start as laid, hooked - a jump in its place - or changed, whether every other byte of the sites is as the fake laid it, the array's count and capacity, and the files through the loader: those it counted, those refused, and how many came back with the loader's result - 1, or 0 when refused - and with the last error the fake set before the call.

The weapon components' sites lie in `.fakesit` too (section 11.10.2): the rebuild, the sort, the update of the count and the pool's size call, laid out so that `W-E-C1` to `W-E-C4` match, and the list's 15 other leas with the registers the game's have, 17 in all.
They lead to the list in the fake's data - 470 entries of 8 bytes with the count right after it and the files' blobs and their count beyond, as the game lays them out, 412 definitions from 23 files - and the size call to a stand-in for the game's lookup, which gives the default or the size the fake's configuration names and counts its calls and those made with the game's arguments.
Options `wc...` set up the scenarios: a pack's file that loads 11 seconds after the other actions begin, the list rebuilt with 430 definitions from 24 (`wcpack`), the game's own list full (`wcfull`), 556 definitions from 40 files (`wcmore`), the list overflowed - the 471st definition's pointer over the count (`wcover`) - the configuration naming 600 slots or 20,000 (`wcconfig`, `wcconfigbig`), a load put back to the game's own list once the first summary is in `TweaksV.log` (`wcputback`), the list another mod moved, a lea fewer, one more, one with a 49 prefix, the count's references disagreeing, the count 4 bytes further, 8 bytes before the list, outside the game or 20,000 entries on, the pool's default 0 and the size call another mod hooked.
The report's field (`wc: loads 17 moved 0 game 0 other, moved list RW, copied then 0, other bytes as placed, data as placed, size call to a stub, RX: 1494 and 1494 slots, lookups 2 of 2 as called, last error kept`) says where the 17 loads lead, whether the moved list holds the game's entries as TweaksV found them and zeros up to 16,854, whether every other byte of the sites and the data are as placed, and what the size call leads to, which the report calls twice as the game makes its pool - the game's lookup; TweaksV's stub, which must be read+execute and is left out of the call stubs the report counts for the other parts; or another mod's hook, not called.
The loader of one `weaponcomponents.meta` lies in `.fakesit` too, after a NOP: its head as the game's begins and `W-E-C5`'s bytes at +0x13, with its calls of the fake's allocator and memset and its blob array, then a call of the fake's body, which appends the file's blob with its definitions, keeps the list's count the blobs' sum and destroys the callback; the base game's 23 files are real blobs.
The fake makes the pool through the size call, as the game does before any definition - `wcearly` before TweaksV starts instead - and `wcmount` mounts 11 files of packs through the loader's head, entries and callbacks as the game's mounter hands them; `wcempty` starts with no blobs and no definitions, the first file of `wcmount` mounted before the pool is made and the rest after it, `wcbase` loads them again after the blobs are cleared, `wcmany` mounts 1,101, `wcfill` mounts 36 of 470 definitions each, `wcbadblob` gives a blob 500 definitions, `wcbadcount`, `wcnopointers`, `wcnoblob` and `wcnoarray` make each other read of the blobs fail, `wcheap`, `wcvtableout` and `wcdestroyout` give the callback a callable on the heap, a vtable outside the image and a destroy outside it, `wcbadtype` gives the entries another type, `wcloadercount` and `wcloadercapacity` lead `W-E-C5`'s read of the blobs' count or of the capacity elsewhere, and `wcloaderhooked` puts another mod's jump at its head.
`wcgrown` and `wclarge` lead the count's references 600 and 16,500 entries past the list, and `wiinittwice`, `wiloadertwice`, `wcpooltwice` and `wcloadertwice` copy a pattern's bytes once more into `.fakesit`.
The report's field `wc loader hooked, files mounted 11, loaded 7, destroyed 11 (0 on the heap), list 472, last error kept 11, callables left 0` says the rest.

The siren setting IDs' 14 sites (sections 11.14 and 11.16.1) lie in `.fakesit` too, each a match of its pattern with the bytes the part checks at their offsets, written from their hex text before TweaksV loads, the rest `CC`, and beside them a 15th array, which holds a fifth `lea` of the table only with `sififth`, and the 16th and 17th, a pack's carcols callback and the base builder, each real code with a NOP before it: the prologue `I-E-H1` or `I-E-H2` matches, a call of a body of the fake's, the epilogue.
The data they lead to lie in the fake's data, at the game's sizes and offsets: the two registrations' member data pointers and offsets, the members' data - the two ID members of type 0x01, every member's offset at its +0x08 - the ID table of 256 entries, the carcols data with 24 siren settings of 0x5E8 bytes, their array, count and capacity at +0x48, and the pointer to it.
The sites the part rewrites run as code, through a small way in of `.fakesit` that loads the registers from a structure and stores them back: each load and store of an ID with a `ret` behind it, the dedupe's load and compare loop, the three constructors with jumps over what the findings do not name; so what the part writes is proved by what it does - the width of a load, the loop's jumps - not by its bytes alone.
With `siload` no settings are loaded and the table holds zeros until the game's init runs, once TweaksV has started: its own table filled and the base loader called through the site's call, with four arguments and for a result and a last error it checks; the loader finds the table in use all free, constructs the base file's 24 settings by the constructor's code, gives each its ID through the parser's rule - as wide as the member's type says - runs the builder through its start and looks up three variations; the fake's loaders go by the sites as they are, the table the `lea` leads to, the width of an ID and the limit, so a site not rewritten shows.
Further options `si...`: the init a second time (`sitwice`); a base setting's ID taken by an earlier one and the next without an ID (`siclash`); three packs' carcols through the callback's start, with IDs past 255, one taken, one twice, one without an ID, the low byte 0xFF and none at all, each setting read by the dedupe's load as code, every register the stub must keep compared, and five variations (`sipacks`); 20,001 packs' carcols of no settings (`siflood`); a pack's carcols of 4,097 settings of one ID (`sibigpack`); the callback's page not executable while TweaksV starts (`sihooknoexec`); the byte before the two hooked functions a 00 (`sibyte0`); a limit past the fixed size (`silimit`), one that disagrees (`sidisagree`), four that agree on 512 (`si512`), one that is no `cmp rsi` (`silimitop`); a pack's 6 settings appended 12 seconds after TweaksV loaded (`sipack`), or the carcols pointer null until then (`sinone`), the fake staying to 15 seconds for the summary after it; 300 settings, given their IDs as the game's builder gives them (`simany`); the settings' array unreadable (`siunreadable`); the variations' ID member's type a word (`sitype`), the members' offset fields written only after TweaksV has started (`siregister`), as a game that registers its structures late would, and an ID member's offset field another member's (`sioffsetfield`); and one site each not as the findings have it (`siwidened`, `simembers`, `sioffsets`, `sidataout`, `sidatardata`, `sitableout`, `sioffsetsout`, `sicarcolsout`, `simoved`, `sisplit`, `sififth`, `sijz`, `sifill`, `sicallhooked`, `sicarcols`, and `sibadt2`, `sibadt3`, `sibadt4`, `sibadc1`, `sibadc2` and `sibadc3`, a site of that match outside its pattern).
The report's fields: `siren ids: sites as placed 17 of 17, data as placed`, or `data widened` when only the two types have changed, each by the two they were placed with; `siren writes: 17 of 17 as the part leaves them, a pack's dedupe's load a word's, 0 hooks, types 0x3 and 0x3, member data RW, table moved, RW, the game's 256 copied, the rest 0xFF, base loader call to a stub, RX`, each site's bytes compared with the writes as a text of the fake's own, the stub of the dedupe's load and the hooks' jumps apart; `siren code: loads and stores 11 words 0 bytes, loop words, constructors 3 words 0 bytes`, what the sites did as code; and with `siload` `siren load: ...`, what the init, the loader, the builder, the packs and the variations found.

The siren count's two sites lie in `.fakesit` too - the vehicle lights' calls of the renderer and of the getter (`I-E-B1`) and a read of a glass bone (`I-E-B3`) - and its renderer and find-bone-by-name are carriers, compiled functions of the fake's own that its unwind data names, rewritten before TweaksV loads: the renderer into a jump on to the fake's renderer, with its read of a siren's bone (`I-E-B2`) behind it, find-bone-by-name into the game's prologue with its test of the bone count (`I-E-B4`), undone at once, and a jump on to the fake's lookup - so that TweaksV checks both by real unwind data, as it does the game's, and neither is on the stack while the fake's own code runs.
The raise's sites lie there too: the state's allocation in `.fakesit`, the renderer's call of the getter and its reads in the renderer's carrier, the tree reader a third carrier, its real prologue undone and on to a reader of the fake's; both getter calls lead to `I-E-M1`'s site, real code now, the model's position at +0x53B; `srdraw` has the fake register the lights' member, read settings of 20 to 40 items through the array member's slot 1, copy each into the game's array at its model's position and give each car a state as big as the site says, not cleared, then draw through the call at the site: its renderer runs the game's loop over 20 lights through the getter's call, the vehicle lights' own call of the getter comes after it, and it checks each light's value against its file's item at the item's true index, each stand-in, each block, each own setting and what each getter call gives, models at position 0, at 255 and past the count among them (`siren lights: ...`, `siren raise sites: ...`).
Ten vehicle models lie at the game's offsets, each with its bone structure and skeleton.
With the option `scdraw` the fake draws four of them through the call at the site - the police car with 20 sirens, one made for 32, one whose `siren9` and `siren10` are bones 128 and 129, one with 8 - 100 passes of each from each of two threads at once; its renderer checks that it got every argument as the fake handed it to the call, the stack's slots whole, and leaves a result and a last error of its own, which the call must hand back; its lookup counts each call and that it comes after the renderer drew that vehicle, on the same thread.
With `scodd` three more models are drawn (one whose bone structure cannot be read, one without a skeleton, one whose names disagree with its bone structure), with `scfault` one whose bones by name lie at an address that cannot be read, so that the fake's lookup faults, with `scmore` two more 11 seconds later, with `scfull` 1,100 more, one pass each; with `scnounwind` find-bone-by-name's pattern lies in `.fakesit`, where no unwind data names a function.
The report's `siren draws: ...` says how many passes went through what - TweaksV's stub, the renderer itself, or nothing when the call leads to another mod or another function - with the renderer's calls, its arguments, its result and last error and the lookups, and `siren sites and data: as placed` that nothing but the rel32 of the renderer's call has changed.
The sirens per vehicle's damage: the fake holds the game's damage code as code that runs, around the patterns' bytes, the eight functions' loops in `.fakesit` and the function that makes a bone structure and the game's fill over carriers its unwind data names, so that what TweaksV writes is read by the real instructions; the stored damage state's loop lies beside them and stays as placed.
With the option `sdrun` it sets the pool up by its site's stride, makes seven models through the call at the site, with arguments, a result and a last error to see handed on - 20 sirens, 32 with names past `siren20` in other cases, 24, a few past 20, a plane with gear doors in its first glass slots, one with bones past 127, 8 sirens - and runs every loop on them.
`sdnoholder`, `sdbadholder`, `sdbadstructure`, `sdfillfault` and `sdmany` make more models; `sdchanged:L`, `sdfar:L`, `sdfourth:L`, `sdhooked`, `sdelsewhere`, `sdallocout`, `sdnotstart`, `sdnounwind`, `sdpoolmade`, `sdpoolshape` and `sdpoolout` change a site and stop the loops.
The report's `sd sites: ...` says, in every run, whether the code stands as placed or raised to one N with nothing else changed and where the call leads; with `sdrun` come `sd pool`, `sd models`, `sd fill` and what each loop left, `sd hit`, `sd impact`, `sd wreck` and `sd flags`, and after them `sd children`, how many lookups of a glass bone's child were asked about a bone that is none.

The ped props' sites lie in `.fakesit` too - the eleven narrowings with the calls in their matches, `A-E-G1` twice and a third copy whose first byte becomes `48` only with `ppg1thrice`, the per-anchor count's start and its loop 0x30 bytes in, the model info store's lookup, the calls no pattern names, which bring the per-anchor count's to 7 and the metadata lookup's to 17, two calls that lead elsewhere - into the count's loop and to the texture count - which the scans must not count, and one more call of each function, its `E8` set only by `ppcountcall` or `pplookupcall`, and room for a second copy of one match, filled only by an option - and the stand-ins for the texture count and the metadata lookup are functions of the fake's own that nothing calls.
The store's four globals lie in the fake's data, its data in 16 MB of memory of the fake's own at the game's offsets: 60,010 model indices of 16 bytes, not a multiple of 32, so that the bitmap's last dword holds bits past the count; 601 other models, every 100th index, whose variation collection pointer leads to a page of no access; `mp_m_freemode_01` with 30 files and 212 props on anchor 0, 38 of them in its first file, which also holds a second entry of anchor 0, which the game does not count, and an entry with no props; `mp_f_freemode_01`, its type byte 0x46, with 28 files; 40 more peds; a ped without variation data, a null model info, a ped whose bit is clear and one past the count.
Options (`pp...`) add files - `ppgrow` after the first summary - an entry of anchor 13, 10,001 more peds past 255, a ped of 258 files with 65,790 props on one anchor (`pphuge`), an implausible count and stride, a file in memory that cannot be read, a narrowing another mod has widened (`ppwidened`, and `ppg1widened` for one of `A-E-G1`'s two copies), one call of each function another mod has redirected (`ppcountshort`, `pplookupshort`), a second copy of a pattern that must match once (`ppt1twice`, `ppc1twice`, `ppc2twice`, `pps1twice`), and each site not as expected.
The report's field says whether the ped props' sites are as the fake placed them, widened - the eleven narrowings' `B6` made `B7` and nothing else - or changed otherwise, and whether the store's data are as placed (`pedprops: sites widened, data as placed`).

The texture budget's sites lie in `.fakesit` too, as `Q-E-T1` to `Q-E-T5` match them: the start-up's stores of the most streamed objects and of the four budgets, the shift between them and the call after them; the budget's getter; a resource's placement; the update check, its flag's compare and its factors' multiplications - the raise factor's with a REX - at their distances; and beside them the budgets' neighbours and readers, which the scan for their writers must not count - stores of 4 bytes before them, of 4 bytes after them with an imm32, of 16 bytes before them with VEX, of 8 bytes after them, and loads and compares of them, `mov`, `movlps`, `movdqu`, `vmovdqu`, `cmp`, each a load no reading of its bytes takes for a store - and nine writers it must count, each leading to the budgets only with its option (`tbwritemov`, `tbwritebyte`, `tbwritesse`, `tbwriteavx`, `tbwritelock`, `tbwriteimm`, `tbwriteextract`, `tbwriteword`, `tbwriteaddword`: a `mov` of 8 bytes, of a byte with an imm8, `movups`, `vmovdqu` of 32 bytes, `lock add`, a `mov` with an imm32, `vextractf128`, a store behind a `C4` VEX prefix with an imm8, of 16 bytes whose last is the budgets' first, and a `mov` and an `add` of their last word with an imm16, which would lie past their end with the immediate read as an imm32), else to a dummy; and in a block of its own six writers behind a byte that once hid them from the search (section 8), each leading to the budgets only with its option, else to the dummy (`tbmisread0f`, `tbmisreadc5`, `tbmisreadc4`, `tbmisreadf3`, `tbmisread38`, `tbmisreadf2`: a `mov` after `and eax, 0Fh`, a `mov` of 8 bytes after `mov al, 0C5h`, a `movups` with a REX.B from 8 bytes before the budgets after `mov al, 0C4h`, which the VEX reading takes for a `vmovsd` of 8 bytes short of them, a `movd` after `mov al, 0F3h`, which the `F3` as its prefix makes `movq`'s load, a `mov` after `mov eax, 380F0000h`, which the `0F 38` as an escape makes no store, a `movbe` after `mov al, 0F2h`, which the `F2` as its prefix makes `crc32`'s load), and a load whose bytes read as a store, a `movups` of budget 0 (`tbmisreadload`).
They lead to the counters, laid out as the game's in the fake's data at the game's own sizes - textures 2456 MB and buffers 389 MB in 22222 and 1234 objects, the mark at 2900 MB, 98304 objects allowed, the budgets 1500, 1500, 3000 and 3000 MB - to the index 2, the flag down and the switch on with its factors 0.9, 0.8 and 0.95, and the call to the game's next function, a function of the fake's own.
Once TweaksV has started, the fake runs the game's start-up as the game does after `DllMain`: it stores the most and the budgets again - with `tbconfigbig` a `gameconfig.xml` that gives budgets 2 and 3 9000 MB - and makes the call after its stores through what its rel32 leads to, TweaksV's stub or its own next function, with four arguments that function checks; the function keeps the budgets as they are while it runs, which must be the raised ones, and gives back a result the start-up checks; a call that leads to another mod is not made.
With `tbnoindex` the texture quality's index is 7.
With an option `tb...` a site is not as the game has it - the shift by 21, the call another mod hooked, the index outside the game, the update check's counters 8 bytes off, the placement's most 4 bytes off, the update check's raise factor's multiplication an addition, `tbmostodd`, `tbbudgetsodd`, `tbindexodd`, `tbcountersodd` and `tbswitchodd` an address not aligned to the size of its reads, `tbt1twice` to `tbt5twice` a copy of a site's bytes in the update check's NOPs, so that the game holds its pattern twice - and with `tbgrow` the fake grows the textures by 200 MB and 800 objects past the budget, its mark with them, and raises the flag once TweaksV's first summary is in `TweaksV.log`, and with `tblate` its data are all 0 until then - as before the game's start-up reads `gameconfig.xml` - and set as the start-up sets them, its call made then; and with `tbhigh` it raises one high at a time - the textures' bytes, then the game's mark, then the objects - each once the summary before it is in the log, the flag left down; each then waits for the next summary.
With `tbindexunreadable` the index lies on a page of its own, which cannot be read while TweaksV starts.
With `tbhalfraise` the data lie over two pages, budgets 2 and 3 first on the second, which turns read-only right before the start-up's call, so that TweaksV's raise stops at its write of budget 2, half done; a vectored handler of the fake, registered with this option alone, which takes only a write to that page, holds the raise there for 12 seconds, past the time of the first summary, puts into the report how many summaries came meanwhile and makes the page read/write, so that the write goes on; then the fake waits for the next summary.
The report's field gives the five sites as placed - compared with their copy taken before TweaksV loads, but the call's rel32 - where the call leads (`the next step`, `a TweaksV stub` or `elsewhere`), the data as the fake set it but the budgets, its page read/write and the budgets as they are, the start-up's call (`start-up: through a TweaksV stub, the next step called 1 time, its arguments as handed, its result back, the budgets then 6000, 6000, 6000 and 6000 MB`), the block of the readings as placed (`the misread block as placed`), and the RVA of every site and datum, the index's own page among them, against which the suite checks the part's lines.

The Legacy packs check's archives are made by the full fake game itself before it loads TweaksV, as its options say (`fake_game_full.cpp`): `lpmake KIND PATH` writes a crafted RPF7 archive of a kind at a path - in full, `\\?\` in front, so that a path below `mods\` of 254 bytes and more is written too - its folders made - its header, its entry table with the root folder first and every other entry in it, its names, then each binary's data at its block of 512 bytes, as PATTERNS.md ("RageOpenV") has the format.
The kinds: `legacy` (two `yft` 162, a `ydr` and a `ydd` 165, a `ytd` 13 and a `vehicles.meta`), `enhanced` (the same with 171, 159 and 5), `textures` (an Enhanced model and a `ytd` 13), `others` (Legacy's numbers on a `ybn`, a `ymap`, a `yftx` and a name that is only `yft`, a `ytd` 162 and a `YFT` 171), `nested` (a `ytd` 5 beside a nested archive with a `yft` 162, one whose header is NG and one whose bytes are encrypted), `deep8` and `deep9` (a `yft` 162 eight and nine archives deep), `ng` and `aes` (the legacy kind under those headers), `plain` (the legacy kind with 0 for its encryption), `empty`, `rootonly`, and the damaged `badmagic` (a zip's first bytes), `nocount`, `toomany` (a header alone with 1,048,577 entries), `pastend` (cut to 64 bytes), `pastnames`, `noroot`, `badname`, `nestedout` (a `ytd` 13 beside a nested archive at block 0x7FFFFE, past its end), `nestedself` (one at block 0), `compressed`, `mixed` (a nested archive whose bytes are encrypted beside a compressed one), `notrpf` (a nested `.rpf` that is a zip), `nestedlong` (a nested archive whose size runs past its holder's end), `longname` (a `yft` 162 whose name has no zero within 256 bytes), `farname` (a `yft` 162 named with 255 bytes at offset 65,535, the last a file's name can have, in names of 70,000 bytes), `oddname` (a `yft` 162 named with a line feed and a byte outside ASCII), `manynested` and `manynestedok` (4,096 and 4,095 archives nested at one place) and `bigtables` and `heavy` (5 and 3 nested archives at one place of 1,048,576 entries, 16 MB of entry table each).
`lpmany KIND N DIR` writes one and makes the others hard links to it - 1,023, the most NTFS gives a file - and copies of it past them, `p00001.rpf` on; `lplock PATH` holds a file without sharing from before TweaksV starts until the report; `lpbadname` makes `mods\lpbad<U+D800>.rpf`, a legacy archive whose name is not valid UTF-16 (an unpaired surrogate), and holds it open with delete on close, so that it goes when the fake ends; `lpwait` reads `TweaksV.log` as TweaksV writes it - every 20 ms, on from where it stopped - until the check's last line comes, or its problem that it is not done, 40 seconds at most, then waits 1.5 seconds for the box of a problem after the start-up summary; `lpfree`, once `lpwait` has waited, opens each pack `lpmake` and `lpmany` made and the folder it lies in without sharing, which fails while any handle is open on it - the check's on a pack, a walk's on a folder - a moment's hold of another program's waited out, 2 seconds at most.
The report's fields: `lp: N made`, `legacy packs: done` (or `no last line in 40 s`) and `lp: all N free` (or `lp: M of N held`).
The full fake game's `latefailures N`, once everything else has run, places the script program N times, 2 seconds apart, with a code length past what `TEST_DUMP_SCRIPT` copies: with that key each placement is a problem, far enough from the next for a box of its own (I19 and I20).

The ray tracing buffer's three sites lie in `.fakesit` too, as code the fake runs: the set-up, a function of its own around `T-E-B1`, whose call leads to the fake's buffer maker - a compiled function its unwind data names, which keeps the count and the stride in its buffer object at the game's offsets, bits of its own above the stride's 12 - and whose store sets the buffer object's global; the two checks, each a function around `T-E-C1` or `T-E-C2` that takes a vertex count and a record, takes the range from the slot's counter by `lock cmpxchg` or jumps to its refusal, which writes every register but rax into the record; and the frame's reset, a third `lea` of the counters.
The frame slot, the counters and the global lie in the fake's data, 0 while TweaksV starts; once TweaksV has started, the fake runs the set-up, as the game's renderer does, and leaves the counters as a frame does, the last finished frame's at 2,811,203 vertices.
With `rtcheck` the ropes' check takes 100 vertices, then 1,210 refusals follow, 600 geometries from each of two threads at once and 10 ropes, each count past what is left and the upper half of rcx not 0, and the fake compares every refusal's registers with those the check left - rsp included; with `rtgrow` the finished frame grows once TweaksV's first summary is in `TweaksV.log`, then the frame flips to the other slot.
Options `rt...` make the buffer made already, the immediates another mod's or disagreeing, the maker's call hooked, into the maker or to the reset, where no unwind data names a function, the ropes' slot or counters other ones, the buffer object's global, a frame slot or the counters outside the game, off their alignment or in the hole - a page of the fake's data, inside the image, of no access while TweaksV starts - the counters on the image's last 4 bytes, a fourth `lea` or no `lea` of the reset, either refusal jump hooked, `T-E-C2` at the end of the edge's first page, whose second cannot be read while TweaksV starts, and each pattern a second time, in the twin.
The report's field gives the buffer as the set-up made it, what the checks did, the sites as placed but for the five fields the part may write, those fields, where the jumps lead - the game's refusal, or a stub, read+execute, on to it - and the RVA of every site and datum (`rt buffer: made with 4194304 vertices (stride word 0x7014), 1 maker call, flags 0x89, view 1, r9 0 | rt sites: as placed, count 4194304, checks 4194304 and 4194304, jumps to the game and the game | rt rvas: ...`).

The static bounds store's init lies in `.fakesit` too, run as code, a NOP before it, holding `C-E-L1`'s bytes, its second call to a function of the fake's, and `C-E-D1`'s lea beside it.
Once TweaksV has started the fake makes four stores through it: the lookup a table of hashes with the -1 rule, what comes back handed to the store's pool, the store built from that at the game's offsets, name map and streamer among them.
An add-a-name traps as the game does, for `sbnames N`.
