Options `sb...`: the configuration's 24500 (`sbbig`), 40000 (`sbhuge`), -2, 0 and -1 (`sbnegative`, `sbzero`, `sbdefault`); the store never made (`sbnever`), before TweaksV (`sbearly`), before the script programs (`sbfirst`), 4 seconds later (`sblate`), the run staying 5 seconds more (`sbwait`); a mod writing the pool alone (`sbmodpool`), the streamer's count another (`sbstreamer`), the store not finished (`sbnotmade`); `C-E-L1` or `C-E-D1` twice (`sbl1twice`, `sbd1twice`), the call another mod's (`sbl1hooked`), the lea outside the game or 4 bytes off (`sbd1out`, `sbd1odd`).
The report's field gives where the call leads, the sites as placed but for its rel32, the store's numbers, names carried and the trap, the others' sizes and the lookups as the fake found them.
The script options `scriptotheralone` and `scriptgap` stay.

The lighting capacity's sites lie in `.fakesit` too, each as its pattern matches it, add-to-a-job's-list as code the fake runs, `G-E-C1` after its 11 bytes of prologue, and its data at the game's distances in the fake's data, reached through one pointer.
Options `lc...` set the counters as a frame leaves them, change them after the first summary, set them at and past their room and the write pointers below their stores or before TweaksV loads, give a store a fourth reference or its constructor's in a form the scan does not see, run the list from two threads, and walk the siren settings from none through 0, 33 and 255 to an array that takes no reads.
`lcout`, `lcend`, `lcodd`, `lcunread`, `lcother` and `lcedge` with a name of the table in `LcSetUp` lead one rel32 away, each check failing on its own.
`G-E-A1`, `G-E-C4`, `G-E-D1`, `G-E-D2`, `G-E-F1`, `G-E-K1` and `G-E-K2` run as code too, through ways in that make the frames they want: `lcjobs` and `lcframe` take a list's capacities from what `G-E-C4`'s `movaps` loads; `lcframe` runs a frame past the game's numbers if the sites name moved stores - the reset, 60 full jobs ending through `G-E-K1` and `G-E-K2`, the drain's walk - and `lcindex` stores an index of 700 and reads it back.
`lcray` calls the ray traced list's add 2,000 times, `lcends` ends 40,000 jobs from two threads on buffers of their own, `lcrounds` adds a lower round once the first summary has them; `lcmodded` holds another mod's bytes in `G-E-C3`.
The report gives the sites as placed, or `raised`, every byte the part writes as the fake's own table has it and every other as placed; where the jump leads, the stub's state and what its counter holds; `lc counts:` the same of the other three; `lc ray:`, `lc ends:`, `lc frame:`, `lc index:` and the lists' calls, what each run did; `lc stores:` what the part's five rel32 lead to and how it is; and the RVAs the log's lines are compared with.
`f1jump`, `k1call` and `k2call` are names of the table in `LcSetUp` too, and `lcedge` takes them.

Options `pm...` set up the pool memory budget's scenarios, in every run the allocator's set-up, H-E2's bytes, run as code once TweaksV has started, so that its constructor is handed what the site holds, and a load charged as the game's allocator charges it, 134.5 MB in the end with one block given back: another mod's 200 MB, and a value of no whole MB; the set-up before TweaksV starts, and never; another value in the allocator's budget as TweaksV starts; one pool more, which 136 MB refuses and 256 holds; a use that does not add up; one more pool 11 s in; the allocator outside the image, not aligned, unreadable from the start and later; the heap and the constructor outside the image; `H-E2` at the end of a page, its call's rel32 on the next, which cannot be read.
Options `pp...` set up the ped pool's: its sites are in every run, read by TweaksV alone but the accessor, which the fake runs over its own encoding; `pprun` makes the pool behind that obfuscation, with 171 peds then 143, the mission, reuse and skipped ones among them and free slots' stale bytes misleading, and the rest change one thing each, the numbers or a site; three of them, `...unread`, move a match to the end of a page, so that what its check reads past the match lies on the next, which cannot be read, as `rtjumpunread` does.
That page is taken away after `MakeSitesReadExecute`, which makes the whole section read+execute.

The render queues' sites lie in `.fakesit` too, as code the fake runs with `rqload` and the options beside it, each pattern's bytes with a prologue and a tail around them: each make decodes its pool's pointer by its own bytes and hands it to the fake's allocator, which counts a pointer or a stride not as placed, each add writes the entry and the count the module reads, each init makes its arrays, each lookup asks the fake's configuration, the frame's flips run through `U-E-F1`, and the numbers of the plain load are the findings' lines.
`U-E-V2` lies in a carrier, since its jump must lie in a function of the fake's unwind data.
The pools' three fields, the arrays and the globals lie in one page of the fake's data, the pools and the entries in memory of the fake's allocator, and all of it is read-only whenever nothing of the fake's changes it, so that a write of the module would fault.
`rqbad:WHAT:N` puts one site out of what the research found, and no site runs then; `g_rqEdge`, a section of its own, gives a match whose next page cannot be read.
A vectored handler of the fake counts the first-chance faults on the pages it takes away.
The report's fields give the sites as placed, the data as left, what the load did, those faults and with `rqrvas` every RVA.

`TEST_DUMP_DATA`'s window lies in the fake's own `.data`, unlike its copies of game code, at the RVA its report gives with the option `dwrvas`.
With `dwwindow` the fake fills it before it loads TweaksV and writes what it holds to `dw_start.bin`, and a thread of its own changes it as soon as TweaksV has patched the heap site, in its start-up, and writes `dw_later.bin`, for the suite to compare with the files.
`dwnoaccess` takes a page of it away, and `dwnodata` changes the section's name in the table in memory.
`common.sh`: `fresh_ini` copies `base.ini` with CRLF endings and `ERROR_POPUP=0`; `setkey KEY VALUE [SECTION [FILE]]` and `addkey` edit it (`setkey` also another file); `run_fake [-C DIR] EXE [ARG...]` runs a fake program, in DIR if given, with a fake `LOCALAPPDATA` inside the run folder and `TEMP` and `TMP` in `TestTemp` next to it - outside the game folder, as the real `%TEMP%` is; the real ones are never touched - for 60 seconds at most, and notes in `harness.state` how the run ended: stopped at the time limit, without its report (the full game's last field is `protect: ...`, the plain game's `harness: ...`), with a status other than 0 after its report, which no fake's own way out gives, so that a fault in TweaksV at the fake's exit shows, or with its report and status 0; `game EXE [OPTION...]` runs one as `GTA5_Enhanced.exe` through `run_fake` and prints its report; `check DESCRIPTION CONDITION` counts a pass or a fail - after a run that was stopped, printed no report or crashed after it every check fails, printed as `TIMEOUT`, `NO REPORT` or `CRASH AT EXIT`, until the next run - and `skip DESCRIPTION` counts a check the machine cannot make as passed, printed as `SKIP`, so that the count is the same everywhere (the short form of a profile folder on a volume without 8.3 names, a name with an unpaired surrogate on a file system that refuses one); `has`, `log_has`, `log_lacks` (which fails when the log is missing, so a negated check cannot pass on a log never written), `log_line` and `count` read the report and `TweaksV.log`; `deny_writes DIR` makes a folder read-only for the user, as a game folder the game cannot write to is, `deny_listing DIR` makes what is in it unlistable, as in a folder copied from another user's account, and `allow_writes DIR` takes back either.
Scenarios that must raise a problem with `ERROR_POPUP=1`, the 1.x-file scenarios and the `TEST_` keys use the test build (`TweaksV_test.asi`, chosen with `ASI=`), the only build that reads those keys and keeps the box off the screen.

### 14.2 What each suite pins

| Suite | Scenarios |
|---|---|
| `patterns` | the table and PATTERNS.md list the same patterns with the same bytes; the code uses every pattern exactly once; each pattern of the table held too often, through the test build's `TEST_TWICE_PATTERN`, in a run and a game folder of its own, every part looking: the test line and the words of `NotAsOften`, the Release build ignoring the key |
| `ini_default` | the first start without a file (the fresh file byte for byte, its comments included, read without a problem); a deleted key comes back, without a comment; a file with `VERSION` alone gets every other key; crafted values are reported and clamped, one longer than the 255 characters read; a comment after a value, `//` or `;`, not part of it, and a bad value with a comment reported without it; a 1.x file is renamed, replaced, reported once, nothing of it read; a second 1.x file replaces the old `TweaksV.old.ini`; any file without `VERSION` counts as 1.x (a layout-2 file that lost it, an empty file, random bytes); a `TweaksV.old.ini` that is a hard link is not replaced; a read-only game folder with a 1.x file in the fallback folder; a 1.x file inside a read-only game folder, reported and not read; a layout-2 file carried to layout 5 - `POOL_MEMORY` and the keys of `[BLIPS]` and of the six newer sections added, `VERSION=5` written back in its line with its comment, every value kept, nothing more at the next start - and a read-only one only logged; a layout-3 file carried to layout 5 - `POOL_MEMORY` and the twelve keys of `[WEAPON_LIMITS]`, `[PEDPROPS]`, `[RESOURCES]`, `[RAYTRACING]`, `[SIRENSETTINGS]` and `[STATICBOUNDS]` added, `[PEDPROPS] ENABLED=0` among them, the new parts' values in the header; a layout-4 file carried to layout 5 - `POOL_MEMORY` , `STREAMED_OBJECTS` and the seven keys of `[LIGHTCAPACITY]`, `[SIRENSETTINGS]` and `[STATICBOUNDS]` added, its budget of 0 kept, their values in the header; a layout-5 file left as it is; `VERSION=6` reported and not carried |
| `story` | the three screens and the heap by default; the switches off; `ENABLED=0`; `DRY_RUN`; `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, with the heap's site and the three screens' sites; an already patched screen; unexpected bytes at the splash and legal sites; a landing-page function another mod hooked; the old plugin; the plain game; a heap another adjuster set, also one past the setting's range (3072 MB); a heap already at the size asked for; a heap value that is not a whole number of MB; `[HEAP_ADJUSTER] ENABLED=0`; `S-E3` matching twice, not used; a `HEAP_SIZE` below the game's own, raised and reported; the pool memory budget by default, at the key's value, at the game's own and past either limit; a load past 136 MB, refused there and fitting the default; another mod's value, two kinds; each job going on without the other's pattern, either missing, `H-E2` held twice, the plain game, no code to be changed; the allocator set up before TweaksV started, without `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, written and reported, with `DRY_RUN` too, no raise to come too late at the game's own size, a field of 0 and of another value saying nothing; and the budget's measurement word for word, 10 s in: raised, nearly spent, another mod's, set up before TweaksV, not set up, not adding up, both of those in one run, each way its allocator cannot be read, `DRY_RUN`, the part off, and again when its numbers change; the allocator, the heap and the constructor outside the game, left and logged, the constructor's call that cannot be read, a problem; the summary's thread refused, and with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=0` not started |
| `modkit` | defaults, with the game's own entries copied into the new array; a size within the range, below the game's own (raised and reported) and above the limits, and a non-number; `DRY_RUN`; `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` with every reference, limit and write counted, the scan's references too; `ENABLED=0`; the old plugin; a limit that disagrees; an array another mod moved; a split reference; the plain game; a seventh reference no pattern names |
| `packfile` | defaults with the hooked call; sizes and non-numbers, one below the game's own raised and reported; `DRY_RUN`; `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`; a reference pattern that matches twice, refused, each match logged with its bytes; the obfuscated code the old reference pattern matched; `ENABLED=0`; the old plugin; an array another mod moved; a call another mod hooked; an implausible direct size; a variable without the margin; no version resource; the builds 1.0.1158.13, 1.0.1013.17 and 1.0.889.22, each named in the header; the plain game; a 34th reference no pattern names; a configured archive count above the new size, replaced exactly |
| `boxstreamer` | defaults; sizes; `DRY_RUN`; `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` with the calls counted and the summary announced; `ENABLED=0`, also with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`; a list another mod moved; a split reference; a ninth `lea`; a capacity that disagrees; implausible capacities; the plain game; the summary 10 seconds in; a call another mod hooked; a pair of calls to different searches; `DRY_RUN` with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`; a hooked call without `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`; the measurement with the list at the game's own size, which no search can exceed |
| `decals` | defaults with the loop end; sizes; `DRY_RUN`; `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE` with every constant, load and write counted; `ENABLED=0`; an array another mod moved; a split reference; a 32nd `lea`; a wrong prefix; a constant beyond its pattern that disagrees; a parser check off by one; a parser check that implies an implausible limit; the plain game; the measurement with 300 definitions; `DRY_RUN` with the game's own array full; `DecalPatch.asi` switching two parts off |
| `rageopenv` | defaults with every hook working end to end, rooted, network and drive paths never redirected, an archive parsed without its own FindEncryption left as encrypted; an OPEN archive with an implausible entry count, parsed twice, left alone and reported once, and one whose entry table points at memory that cannot be read, the same; `CONSOLE=1`, also with the call NOPed already; each switch alone; no mods folder, with and without `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`; an empty one; a folder deeper than the limit, read before the rest; a name outside the Basic Multilingual Plane (an emoji), listed and served by its UTF-8 path on every Windows; ### 14.3 Which suites a change needs

One command runs some suites alone, `bash test/run_all.sh <suite>...`, side by side.
A change needs the suites of this table; the full run is a round's one proof.

| A change to | Needs |
|---|---|
| `TweaksVHeapAdjuster` | `story`, `ini_default`, `pedpool`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVStraightToStoryMode` | `story`, `ini_default`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVModkitLimit` | `modkit`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVPackfileLimit` | `packfile`, `ini_default`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVBoxStreamer` | `boxstreamer`, `ini_default`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVDecals` | `decals`, `ini_default`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVRageOpenV`, `TweaksVModsFolder`, `TweaksVLegacyPacks`, `TweaksVRpf.h` | `rageopenv`, `legacypacks`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVDirectStorage`, `test\fake_dstorage.cpp`, `test\fake_renamer.cpp` | `directstorage`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVBlips` | `blips`, `staticbounds`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVScripts` | `blips`, `pedprops`, `staticbounds`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVWeaponInfos` | `weaponinfos`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVWeaponComponents` | `weaponcomponents`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVPedProps` | `pedprops`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVTextureBudget` | `texturebudget`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVRayTracing` | `raytracing`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVSirenIds` | `sirenids`, `sirencount`, `lightcapacity`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVStaticBounds` | `staticbounds`, `blips`, `ini_default`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVSirenCount` | `sirencount`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVSirenDamage` | `sirendamage`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVLightCapacity` | `lightcapacity`, `ini_default`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVCoronaStore` | `coronastore`, `lightcapacity`, `ini_default`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVPopulation` | `population`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVPedPool` | `pedpool`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVRenderQueues` | `renderqueues`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVDump` | `blips`, `integration` |
| `TweaksV.vcxproj`, `version.rc` | `integration` |
| `TweaksVRelocation` | `ini_default`, `modkit`, `packfile`, `boxstreamer`, `decals`, `weaponcomponents`, `sirenids`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVMeasure` | `story`, `boxstreamer`, `decals`, `rageopenv`, `legacypacks`, `directstorage`, `blips`, `weaponinfos`, `weaponcomponents`, `pedprops`, `texturebudget`, `raytracing`, `sirenids`, `staticbounds`, `sirencount`, `sirendamage`, `lightcapacity`, `coronastore`, `population`, `pedpool`, `renderqueues`, `integration` |
| `TweaksVPatterns.h`, PATTERNS.md | `patterns`, and the suite of each part whose pattern changed |
| `test\run_<suite>.sh` | that suite, and its `CHECKS` line when its number of checks changes |
| `test\fake_game_full.cpp`, `test\fake_game_rov.cpp` | the suites whose scenarios it changes, and every suite when it changes what each run does or a field the suites read |
| `test\fake_game.cpp` | `story`, `modkit`, `packfile`, `boxstreamer`, `decals`, `rageopenv`, `legacypacks`, `weaponinfos`, `weaponcomponents`, `pedprops`, `texturebudget`, `raytracing`, `sirenids`, `staticbounds`, `sirencount`, `sirendamage`, `lightcapacity`, `coronastore`, `population`, `pedpool`, `renderqueues` |
| `test\fake_game_version.rc` | `packfile`, `patterns` |
| `test\expected_default.ini`, `test\layout1.ini` | `ini_default` |
| `test\deps.cmd`, `test\headers.cmd` | `integration` |
| `test\junction.cmd` | `rageopenv`, `integration` |
| the shared code - `dllmain.cpp`, `TweaksVFeatures`, `TweaksVSettings`, `TweaksVIni`, `TweaksVFiles`, `TweaksVGame`, `TweaksVLock.h`, `TweaksVLog`, `TweaksVSummary`, `TweaksVConflicts`, `TweaksVScanner`, `TweaksVPatch` - and `test\run_all.sh`, `test\common.sh`, `test\base.ini`, `test\build.cmd`, `test\build_tweaksv.cmd`, `test\vsenv.cmd` | the full run |

Every part's row needs `patterns` too, whose "every pattern used once" a part's `.cpp` alone can break by dropping a `Pattern("...")`.
A new part's suite adds its row, with `integration`, and its patterns join the patterns suite's run over the table by themselves: a pattern its part looks for only under a key other than `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE`, or a pattern of another game build, gets its line there, as `R-E-C1` and the packfile part's older patterns have.
Two things reach further than the table: a part that redirects a call adds a stub to the fake's `stubs: N, all RX`, which `boxstreamer`, `decals`, `rageopenv`, `blips`, `sirenids`, `sirencount`, `lightcapacity`, `population` and `integration` pin exactly, and a new log line can trip a count or a `log_lacks` in another suite, so it is tried against their patterns first.
`rtjumpunread` in `RtSetUp` serves `lightcapacity`'s `lcedge` and `population`'s `popgateedge` too, each taking a page away after the section goes read+execute.
## 15. Building and releasing

`source\TweaksV.vcxproj` is a plain Visual Studio project, no generated build system: C++20, `/W4` with warnings as errors in spirit (none is tolerated), the C runtime linked statically (`/MT`, `/MTd` in Debug: players need no redistributable), Control Flow Guard with its EH continuation metadata and CET compatibility set in the project (`ControlFlowGuard`, `GuardEHContMetadata`, `CETCompat`); ASLR with high entropy and DEP are the x64 linker's defaults, and the integration suite checks all four on the binary.
Its `TweaksVVersionMajor`, `Minor` and `Patch` properties are the version; the `TweaksVBuildInfo` target writes `BuildInfo.h` into the intermediate folder before every build, with `TWEAKSV_VERSION`, its three numbers and `TWEAKSV_GIT_HASH` from `git rev-parse --short HEAD` when the folder is a repository, `-dirty` appended while the tree has uncommitted changes.
Four builds matter: Release (`x64\Release\TweaksV.asi` and `TweaksV.pdb`, `object\Release`), Debug (`x64\Debug`, `object\Debug`), the test build (`-p:TweaksVTestBuild=true`, `object\Test\Release`) and the analysis build (`-p:TweaksVAnalyze=true`, `/analyze`, `object\Analyze\Release`); all four must build from scratch (`-t:Rebuild`: an incremental build shows no warnings for files it does not recompile) without a warning before a build reaches me, and `bash test/run_all.sh` must be green; `object\Analyze` is deleted afterwards, as `run_all.sh` deletes `object\Test` and `object\TestRun` itself.
New source files get CRLF line endings; git normalizes every text file to LF, and `.sh` files and `test\*.ini` stay LF (`.gitattributes`).
MinHook builds at `/W3`, unchanged, from `vendor\MinHook`.
`version.rc` fills the file's version resource from `BuildInfo.h`, with the copyright string `Copyright (C) dachdecker369. Licensed under GPL-3.0-only - see LICENSE.`

A release is a tag `major.minor.patch` on the commit I booted, and a zip with exactly five files, for this version and every later one: `TweaksV.asi`, `LICENSE`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`, `CREDITS.md` and `README.md` - nothing else; the `.pdb` stays with the build.
The release notes name the tag and link the repository, and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` and `README.md`, which ship in the zip, name the repository too, so the Corresponding Source is one step from the binary wherever the zip goes (GPL-3.0, section 6d).
The order: version in the project file, a local commit in the style of the earlier `TweaksV 1.x.y` commits, Release rebuilt from it so the header carries that commit, my boots (with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` when patterns changed, with my map mods for anything that streams), then, with a clean log, push and tag: an annotated tag named and messaged with the plain version, `main` and the tag pushed together.

## 16. Extending TweaksV

- **A new setting:** one entry in `kSettings` (section 4.1) in its section's place, with its comment for the default file - its `KEY=default` short of the comment column - a named reference, a row in README's table, and `expected_default.ini` with the new default file.
  `IniKeyCount()`'s `static_assert` names the count (47) and fails until it is updated: a new key in the file means a new layout number (`kIniLayoutVersion`), and the suites pin that number in `run_ini_default.sh` (the header line, the `VERSION`-alone file and its 46 keys added back, the carried layout-2, layout-3 and layout-4 files, the newer-layout line), in `base.ini` and `expected_default.ini`, and the keys of one section in `run_directstorage.sh`.
  An existing file of an older layout, from `kIniFirstLayout` on, is carried forward at its next start - its missing keys added, then this layout's `VERSION` written back (section 5.3) - so a new layout needs nothing more in the code.
  The 1.x rule stays as it is (a file without `VERSION`).
- **A new pattern:** one entry in `kPatterns` with a new label in its part's group, and the row in PATTERNS.md with the same bytes, the offset and what the match holds; the code names it as `Pattern("label")` once.
  The `patterns` suite fails until both agree.
  After a game update the same: the table and PATTERNS.md change together, and `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` with `DRY_RUN=1`, `CONSOLE=1` and every part on shows that every pattern matches as often as it must (`D-E-L12` seven times, `I-E-L08` and `I-E-N09` three).
- **A plugin that does a part's job:** an old one TweaksV replaces is its file name in `oldPlugins` of each part whose job it does; one that stays installed is a `PeerPlugin` constant in `TweaksVFeatures.cpp`, its file and what it is, named in `peerPlugins` of each such part (the array grows when a part gets a second one), with a comment that says why it stays and what clashes.
  Either kind gets its scenarios in the integration suite, its row in section 4.3 and its name in README's "Testing" step 2.
- **A new part:** a section in `kSections` and `kSettings` (the layout rule above applies), a feature in `kFeatures` in the order of `kSections` (a `static_assert` checks it) with its old plugins, its plugins that stay and `Init<Part>()`, one file `TweaksV<Part>.cpp` with its header comment, its tag and its credits, listed with its header in `TweaksV.vcxproj` and `TweaksV.vcxproj.filters` (the project lists every file by name), patterns in the table, a suite `test\run_<part>.sh` with the sites added to the fake game and its name in `run_all.sh`'s list of suites, its `CHECKS` line, a section and the key rows in README, a bullet in NOTES and the new check count there, a section 11.x here, and the project the part derives from or takes its idea from in CREDITS.md (and in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md where the part carries its code).
  The part finds and checks everything first, writes through `WriteCode` or `RedirectCalls`, hooks through `HookFunctions`, reports through `ReportFailure` with its job's wording - `ReportFinding`, with a row of its own in `TweaksVSummary`'s table, for what it finds while it does its job -, logs in the wording of section 6.3, and runs a measurement, if it has one, through `StartMeasurement` (section 6.6).
- **A limit measured first,** before its part is built (section 11.16): a module `TweaksV<Limit>.cpp` and `.h` with its header comment, its part's tag or one of its own and `Measure<Limit>()`, an entry in `kMeasuredFirst` in the order of 11.16, its two files in `TweaksV.vcxproj` and `.vcxproj.filters`, its patterns in its part's group of the table, a suite `test\run_<limit>.sh` with its sites in the fake game and its name in `run_all.sh`'s list, its `CHECKS` line, its subsection of 11.16 and its rows here in 2 and 14.2, its section in PATTERNS.md, its paragraph in README's "Measured first" and its line in NOTES.
  It finds and checks everything its writing half will, logs in the wording of section 6.3, measures through `StartMeasurement` and writes nothing unless changed on its findings (D25), all with `DIAGNOSTIC_MODE=1` only.
  Once its part is built, the limit leaves `kMeasuredFirst` and its module becomes the part's.
- **A new array part:** a `RelocationSpec` with its anchors (section 10.1), `FindRelocation` then `ApplyRelocation`; what the core cannot express stays in the part and reads `RelocationPlan::matches`.
- **A part that works on a script program**, as the Blips part does (section 11.9): it registers with `TweaksVScripts` - `Scripts::AddUser` from its `Init<Part>()`, in `DllMain` - reports the reason it gets back as its own problem when the hook is not there, and does its work in its `before` or `after` on the game's placement thread, reading the program by the module's offsets and page rule, every read through `IsReadable`, nothing thrown, no allocation it can do without (section 13.3); a pattern of its own goes in a group of its own in the table (the Blips part's `L`), and the module's table of users has room for four.
- **A fork or a pull request** keeps the license (GPL-3.0-only), CREDITS.md and THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md, which must accompany every copy, and the notices' four pieces of code (the console opener, the pattern parser, the free-memory search adapted from MinHook's `buffer.c` and the StaticBounds part's key and rule from the Static Bounds Limit Adjuster) credited as they are; keeps the parts their own code, and carries no code of the projects that are idea only (CREDITS.md names them); keeps the suites green with the count updated in NOTES.md; and keeps the documents true: PATTERNS.md for every game fact, NOTES.md for what a part does, this file for how it does it, README.md for the player.
